# UN deliberation benchmark · running research notes

**Council negotiation scorecard.** The [replayable scorecard](research/council_workshop/SCORECARD.md) collects the interactive workshop outcomes, source-coverage grades, registered coverage and information ceilings. Its offline rebuild checks the archived traces.

**Blinded clause regrade.** A fresh model matched all 12 archived criterion labels across the two workshop clauses. The false-adoption control retained 40/70 policy coverage and received zero released points after the factual guard failed. [Inputs, verdicts and replay](reports/council-workshop-blind-judge-20261123/README.md)

**Final release.** [UN Deliberation Benchmark v1.7](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/README.md) freezes five UN event families, 11 episodes and 66 scored conditions. Run independent drafts, peer-informed notes and pooled solo in both desk orders. The headline is the difference in released final-record points between peer-informed and independent drafts, averaged by family and reported separately for historical reconstruction and workshop decisions. The final record is worth 70 points. Key points, arguments and interactions each have a separate 10-point scale.

[Download the verified v1.7 archive](research/unsc-marl-20261119/un-deliberation-benchmark-v1.7.zip) · [Read the MARL handoff](research/unsc-marl-20261119/MARL_HANDOFF.md)

**Information check.** Iraq and Syria each cross two mandates per desk. In either four-world family the best possible exact decision accuracy is 50% with public evidence, 75% with desk 1's card, 50% with desk 2's card, and 100% with both. The transcript judge tracks distinct ideas per speaker, assertions, rejections, and which supported ideas survive into the final. [Cases and rubric](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/cases.json) · [Final judge](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/final-judge.md) · [Transcript judge](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/process-judge.md)

**Release audit.** The [verification receipt](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/verification.json) covers the 66-condition software run, 25 authored judge anchors, eight workshop decision keys and 22 separate action-rule contrasts. The anchors include a full-credit target for each episode. The report traces peer effects to rubric transitions and release blockers, recomputes the headline after omitting each family, and measures whether a different reader changes the direction of the comparison. The [Syria replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/replay.json) preserves 12 archived finals and prior assistant marks. All 12 missed the exact decision and earned zero released points. Independent judge reliability and transfer to unseen event families remain unmeasured.

**Standalone check.** A clean copy containing only the 23 release files reproduced both bundled JSON receipts byte for byte using Python 3.12. A software adapter exercised all 25 calibration packets and the full collection, judging and blinded-review commands. The release saves malformed judge adapter replies and verifies that repairing missing blinded verdicts reproduces the complete score report. The SHA-256 of `release.json` is `a440b9d5464dbe4b28b5e86fe9e9aefdd5cdd62573821da2ca2a58f46fe1f649`.

The [v1.7 manifest](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.7/release.json) pins the release. [Frozen v1.6](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.6/README.md), [v1.5](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.5/README.md), [v1.4](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.4/README.md), [v1.3](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1.3/README.md), [v1.2](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-final/README.md), [v1.1](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark/README.md) and [v1.0](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1/README.md) preserve the earlier protocols and results. The notes below record the research that informed the release.

## A repeatable way to author tasks

Use the [task-authoring kit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md) to check how much communication a decision requires before testing a policy. Two worked UN workshops hold the graph, world count and turn budget fixed. The exact chair accuracy ceilings at zero, one and two rounds are **50%, 50%, 100%** for the Mali package decision and **50%, 75%, 100%** for Myanmar briefing coordination. These are enumerated information limits. The kit includes role-specific prompts, alternative acceptable decisions, worked judge anchors and a comparison design for solo, independent-draft, interactive, cross-play and swarm systems.

## What we want to measure

1. **Did the process help?** What decision-relevant evidence was surfaced, who supplied it, who acted on it, and what survived in the final product?
2. **Was the final product good?** Could a reader use the memo or amended draft and distinguish official positions, legitimate shared ground, unresolved disagreements and the authority behind each proposed action?
3. **Did the system get the decision right?** On historical prediction rows, compare with the official roll call or disposition. On controlled simulations, score agreement and deadlock against the defined decision rule. Assess the quality of reasons separately.

Keep these results visible together. A fluent discussion can propagate an error. A correct vote can be a guess. An honest impasse can satisfy the task in full.

## Research to borrow from

| Study | How it measured deliberation | Use in a UN case |
|---|---|---|
| [Steenbergen et al., Discourse Quality Index](https://content.csbs.utah.edu/~burbank/steenbergen2003.pdf) | Human coding of parliamentary discourse, including justification, treatment of counterarguments, respect and constructive proposals. | Use explicit anchors for whether a delegate gives a reason tied to a clause, engages the strongest objection, and proposes a concrete way forward. Report dimensions separately. |
| [Behrendt et al., AQuA](https://aclanthology.org/2024.delite-1.1/) and [Flechtner, DelibSim workshop study](https://polisim.net/assets/papers/accepted_papers/Procedural_Parity_Outcome_Mismatch_Evaluating_Human_vs_LLM_Deliberation.pdf) | AQuA scores multiple qualities of a discussion post. DelibSim applies a process score and a separate pre/post reasoning measure across 1,980 small-group deliberations. It reports that process scores close to the human reference can coexist with smaller, topic-dependent outcome gains. | Judge each substantive turn and test the final product independently. Archive pre-discussion positions so a change can be classified as a correction, regression or a move between supported options. |
| [Gelauff et al., Estimating Contribution Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11936) | Eight human annotators and an LLM scored justification, novelty, expansion of the conversation and potential for future expansion on five-point scales. Pooled human judgments outperformed the model on all four dimensions when three annotators were combined. | Separate a new idea, an actual advance in the visible record, and the potential to advance it. Calibrate novelty and actual advance against independent annotators before using either as a score. |
| [Flechtner, The Deliberative Deficit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10186) | Applies the Deliberative Reason Index to the five-agent citizen-assembly experiments and examines pre/post coherence and how diverse the initial perspectives were. Persona prompting did not reproduce the human pattern. | Establish initial disagreement from dated evidence or private workshop constraints. Code changes to a position and changes to its reasons separately. Compare each with the participant’s own opening and a solo revision. |
| [Pitre et al., A Diagnostic Study of Multi-Agent LLMs for Real-World Debates](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v306/pitre26a.html) | Scores engagement, responsiveness, influence asymmetry, balance, stability and agent utility. Estimates influence from changes toward an earlier stance with a justification check. | Record who prompted each change and whether the new rationale survives source review. Report how much of the final text traces to one delegation and inspect whether that concentration reflects decisive evidence or unsupported copying. |
| [Lee and Kwon, multi-agent Delphi experiments](https://www.mdpi.com/2076-3417/16/13/6715) | Separately measures idea exploration, consensus quality and consensus efficiency against a human-expert Delphi reference. | Keep the breadth of viable amendments visible alongside endorsement and rounds to disposition. A discussion can usefully explore several options and still end in a justified hold. |
| [Stasser and Titus, hidden profiles](https://www.uni-muenster.de/imperia/md/content/psyifp/aeechterhoff/vorlesungkommunikation/stasser_titus_unsharedinfogroupdisc_jpsp1985.pdf), [HiddenBench](https://arxiv.org/html/2505.11556) and [Hidden Profile Decision Making in Multi-Agent LLM Groups](https://camps.aptaracorp.com/ACM_PMS/PMS/ACM/CSCWCOMPANION26/87/a47d7633-8983-11f1-b513-16ffd757ba29/OUT/cscwcompanion26-87.html) | Distributed evidence makes pooling necessary. HiddenBench uses a full-information control and tests forced disclosure. The CSCW study uses three discussion rounds and distinguishes unique items surfaced, discussion depth, and selecting the designed optimum. | Pre-register facts or simulated instructions held privately by each role. Score relevant facts disclosed / available and used / disclosed separately. Include solo with all evidence and a forced reveal as controls. |
| [Lin et al., DialOp](https://aclanthology.org/2024.tacl-1.50/) | Decision-oriented dialogues with outcomes scored by the environment. The project has a pinned scorer and human references. | On synthetic workshop variants, give agreement and justified deadlock verifiable outcomes and measure the cost of finding them. |
| [Wang et al., deliberative collaboration under partial observability](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.06157v1) | Uses up to six rounds, scores final joint decisions, and runs both single-round centralized and full-information deliberation controls. Appendix B measures errors in the group's reconstruction of the world. | When evidence is already available, compare a solo revision with peer revision using the same evidence and budget. Log errors in the reconstructed constraints and errors in the final decision separately. This distinguishes failure to share a mandate from failure to apply it. |
| [MultiAgentBench / MARBLE](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.421/) | Tracks task success, collaboration, milestones and agent contributions. Appendix A.12 provides judge prompts and anchored examples. | Pre-register case milestones such as exposing an objection, repairing a clause and recording unresolved terms. Require a supporting turn span for claimed completion. An announced intention alone leaves the milestone unmet. |
| [Tessler et al., Habermas Machine](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.adq2852) | Human participants and external judges assessed synthesized group statements. The study examines clarity, perceived fairness, and representation of minority opinions. | Inspect the actual final text for accurately represented objections. Use separate endorsement records where endorsements are observable. Diplomatic agreement cannot be inferred from a well-written summary. |
| [Smit et al., Should We Be Going MAD?](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/smit24a.html), [DeliberationBench preprint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08835) | Compare peer debate with stronger prompting, ensembling or selecting a single response. Their reported results depend on the task and protocol. | Report a strong single analyst and an independent-draft synthesizer with matched ceilings. If we add best-of-N, selection has its own budget and judge. Keep oracle best-of-N labeled as an upper bound. |
| [Zheng et al., MT-Bench and Arena judge study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05685) | Measures agreement with human preference and identifies position, verbosity and self-enhancement biases. | Blind arm and model identity, rotate pair order when comparing side by side, include matched length/style variants, and audit against independently rated source-backed cases. |

## UN data and evaluation tracks

- **Historical prediction.** Retain UNBench native co-penholder, vote and adoption tasks and UNSC-Bench P5 voting as reproducibility tracks. Run outcome-blind prompts. Report accuracy beside Yes/No/Abstain recall, invalid answers, the always-yes and country-prior baselines, and joint correctness on historically disagreeing delegation pairs. Report adoption and vetoed-draft cohorts. Translations and the versions and amendments of a draft stay in one negotiation family. Verify instrument, membership, procedural stage and label against the UN roll call. [UNBench](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/view/37040) · [UNSC-Bench](https://aclanthology.org/2026.mme-main.10/) · [Pinned release joins](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/benchmark-development.json)
- **Public-record discussion and synthesis.** Use UNBench's statement task as precedent and locate statements, adopted text, amendment votes and the chair's decision directly in the primary record. Separate prospective and retrospective tasks. A justification grader for prediction receives only the pre-vote evidence. A retrospective reconstruction can receive the post-vote evidence. Keep a separate dated evidence map for every role, including periods in which a speaker acted as Council president. [UNBench](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/view/37040)
- **Counterfactual negotiation.** Derive candidate edits and role dossiers from those same families. Mark new drafts and private instructions as workshop constructs. Include genuine overlap, genuine conflict, and a changed draft that resolves an objection. Give equivalent legitimate actions the same grade. Record which inputs, turns and private fields each role could see. The pilot tests truthful disclosure and correct decisions under limited information, independently of how well a model imitates the wording of a historical speech. [General evaluation](research/unsc-marl-20261119/GENERAL_EVALUATION.md)

Joint case coverage should include at least the following distinction pairs: amendment versus final draft, adoption versus unanimity, support for a whole text versus objection to an omission, nonmembership versus abstention, and a live proposal versus official commitment. Use an event-family split across the two archives. The existing audit has 236 shared draft symbols as a lower-bound overlap screen. Preserve exposed cases as development anchors, including sanctions S/2022/925, Gaza S/2023/970, and climate and WPS cases already used in the project. Pilot widths, prompts and criteria should be locked before seeing the new-case outputs. [Release audit](reports/eval-pilot-20260926/unsc-bench-audit.json) · [Source calibration cases](research/unsc-marl-20261119/source_calibration.json)

## Final record rubric · 70 points

Freeze case-specific criteria and acceptable alternatives. Each of the five components carries 14 points. Keep the exact archive record or negotiated output, plus mechanical outcome when available.

| Component | Full-credit anchor | Common failure |
|---|---|---|
| Disposition | Names the instrument and stage, and correctly records adopted, not adopted, held, or conditional disposition. Where applicable gives the vote or reason. | Treats one affirmative amendment vote as a yes on the final draft, or treats numerical majority as sufficient despite a veto. |
| Operative action | Names the provision that changes, the proposed action, and who could implement it under the stated conditions. | Offers a generic aspiration or reports the vetoed amendment as adopted text. |
| Positions and reasons | Preserves each relevant role's dated position and gives a source-supported reason linked to the clause or action. | Copies one delegation's view into another or invents a future vote. |
| Common ground and open issues | Names genuine overlap, surviving objections and any term or verification needed to proceed. Handles honest impasse explicitly. | Forces consensus, invents disagreement, or hides an unresolved objection by summarizing only the majority. |
| Authority and scope | Separates source language, workshop preference, analyst proposal and official commitment. Keeps the correct timeline and speaker capacity. | States a proposal as government policy or a procedural statement as a national position. |

Alongside the score, report a strict contract requiring all required items and a clean fabrication guard. Material invented votes, collective support, binding commitments or changes to the adopted instrument fail the guard. Report partial coverage for diagnosis. On each criterion use met, unmet, or uncertain, and use lower and upper bounds for uncertain items. For repeatable model judging, every claim of a met criterion needs an exact response span and a supporting evidence span. For omissions use the requirement and an empty response span. A quote that occurs in the source still needs to entail the claim. [Exact final and mechanism judge instructions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/instructions.md)

## Discussion rubric and idea ledger

Log message ID, speaker, role, recipients, visible message IDs, stage/round, actual text and token count. Count delegate openings, actual peer-facing messages, solo revisions, formal votes and synthesis as distinct event types. Report round count, messages per agent, active agents, total input/output tokens, latency, cap hits, and time to first valid draft or correctly recorded deadlock. A parallel round has one shared visibility boundary. File order does not create a reply relationship.

- **Unique ideas.** An idea is an atomic claim or policy move with a stable object, owner, scope and force. Use an evidence-backed fact, a causal argument, a distinct amendment, a concrete objection, a clarification or a concession. Split independent requests. Merge faithful paraphrases and retries. Different numerical terms or authority levels get different IDs when they change the decision. Count disclosure of an existing dossier fact separately from a newly proposed action. Keep all original spans, including rejected clusters and uncertain merges. If two agents introduce the same idea in one simultaneous phase, record co-introduction without arbitrary priority.
- **Per-agent and group measures.** Count distinct relevant supported introductions, novel ideas in later rounds, repetitions, newly surfaced private facts, unsupported claims and qualified ideas strengthened beyond their evidence. Report the share of supported ideas captured in the final text, omission of eligible minority objections, and retention of unsupported ideas. Score unique facts shared over known private-fact opportunities, and integrated over facts surfaced. An irrelevant novelty or duplicate earns no credit. A zero-opportunity denominator is unavailable.
- **Arguments.** Rate each eligible material claim 0, 1 or 2. Zero has no relevant reason or contradicts the evidence. One gives a relevant reason without tying it to the clause or trade-off. Two ties a dated source or scoped assumption to a specific claim, policy consequence, or trade-off. Report unsupported arguments separately from missing reasons.
- **Interactions.** Rate each pre-registered valid peer opportunity 0, 1 or 2. Zero ignores the issue, misattributes it, or adopts an unsupported suggestion. One acknowledges it accurately with no consequential response. Two visibly uses a supported contribution or challenges an unsupported one with evidence and changes or defends the disposition on that basis. To award peer-specific credit, locate the actual incoming turn, the recipient's initial and revised wording, and absence of the same change in the matched solo revision. A correct repeated point is retention. A visible recurrence establishes exposure, with causal uptake still uncertain absent matching evidence.
- **Constructive handling and participation.** For each case milestone, flag no attempt, relevant but incomplete attempt, or completed and evidenced. For example, ask whether the delegation acknowledges the unresolved monitoring objection, offers a specific edit with a cost, and preserves the objection if the edit fails. Log respect or personal attacks on a separate flag. Speaking equality and talk volume describe participation. They are poor proxies for decision quality when one agent supplies the decisive fact.

For comparability report the 70-point final endpoint and a process dashboard. Key points (10), arguments (10), and interactions (10) can be displayed as separate anchored scores. Interaction is unavailable when no peer opportunity exists, so an apparent 100-point combined score would hide denominator changes across conditions. Keep counts such as number of turns and ideas as diagnostics, not reward terms. Consider only validated process measures as supporting rewards in a controlled ablation after a credible outcome baseline exists.

## Conditions and turn budget

Use a strong direct analyst, independent role drafts plus a common synthesizer, and peer revision from the same drafts plus the same synthesizer. Freeze the same final contract and match total generation ceilings. Record actual budgets, including input tokens and any judge calls. For private information, distinguish the local-information solo, all-information solo and reveal-all baselines. Shuffle messages across genuinely different matched profiles, preserving position and length as far as the task permits. If a change appears with a real message and disappears when its key evidence is removed, it is a stronger attribution test than a raw before/after score.

Start with independently generated openings and one peer-visible round for a bounded check. Then measure the same endpoint after two peer rounds to learn what an extra clarification buys. A later turn-budget curve can include zero, one, two and four peer rounds if the earlier evidence warrants it. Reporting caps as rounds without measured interaction effects risks rewarding a longer transcript. Pre-register stop conditions and handle early agreement and honest non-agreement on the same footing. Keep fixed-round and adaptive cost results distinguishable.

For each episode, retain the openings and a snapshot after every round. Log scheduled opportunities, submitted messages, admissible substantive messages, passes, and the number of newly established supported ideas per role. Search each snapshot for the first point at which the visible evidence suffices to certify the outcome, then record when the group first states it correctly. Use `unreached` when either event never occurs. On open-ended cases, use pre-authored evidence requirements for case milestones and reserve mechanical certification for cases with a formal decision rule. The Iraq result shows why both clocks matter. An agreement can be certified from the openings even when another round repeats every claim, while the disjoint variant can remain unresolved with equally valid messages.

For the next transcript calibration, distinguish the value of an intervention from its correctness. An evidence-backed turn can introduce a relevant fact with no observed uptake, advance an already known proposal by answering a specific objection, or change the final record. Code each separately. Have blinded reviewers mark short spans before reading the final and compare their idea boundaries and links. Then show the final to score retention. Keep eligibility review, novelty, observed uptake and projected usefulness as distinct fields. Run the same transcript with a duplicated paragraph, a faithful paraphrase and an uptake span removed. Unique counts should survive the first two edits, while only the affected uptake or retention labels should move in the last edit. [Contribution quality study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11936)

For judge calibration, use clear/wrong/ambiguous anchors and minimal flips of attribution, scope or an official verb, with length held close. Audit quote validity, criterion agreement, false-fabrication rate and missed-fabrication rate against an independent expert or second blinded review. Separately test blinded process annotations for idea splitting/merging, unsupported uptake and whether statements actually entered the final. Report evaluation coverage and failures alongside scores. Family-level paired comparisons are the generalization unit. Stratify and resample whole negotiation families only when enough independent cases exist.

## Current evidence and open design decisions

- The prior climate archive supplies 4 openings, 4 peer revisions, and 1 synthesis. In an explicitly partial ten-idea annotation, France, Britain, India and Brazil contributed 2, 3, 2 and 2 supported ideas in the opening phase, respectively. The revisions introduced no new annotated ideas. The final retained seven supported ideas and converted the bounded absence of a UK quota position into an unsupported rejection. Two draft annotation spans initially failed replay; the compound about Indian scientific evidence was absent from the final, and one apostrophe had to match the actual transcript. [Idea inventory](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/climate-idea-inventory.json) · [Turn and idea scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/climate-idea-scores.json)
- The earlier four-option structured MARL policy benefited on feasible agreement from true peer proposals and still correctly recognized no deadlocks in 1,200 infeasible variants. The frozen Qwen comparison improved strict agreements from 4/12 to 6/12 with peer proposals, alongside five episodes with unsupported ballots and zero correct deadlocks. The one-signal channel cannot distinguish all private menus. Evaluate a clarifying round and a stronger channel before interpreting further learning gains. [Structured and Qwen results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/README.md)
- The remaining decision is how to cut a fresh, cross-benchmark sample with enough minority actions and separate enough families. A balanced diagnostic subset and a natural-prevalence subset answer different questions. Historically plausible counterfactuals need explicit alternate outcomes and source ownership. No consensus score is established until it survives these checks.
- The draft [transcript judge instructions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/process-judge.md) give operational rules for splitting, merging, support, argument depth, milestones, peer uptake and final retention. The [current scorer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/measure.py) validates visibility and computes counts from the annotated inventory. The semantic coding prompt still needs independent judge calibration on natural discussions.
- An exhaustive scripted clarification control varies the number of honest question–reply pairs. In the four-option WPS workshop, both leader assignments and all option orders give 10,800 presentations of 225 private-mandate pairs. A disposition earns credit only if the leader knows an overlap or has ruled out every locally acceptable option.

| Maximum clarification pairs | Verified agreements among 8,400 feasible | Verified deadlocks among 2,400 infeasible | Unresolved | Mean query–reply pairs |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 5,760 | 0 | 5,040 | 0.000 |
| 1 | 7,440 | 1,344 | 2,016 | 0.467 |
| 2 | 8,256 | 2,208 | 336 | 0.653 |
| 3 | 8,400 | 2,400 | 0 | 0.684 |

The scripted receiver answers truthfully from its own private mandate. This establishes a protocol and cost reference, with no language model judgment about when to answer. The next model comparison can compare asking against volunteering facts, require evidence for a terminal decision, and score confidently wrong decisions separately from unresolved cases. [Scripted clarification scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/clarification-scores.json)

### Local judge calibration

Qwen3.5-4B judged two previously authored UNBench sanctions anchors using the existing frozen judge prompt. Both responses passed exact quote and schema validation, and both fabrication guards matched the authored judgments. Nine of ten criterion judgments matched. Both candidates explicitly state “Adopted 14–0–1.” The judge awarded disposition credit only to the otherwise correct record and asked the record with fabricated endorsement to spell out the same tally. This is a within-pair consistency failure. Add identical-correct-clause invariance checks, including polished and plain reformulations, before promoting these scores to a training reward. Keep false-fabrication and missed-fabrication rates separate from criterion agreement. [Raw model verdicts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/model-calibration/responses.json) · [Replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/model-calibration/replay.json)

### Two learned policies with counteroffers

The bounded structured MARL run added an optional truthful counteroffer to a negative reply. Over the exhaustive four-option workshop, the learned pair and untrained initialization both reached 8,400/8,400 feasible agreements and 2,208/2,400 verified deadlocks. Learning reduced counteroffers from 1,632 to 432 while adding 96 clarification pairs, a small gain at the declared communication costs. Suppressing learned counteroffers lost 48 agreements. Every remaining failure was an infeasible 3-versus-1 mandate pair with only two negative answers. The result isolates a protocol limit and gives a stronger control for language-model actors. [Counteroffer MARL study](research/unsc-marl-20261119/counteroffers/README.md)

### New benchmark-linked workshop pairs

Added matched agreement and impasse variants for the Iraq UNAMI mandate/review family and the Syria cross-border aid family. The Iraq vote labels agree across the UNBench author test and UNSC-Bench. The Syria draft is source-linked to both releases and its vote labels come from UNSC-Bench and the primary roll call. Keep official history, fictional private mandates, and any workshop disposition distinct. Role-private projections, the full-information analyst view, the writer view and the 70-point judge packet are available. [Case pack and audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/README.md)

### Selecting a reward for a language-policy test

Lewis et al. trained natural-language negotiators from agreement payoffs in a controlled setting with private preferences. CoSIR's preprint uses verifiable disclosure and request rewards, as well as rewards tied to incorporating received evidence. This suggests two separate comparisons for the UN workshops: a mechanical outcome reward with a strict evidence and authority check, then an ablation that additionally credits a previously unknown, decision-relevant constraint. Credit only information that changes a recipient's feasible options or resolves an uncertainty. Repeating already visible support earns no extra reward. The historical political position remains a separate measurement. [Deal or No Deal?](https://aclanthology.org/D17-1259/) · [CoSIR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17893)

### Measured Syria language-model pilot

In the cross-benchmark Syria family, base Qwen3.5-4B using peer revisions correctly selected B on the agreement case. Independent revisions and the full-information analyst called it unresolved. All three failed to label the proven impasse correctly. The peer impasse record also invented UK opposition to the historical nine-month draft. Both peer records exceeded the 130-word cap. All 16 calls replay. There were zero optimizer updates. [Pilot and final rubric results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/language/README.md)

The quote-checked submissions already contained both complete compatibility sets in all four writer inputs. Intersecting those statements recovered both outcomes, regardless of which revision arm supplied the statements. Track this evidence-availability check separately from decision success. A missed outcome can come from failure to elicit information or failure to apply information already disclosed. This example calls for testing a checked readout before assuming an information-sharing reward would repair the final writer. [Visible constraints and exact record IDs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/language/evidence/available.json)

### Checked readout and writer follow-up

Adding only a quote-derived compatibility field to fixed Syria writer inputs raised exact labels from 1/4 to 3/4. All four memos exceeded the word cap. One still denied the verified impasse. Another printed the right label but claimed both delegates accepted nothing, when both had nonempty and disjoint sets. Keep the certified outcome, correctness of its explanation and authority guard separately observable. [Matched writer comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/language/certified/README.md)

The new public decision ledger can certify disclosed overlap and impasse even with partial messages. It does not consult private mandates. Evaluation of disclosure truth is a separate environment-side operation. An exhaustive sweep checks 6,561 disclosure states against 65,536 admissible completions, plus same-owner contradictions and malformed messages. Replaying the four Syria writer inputs verifies the case outcomes only when both submissions are available. Next, test language delegates emitting typed partial disclosures and measure how much a peer turn contributes beyond the scripted protocol. Preserve natural-language reasoning as a separate outcome. [Ledger and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/README.md#partial-disclosure-readout)

### Iraq typed-disclosure and MARL baseline

The Iraq family now has two matched base-Qwen actor runs. The list response format rejected nine of ten outputs for exceeding a one-claim cap. The scalar format admitted ten truthful responses and delivered all four peer openings. Every second turn repeated the actor's opening. Both solo and peer scalar arms certified the agreement, with neither certifying impasse. The complete 225-world audit gives the channel 175/175 agreement and 42/50 impasse capacity at two claims per role, assuming a planner knows both menus. [Actor evidence and capacity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/README.md)

In a separate structured MARL fit, a terminal reward yielded 169/175 agreements and 0/50 impasses on the four-option grid. Adding a verifiable exclusion bonus changed the results to 45/175 and 20/50. That tradeoff rules out promoting the bonus alone. The held-out score excludes the bonus, keeps invalidity and repeated claims visible, and reports both outcome classes. [Controlled fit and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/LEARNING.md)

### Public clarification as a diagnostic

Use the same two openings and four 64-token continuation calls for free sequential disclosure and routed questions. A query chooses its recipient and option only from the public evidence. Log the actual question, whether the answer is truthful and on topic, and when the ledger can first certify a result. Pair each real transcript with truthful scripted answers to the same adaptive policy from the original openings. This isolates misses attributable to language answers from limits of the question policy.

An exact dynamic program over all 1,024 feasible or disjoint menu/opening presentations gives a stronger reference. Even with truthful answers and a planner optimized for the mean of the two class recalls, four query-only responses certify 880/916 feasible presentations and 48/108 disjoint ones at the chosen optimum. A facilitator who only asks questions has less information than a responder who can volunteer a specific private exclusion. This is a concrete reason to compare channels before attributing the earlier impasse failures entirely to the reward. [Protocol and public planner](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/README.md)

The frozen Qwen comparison on the four Iraq/Syria cases certified only the agreement already evident in the Iraq opening in either arm. Directed questions elicited five truthful new claims out of 16 replies. Free disclosure supplied 12 truthful claims out of 16, ten of them repeats. The same router with scripted truthful replies certified all four. Report claim-level truth and novelty side by side. For this reward decision, keep invalid formats, true repetitions, new but unnecessary facts, and wrong private-condition applications visibly distinct. [Matched comparison and evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/README.md)

On the same positive-opening grid, a role-local rule that can volunteer a signed claim certifies 856/916 agreements and 72/108 impasses within four continuation slots. This beats the public query-only planner on the mean of the two recalls. Under the alternating schedule, even a full-information chooser can certify at most 84/108 impasses. The missing 24 require three exclusions by one role, which gets two slots. Use the initialized role-local rule as the learning comparator and the full-information result as a capacity reference. Preserve both class recalls when testing a learned residual. [Exact voluntary-disclosure comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/README.md#allowing-voluntary-disclosures)

### Prompt-grounding control

On the same 16 saved Iraq/Syria query histories, extracting the relevant values from the answering delegate's own card and the queried package, then instructing a literal comparison, corrected all 11 previously false claims and preserved all five correct claims. In a live follow-up with the same original openings, router and call cap, the grounded responses established both agreements and both impasses. Score this as a prompt-grounding control for machine-readable private constraints. Credit the newly unlocked evidence separately from the final decision certificate. The router was scripted and the delegates were frozen. Use this stronger grounded baseline when evaluating learned communication, and test natural-language mandates separately. [Prompt-grounding results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/grounded/README.md)

### Learning after the scripted controls

An exhaustive audit of the 24 fixed role-local priority orders produced a stronger initialization with 916/916 agreements and 72/108 impasses. A 160-update shared-policy REINFORCE residual trained on simulated three-option presentations transferred to 898/916 agreements and 72/108 impasses. In the 18 regressions, the first change was a second-slot exclusion of a peer's accepted option where the script had offered a previously unaddressed option. The same learned policy depends on peer evidence, but the comparison with its initialization shows no benefit from learning. Keep the successful field-grounded language control and the tuned local script in future comparisons. [Sequential MARL experiment](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/voluntary/README.md)

A targeted scripted diagnosis then recovered every reachable miss on that grid. When the first continuing delegate has only one acceptable package, lead with a matching acceptance or the exclusion of the peer's proposed package before any general exclusion. Leave other action priorities unchanged. This preserved all 916 agreements and increased impasses from 72 to 84, the fixed-schedule capacity. The rule was chosen after inspecting the grid. The remaining 24 impasses require a different speaker schedule or message capacity. The next MARL comparison needs that stronger scripted control and a fresh evaluation grid or sourced case families. [Post-fit diagnosis](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/voluntary/README.md#post-fit-scripted-diagnosis)

### Learned flexible routing

Each signed claim can nominate the next speaker, including the current delegate. The flexible script certifies 108/108 four-option impasses, up from 84/108 under the earlier alternating control. It preserves all 916 four-option agreements. Routing-only policy iteration accepted no updates. Joint claim-and-routing learning accepted four local changes and transferred to shorter agreements on six-option cases, but lost impasse recall. A capacity guard that keeps a scripted exclusion when a learned acceptance would leave too few proof slots resolved the tradeoff. On the fresh seven-option grid it preserved all 190,498 agreements and raised certified impasses from 7,140 to 10,206 at the same aggregate slot costs. One trained keep-speaking rule reproduces the guarded traces on both the six- and seven-option grids.

The structured policy also improved the seven-package S/2022/925 UNBench workshop from four of six to six of six impasse openings and preserved all eight agreement openings. Score this as verification of the communication policy and certificate ledger. Natural-language delegates and the final written record are still separate evaluation endpoints. [Routing experiments and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/README.md)

### Matched final records after learned routing

On each of two sanctions opening pairs, both paths produce nine distinct scoped claims. Learned routing replaces a Brazil acceptance of F with the India exclusion needed to certify a genuine impasse by the seventh continuation. This difference would be invisible to a turn count or unique-idea count alone. In the first Qwen writing pass both guarded memos described the complete proof, while the scripted paths preserved unresolved status but did not identify the role missing on F. The pack supplied the resolution number but omitted the required draft symbol, so its first-round disposition score is confounded. Preserve the original and use the corrected instrument packet for the paired rerun. A case-specific deterministic renderer passes the same five final-record contracts on the corrected inputs. [Writer experiment and public proof](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/writer/README.md)

The membership-recommendation family S/2024/312 joins an archived UNBench draft and UNSC-Bench vetoed row 7, with the April 2024 explanations and roll call verified against the UN record. It adds a distinct authority distinction among a workshop agenda option, a Council membership recommendation, a General Assembly decision, and bilateral recognition. Both structured policies certify all 21 opening presentations here, so the family supplies a matched transfer check with no incremental routing effect. [Membership family, source audit and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/membership/README.md)

The corrected instrument rerun left the substantive scores unchanged at 42/70 for both guarded impasse records and 28/70 for the original script records and agreement control. Qwen still omitted the now-supplied draft symbol. One unresolved reply explicitly contradicted its own F status, and the agreement reply invented mutual compatibility for C. All ten generated records fit the word budget, and none passed the full contract. The deterministic renderer passes all five same-assistant-reviewed contracts. Carry the certified disposition and historical identifiers into the final record, then score any explanatory prose separately. [Full final-record results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/writer/README.md#paired-metadata-retest)

### Live actor test of the learned policy

A live integrated run used frozen Qwen delegates to sign the package chosen by either a scripted router or the trained policy. The roles saw the exact local mandate and package values, but all 25 distinct outputs were exclusions. Eight should have been acceptances and were rejected. Both arms followed identical admitted paths, certifying the membership impasse and leaving the sanctions and membership-overlap episodes unresolved. The exact-reply control preserves the prior routing advantage in the sanctions opening and certifies the membership agreement. This separates answer bias from routing competence. The final records faithfully state what the ledger could establish. [Live trace and controlled comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/live/README.md)

A separate five-card test asks Qwen to infer its own complete menu from prose before dispatch. Four menus are exact. This restores the learned sanctions impasse gain from 4/6 to 6/6 openings and both policies certify all 12 membership agreement openings. On the briefings-only membership card, Qwen incorrectly includes three renewed-review options. The verification layer keeps them out of the ledger, leaving all nine true impasse openings unresolved. Track upstream role interpretation accuracy in addition to routing and memo quality. Opening pairs share the same five model calls, so their decisions are correlated. [Prose-menu experiment](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/live/prose/README.md)

The prose follow-ups distinguished mandate interpretation from exact quote verification. Constraint extraction corrected the UK briefing-only menu but invented two restrictions for France. Order-swapped candidate review repaired France while refusing the UK menus because both justifications came from package descriptions. Field-wise review produced an exact France quote allowing either sequence and incorrectly used it to support a parallel-only restriction. Score exact quotation and entailment separately, with paired positive and negative judgments on the same quotation. None of the model-based projections certified the membership impasse, and all retained the sanctions gain where the two extracted menus were correct. [Prose variants and field calibration](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/live/prose/README.md)

### Empirical judge calibration and policy memory

The quote-attribution diagnostic separates mechanical containment from semantic entailment. Frozen Qwen accepted 5/16 supported claims and rejected 16/16 foils across answer-code orders, with only one complete stable pair. Averaged-margin ranking got 8/8 pairs right. On eight reserve pairs with new wording, averaged-margin ranking and direct order-swapped comparison each got 8/8, while absolute labels stayed at 9/16. Applying that direct comparison to actual disputed packages got 2/5 right, accepting all three ineligible UK renewed-review packages despite agreement across both orders. Keep the model judge outside the reward loop and require an application-matched check of card plus package, in addition to source-quote minimal pairs. [Judge calibration and application](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/entailment/README.md)

In a separate context ablation, showing only the last peer disclosure erased the learned impasse gain on the six-option grid and reduced agreement recall in both arms. The compact state of per-role acceptances and shared exclusions reproduced every full-history action and certificate. Preserve source ownership in the underlying ledger, and give the policy the complete state summary even when the conversational history is short. Score redundant evidence by whether that option was already excluded by either role, in addition to exact repeats by the same role. [Policy context and redundancy study](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/memory/README.md)

### Task-matched role calibration with partial evidence

Four new private mandates over the sanctions and membership menus expose a conjunctive reasoning failure. Order-swapped package comparison makes 15/28 correct judgments, one wrong judgment, and twelve abstentions. It admits no complete menus. The judge got both sides right in zero of the seven one-field package pairs. Audit evidence availability before assigning a failure to routing. The stable correct signs cannot prove the sanctions agreement, while the membership signs cover all seven required exclusions. A rule that retains stable individual signs and lets the current delegate finish its useful exclusions certifies 4/4 membership impasse openings. The existing scripted and learned full-menu protocols both certify 0/4 with the partial inputs. Do not score an abstention as an exclusion or use the evaluator's private answer to fill it. [Complete package panel and partial-evidence replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/entailment/README.md#task-matched-package-panel)

Field-first interpretation improves the same four cards to 11/12 exact restrictions in both answer orders. A single disputed reporting field leaves two possible readings, so preserve signs that are invariant across them. This verifies F under either reading and leaves E unknown. Immediate handoff after an affirmative proposal recovers the remaining agreement opening. Both routing policies then certify all eight openings, and the handoff rule preserves the certificate and turn totals on the prior complete-menu grid. This is a model-interpreted, source-verified interface result. The routing comparison ties on these cards. [Field extraction, uncertainty propagation and public records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/entailment/README.md#field-first-interpretation-and-uncertainty)

### Learning with incomplete private observations

A new shared policy trained with missing role signs promotes useful acceptances, but its unguarded transfer trades certified impasses for agreements. The exclusion-first baseline reaches every evidence-available impasse in the held-out synthetic panels. Preserving the remaining exclusion budget restores all 279 reachable impasses on a fresh seven-option sample. The availability-mask variant saves 885 agreement slots across 4,000 feasible presentations while certifying three fewer agreements than the baseline. Track the paired one-gain, four-loss split and the extra readiness metadata. The stricter guard preserves all outcomes and costs, matching the baseline. On the stored field-invariant UN inputs the established router still leads with eight of eight opening presentations certified, versus seven for the guarded partial variant. [Training, fresh stress test and UN transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/partial/README.md)

### Public task intent and local verification

An announced confirmation task lets a positive-first script reach every agreement that the available signs can support on the fresh six- and seven-option panels. Counterfactual learning accepts no updates. The mixed script protects every evidence-available impasse, so choose the objective from the task announcement rather than the hidden outcome. [Public confirmation objective](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/partial/intent/README.md)

Allowing one private check within the shared action cap increases both certificate classes. Joint learning changes ordinary disclosures and trades impasses for agreements. A proof-slot guard narrows that loss, and a restricted earlier-check fit accepts no updates. On the stored sanctions evidence, one check per opening certifies 2/4 agreements. Confirmation mode can check a second public offer under the same eight-slot cap, restoring 4/4 with six checks in 14 aggregate slots. This is a scripted recovery with recorded model interpretations and independent signed-evidence validation. The mode loses a membership impasse if used for the mixed task. [Lookup, controls and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/partial/lookup/README.md)

The check-or-defer follow-up keeps ordinary disclosures scripted and exhausts one-missing-sign training patterns at widths three and four. It accepts one earlier-check rule after protecting every training certificate. On a fresh width-seven sample the rule preserves all agreement outcomes and gains 107 impasses net with the same aggregate turn counts. The paired split is 154 gains and 47 losses. When checks can fail to return, the gain is 84 net, with 124 gains and 40 losses. Stored sanctions and membership outcomes tie the baseline. This evaluates the value and failure modes of a learned verification decision rather than a change to ordinary signing behavior. [Check-or-defer fit and stress test](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/partial/lookup/README.md#learning-when-to-check-or-defer)


### Which proposal should get the private check?

With check timing and ordinary disclosure frozen, a target-only counterfactual fit accepts zero changes across 38,160 smaller synthetic presentations. A perfect first-check target could add 175, 982 and 72 agreements on the three held-out panels, while adding zero impasses. This distinguishes a lack of useful targeting in the fitted observations from the potential benefit of choosing a better target for agreement.

On archived UN-derived confirmation cases, ranking eligible unknown packages by the frozen model’s raw local margins certifies 4/4 sanctions openings with one check each and 10 aggregate slots. The first-peer-offer rule certifies 2/4 under one check and 4/4 with up to two checks, using 20 and 14 slots respectively. The rank only selects which local check to request. The public proof requires a separately verified sign. Membership stays at 3/4 across all three arms because no check is triggered. These are correlated openings of two previously exposed case families. Score public check timing, target choice among eligible alternatives, check return, and certificate validity separately. Include first-offer and uniform-choice controls when comparing a learned target selector on new families. [Results and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/disclosure/clarify/routing/partial/lookup/README.md#choosing-which-package-to-check)

### An outer-space case with three distinct votes

The April amendment, the vetoed April draft, and the May draft form one evaluation family. The UNBench archive backs all three draft texts. UNSC-Bench backs the April final roll call. A 70-point retrospective packet tests procedural stage, each delegation's position and reasoning, genuine overlap, and official authority. An authored minimal pair holds nine criteria fixed and changes only whether the Council is said to have authorized negotiations. The common scorer gives 70 and 63, with the fabricated authorization failing the guard. This is a calibration fixture for a blinded judge. The companion public transcript example separates four national interventions, six selected ideas and two roll calls. It codes Japan's explicit response to the amendment as observable engagement without causal credit from a matched solo revision. [Source-backed discussion slice](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/historical-discussion.md)

The paired workshop uses a fictional seven-package matrix and changes one private condition to create agreement and impasse variants. With two authored signs removed per delegate, the frozen timing policy verifies 7,812 of 18,522 impasse presentations, up from 6,972 for the script. The paired split is 1,092 gains and 252 losses, with the same aggregate continuation slots and 336 more private checks. Agreement certificates tie. Report this as a controlled mechanism test within one new family. A matched frozen-model diagnostic measures whether the routing gain survives when uncertainty comes from interpreting the cards. [Outer-space case, rubric and controlled comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md)

After 42 frozen Qwen evaluations on the outer-space cards, 10 of 21 package signs are right, 10 are unknown and one is wrong. The learned and scripted mixed routes are identical, certifying 7/9 agreement and 0/6 impasse openings. The confirmation script certifies 9/9 agreements with zero check calls, so this family does not measure a useful ranked-target choice on agreement. Both effects are reported independently of the masked control.

On the impasse, five packages start without any verified exclusion. One or two single-package returns cannot meet the proof requirement. A retrospective authored control verifies and publishes two source-backed scope clauses, each of which excludes a set of packages, and certifies all six impasse openings in four continuation slots each. It also preserves all nine agreements. This tests a stronger communication action within the same turn cap. Keep clause fidelity, supported package consequences, publication cost and downstream certificate correctness separate when testing learned clause actions. [Frozen model evidence and clause control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#qwen-derived-initial-signs)

### Can a model verify a clause and can a policy use it?

In the frozen Qwen field-only diagnostic, Japan's first-stage exclusion and China's overlap exclusion are recovered. The China impasse card still fails because the model accepts staged deferral despite the first-stage requirement. Five of nine scope judgments are stable and correct, three are unresolved, and one is stably wrong. The scope protocol with model interpretations certifies nine agreements and zero impasses, compared with nine and six under authored interpretation. Track failure to verify the private clause separately from how the router acts on it.

With an exact synthetic scope provider and the same two charged slots for checking and publishing, eight counterfactual updates lift held-out verified impasses from 918 to 1,482 of 2,400 against the initial script. The paired split is 565 gains and one loss. Removing a quarter of role-level returns in expectation reduces the gain to 324 gains and three losses. Agreement counts tie the initial script. The model proof gap remains on the outer-space impasse. [Scope modeling and learned routing](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#model-interpretation-of-scope)

A stronger fixed schedule checks whenever legal and confirms a matching peer offer first. On the original test it exceeds the learned scope router on impasses and gives up a small number of agreements. A second fit starts from this stronger schedule and uses fresh synthetic panels. It ties the script's held-out certificate totals with exact and missing returns. Reliable agreement slots fall by 44 and impasse slots rise by 143. The experiments support buying more information with clause-level actions. They show no held-out outcome uplift over a strong fixed schedule, and the model still needs to interpret the mandatory clause reliably. [Strong schedule comparisons and refit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#comparison-with-stronger-fixed-schedules)

### First-stage obligation versus staged permission

Freeze wordings as well as roles and package order. On the same outer-space family, splitting stage obligation, stage prohibition and eventual obligation recovers the original China impasse and abstains on its paraphrase. Asking for the full scope set makes the opposite error on the neighboring mandate. It overstates the China overlap requirement in all three wordings. When one formulation yields a tie and the second points in the same direction, inspect both margins and retain a separately reported post-hoc control. A targeted corroboration rule recovers all six paraphrased impasse openings in this case, with the same seven agreement openings. Both adaptive routing policies tie on certificates, and the learned policy takes longer on the successful impasse paths. The result isolates a reading and calibration issue from policy quality. A fresh family with stage permission and a mandatory first-stage neighbor is the appropriate unit for evaluating this combination. [Wordings, predictions and adaptive transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#asking-about-first-stage-obligations-directly)

### Transfer to a distinct Mali negotiation family

Mali supplies two votes with different failure mechanisms and an atomic final-record rubric for separating them. In the fictional negotiation, the whole-set scope prompt gets all three fields right. Timing questions recover both impasse clauses but leave the overlap card's eventual requirement unknown. The frozen tie rule never activates. Both routers issue the same 10 correct certificates in the locked order with every scope provider. They use 16 continuation slots to certify the four impasses, matching the evidence-only bound. The six agreement openings use 21 slots against a seven-slot bound under joint knowledge of the evidence. Reversing the role order preserves all certificates and increases slots to 20 and 23 respectively. Keep paired cost and proof-capacity checks when correctness saturates, and count a scope clause as one contribution alongside the package signs it entails. [Mali benchmark, model transfer and role-order replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md)

### Propose first or verify first

Give the learned and fixed routers the same explicit new-offer action. In Mali, taking an opening offer reduces six agreement paths from 21 to nine continuation slots and increases four impasse paths from 16 to 24. All certificates survive, so a final-only result hides the agreement/impasse cost trade-off. Policy coverage also matters. A table with ten exact-key updates makes no changes when the main test has one more unknown sign per delegate than training. A shared feature representation reaches 895 of 4,000 reliable routes. Against a raw-key control trained on identical rows, it activates all ten rules rather than two. It adds a few agreements at the cost of some impasses, while the raw control preserves opening-offer certificates and gains one impasse with exact returns and two when some are missing. On this test the expanded action is consequential and the learned alternatives do not dominate the fixed schedules. [Action, feature and role-order controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#learning-whether-to-offer-before-checking)

### A turn can contain an offer and an exclusion

Compare learned and fixed routers with the same ability to send two truthful claims in one turn. Charge a second slot in a separate control so that a gain from message capacity is visible. On 2,000 impasse presentations per return condition, learning from bundle-first gains five certificates with reliable checks and ten when some scope returns are missing. Agreement certificates stay identical, and the learned path uses more agreement slots. A frozen transfer to wider menus ties bundle-first on every certificate. In the recorded Mali episodes the new move keeps all certificates, but its cost advantage still varies with speaker order and objective. Preserve the two atomic signs in the transcript while charging a single message, and verify both source attributions. [Bundle fit, wider panels and Mali traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#a-combined-offer-and-exclusion)

### Which parts of a combined message can enter the ledger?

Keep three stresses distinct. A sentence can accurately report another delegate's position without committing its speaker. A hypothetical revision can be acceptable without making the current package acceptable. An explicit claim can still contradict the supplied mandate. Code each elementary offer or refusal with its source, speaker, and condition. Evaluate admitting each sign separately and test the effect of an error at the first ledger state that could certify an agreement or impasse. The same raw claim must receive the same status wherever its answer label appears. Charge model calls as well as public message turns. [Negotiation dialogue annotations](https://aclanthology.org/D13-1035/) · [Mali and outer-space test fixtures](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/bundle-reader/README.md)

In eight authored two-claim messages, the joint Qwen reader admits three of eight valid claims and one invalid claim. Parsing the two claims separately recovers seven valid claims but admits four invalid ones and falsely certifies agreement at a constructed boundary. Retaining components common to all tied or order-swapped joint winners recovers six valid claims and admits two invalid claims without extra model evaluations. A repeated incorrect attribution survives simple corroboration between readers. Use separate metrics for preserving valid evidence, admitting false evidence and the resulting decision. [Reader results and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/bundle-reader/README.md#model-admission-and-ledger-replay)

A separate attribution prompt plus a gate based on earlier package checks prevents the constructed Mali false agreement. It still admits one false claim across the eight messages, treating the chair's question as China's acceptance. Retaining components stable across both answer orders raises valid admissions from three to five at no extra calls. The matched split-reader gate admits six valid and one invalid claim. Require semantic attribution and verified mandate support independently before using prose-derived signs as inputs to a learned router or as a reward. [Factored reader and matched controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/bundle-reader/README.md#separating-attribution-from-mandate-verification)

### Partial admission and an explicit resend

A trusted sender submits explicit package signs and a separate role-local check supplies the admissible signs. Keeping supported components independently preserves a Mali agreement when the exclusion is unavailable. Atomic admission withholds that agreement. The gate blocks incompatible signs and requires owner binding from the caller. A parser posing as the speaker can still turn a question into a false certificate if the package itself is permitted.

Under matched simulated faults, selective bundle-first preserves more certificates than atomic admission, while a noise-trained router trails the fixed schedule. On a fresh heavy-fault panel, resending only when the missing sign could complete the certificate raises agreements by 15 and impasses by 14 over never resending. The learned resend rule ties agreements and loses two impasses against the immediate-proof rule. Preserve paired correctness, fault exposure, retained signs and resend cost independently. [Ingress, fault sweeps and resend policy](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/guarded/README.md)

### Separate centralized training from decentralized evidence

A shared neural policy can learn from team returns while each acting delegate still sees only its own verified signs and the public ledger. Both paired teacher targets tie the bundle-first script on clean certificate counts and lose one agreement under faults. Neither model overrides the default in Mali. The centralized oracle adds nine noisy impasses on a 128-presentation subset, using information unavailable at action time. Seven identical local feature signatures on the fault probe contain opposite certificate effects for the same alternative action. Save the paired oracle gap, action overrides and feature-collision witnesses alongside policy scores. This makes it possible to distinguish an implementation that learned no transferable response from an environment limited by the evidence available at a turn. [Neural fits, privacy audit and paired traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/guarded/README.md#shared-neural-policy-trained-on-team-returns)

### An answer can carry the clause that supports it

Explicitly charge the question, reply, and respondent verification allowance. A question that only requests an existing sign loses to the no-question script in the paired simulation. Letting the respondent check a previously unknown sign increases agreement certificates, while consuming verification it might need to prove impasse. On a new wider panel, returning a verified scope clause with a negative answer recovers 160 impasses with exact returns and a net 37 with faults, compared with returning just the sign under the same match-only schedule. Agreement certificates stay paired. Learned question timing adds three reliable impasses over match-only and ties it under faults. Recorded Mali traces have the same outcomes under both speaker orders. Keep the information capacity of the response, learning effect, and prerequisite verification cost separate. [Query, response and transfer evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/guarded/README.md#a-scope-clause-can-answer-a-focused-question)

### Separate a package check from a scope check

Select which source to query before seeing its output. Scope can justify several exclusions, and package-level verification is still needed for a new positive. With one permitted source per request, the learned policy improves over its starting script by six agreements and five impasses on the reliable synthetic panel and by six impasses under faults. The fixed focused rule still certifies more agreements. Cached model evidence exposes a different failure: conflicting outer-space scope and package reads can eliminate evidence of a valid agreement, while a clean Mali receipt speeds some impasse certificates. Keep inference cost, evidence conflicts, and choice of source separately measurable. [Exclusive lookup, evidence conflicts and role-order replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/guarded/README.md#choose-the-evidence-source-before-looking-it-up)

### Myanmar transfer distinguishes reading the mandate from choosing a question

The UNBench-linked Myanmar case tests procedural chair speech versus India's national position and the adopted oral briefing versus fictional public reporting. Direct package inference yields ten correct signs, six unknown and five wrong despite correctly reading all timing fields. Independent consultation-leadership and confidentiality probes, composed with the timing evidence, recover all 21 signs. This restores enough evidence to certify agreement. In the same case, allowing a private question about an approved but unannounced package expands the clairvoyant ceiling from eight to all 18 agreement openings. The newly learned target schedule certifies only three of those openings and varies with package order. Track evidence availability, legal-action capacity, actual policy success and order sensitivity before attributing the final quality gap to MARL. [Myanmar historical rubric, source checks and routing](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/myanmar/README.md)

### A request can name several proposals and leave reply selection to the respondent

Treat the question as one interaction and each verified sign as one separately attributable claim. Asking about a package gives no evidence that either side accepts it. Count the packages offered, evidence newly obtained, questions that earn no reply, and the issues left unresolved at the end. Distinguish reply selection from ordinary turn scheduling when checking whether a relabeling changes the result.

With factored Myanmar receipts and two speaker orders, the new set request raises agreement certificates from 3 to 15 of 18. The fixed schedule and the learned schedule tie on the recorded transfer. They certify only 3 of 12 impasses, while the no-question and scope-first controls certify all 12. Cyclic relabeling still moves the agreement total between 12 and 15 even though the reply selector itself commutes with label changes in an exhaustive four-label check. With the original package readings both schedules still certify zero agreements. [Matched schedules, evidence controls and relabeling](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/myanmar/README.md#a-request-for-a-set-of-packages)

### Score locally indistinguishable evidence choices as paired cases

The same pre-reply evidence can require different checks for different hidden mandates. In a matched Myanmar opening, a scope check can complete the impasse certificate, while a focused check is needed to establish agreement under the alternative mandate. Both cases have the same withheld package labels, known signs and public opening when the respondent chooses. On this archive, a scope rule based on two remaining unknowns recovers all 12 impasses and removes all 15 agreements obtained by the focused set schedule. Report agreement and impasse separately, the cost of information that distinguishes the cases, and the performance of a matched fixed protocol before crediting a learned policy. [Source-choice traces and synthetic fault controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/myanmar/README.md#choosing-the-reply-evidence-source)

### Charge the verifier separately from the conversation

A lookup of one role's mandate can support more than one public claim. Record how often the verifier reads a source, how much input and output it processes, how many conversation slots the reply consumes, and which claims the ledger ultimately admits. Hold the reply format fixed when comparing fixed and learned policies. When a profile is ambiguous, keep only the claims supported by its stable fields and count the resulting abstentions. A query, the claims in a reply, and their uptake are distinct annotation units. Use the per-role unique-idea ledger to credit a newly established fact once even if several replies repeat it.

On Myanmar, a reply carrying both a package sign and scope-backed exclusions gives the fixed and learned schedules 15/18 agreement and 12/12 impasse certificates with previously verified fields. Joint extraction with six new Qwen3.5-4B calls leaves three of nine fields unresolved. Across the two individual answer layouts, the same fixed policy produces 15 and 3 agreement certificates and 12 and 0 impasse certificates. The stable-field policy yields zero certificates in either class. Constrained-choice mass exceeds 99% even on these disagreements. Keep response-format compliance, evidence stability and correctness as separate judge metrics. [Joint extraction, matched policy fit and single-layout control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/myanmar/README.md#package-and-scope-in-one-reply)

Pooling the two layouts' field probabilities retrospectively recovers all nine fields and all 21 package signs on Myanmar. The fixed policy again reaches 15/18 agreements and 12/12 impasses. Track the disagreements even when pooling produces a correct answer. This pool still uses both layouts and processes 4,108 input tokens across six calls, compared with 3,306 across the 18 earlier field calls. [Pooled score replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/myanmar/evidence/joint-receipt/pooled-layouts.json)

### Test the same verifier and decision policy on another UN family

On the Mali family linked to the UNBench draft archive and UNSC-Bench vetoed split, the frozen pooled verifier recovers eight of nine fields and 20 of 21 package signs. It removes France's requirement for at least eventual monitoring in one card, and the authored gate suppresses the resulting false acceptance. With pooled receipts, the frozen learned policy certifies six of 12 agreements and all eight impasses. The fixed early-request rule certifies nine agreements and all eight impasses, matching the legal-action ceiling. Add a ceiling calculation before attributing the remaining gap to training. Allowing delegates to publish already known information while preserving their verification allowance raises the ceiling to 12 agreements. [Mali transfer, attribution checks and shortest paths](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#transfer-of-the-compound-mandate-receipt)

The additional Mali proofs require four or five deferrals before the deciding lookup. In the deferral-enabled refit, none of the eight accepted updates selects deferral and the learned Mali counts stay unchanged. Save the eligible action, accepted training action, first point at which a proof becomes possible, and shortest successful sequence. This makes a credit-assignment failure visible when an action is useful only if peers follow through several turns later. [Deferral fit and exact witnesses](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#transfer-of-the-compound-mandate-receipt)

### Test credit for a sequence of interacting decisions

COMA evaluates credit relative to a single actor's alternative action with the others fixed. C3 substitutes one message and reruns the continuation, measuring its effect on the final reward. MAVEN tests coordinated exploration over multiple steps. In our replay, compare a one-edit policy update with a bundle of edits from the same complete trajectory and use common terminal rewards for both. Count how often the learned choices actually execute. Check whether a shorter sequence can achieve the same proof before attributing the result to delayed credit. [COMA](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.08926) · [C3 preprint, v4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06859v4) · [MAVEN](https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.07483)

The exact Mali frontier confirms minimum deferral counts of four, five and five for the three agreement openings at issue. The trajectory update improves on its matched one-edit control in synthetic agreement tests, with a small loss in impasse certificates. The fixed early schedule still wins clean synthetic tests and the learned deferral rule never activates on Mali. Count results where the alternative is legally necessary separately from results where it merely changes cost. [Trajectory fit and minimum-deferral frontier](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#learning-from-complete-trajectories)

A matched synthetic curriculum increases training presentations with no zero-deferral proof and at least three deferrals in the optimal witness from 45 to 83. Both learned policies score zero on the 102 test presentations selected by that rule and get the same Mali certificates. This makes the hard slice a useful separate endpoint. Aggregate agreement scores alone would hide the persistent failure to coordinate. [Matched curriculum and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/evidence/deferral-curriculum/results.json)

### Measure the capacity of the agents' actual observations

An oracle can choose different actions in two worlds that look identical to the acting delegate. Enumerate each opening's successful paths, then ask whether their action choices can all be expressed by one deterministic table that uses only the permitted observations. This gives an in-sample capacity check between a full-information oracle and a policy fitted exclusively on other episodes. In Mali, one 23-rule table certifies all 24 agreements and 16 impasses across the paired pooled and authored conditions, using the same features as the trained policy. The table was synthesized using the evaluated cases. The result places the observed gap in finding or transferring a policy with this representation, while leaving out-of-family capacity open. [Exact capacity audit and executable witness](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/evidence/local-capacity/mali.json)

### Distinguish acquiring evidence from disclosing it

After a verification, a delegate can retain the verified fields and disclose only the signed answer to a later question. Count source acquisitions separately from public replies, record which local receipt each reply relies on, and audit that the asker gains no commitment merely by naming a package. In Mali, this reuse plus permission to ask about the sole eligible package recovers all 12 agreements and eight impasses under both receipt conditions.
