On synthetic held-out menus it gains 229 reliable agreements and 18 reliable impasses with the same fixed schedule. Under faults it gains 116 agreements and loses four impasses. Keep the fixed protocol as the matched baseline for further team-reward learning. [Mali trace, verified receipt reuse and synthetic controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#reusing-a-verified-receipt)

### Record checkpoint selection and action coverage

Under the receipt-reuse protocol, a shared neural policy trained from terminal team reward converges to the fixed early-request schedule. It saves turns on reliable synthetic cases and loses 11 impasses with faulty receipts. The validation guard retains the initial checkpoint. A separate learned gate for asking early is never triggered on the wider held-out menus. Report the selected epoch, changes actually executed, class-specific costs and performance against both fixed schedules. A training update or an accepted rule alone does not establish a deployment gain. [Team-reward fit and opening control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#team-reward-fit-with-receipt-reuse)

On Myanmar, the fixed after-opening policy with receipt reuse reaches 18 agreements and 12 impasses in the original package order using complete archived evidence. It drops to 12 agreements in two cyclic package orders. The final neural checkpoint certifies nine agreements in every order. Keep the original-order score, the full rotation range and the receipt coverage together. [Cross-family replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/evidence/cache-transfer/myanmar.json)

### Test both package relabeling and attribute recoding

Sorting by the public package descriptions makes all seven package rotations produce the same trace and reaches all 18 Myanmar agreements and 12 impasses. A learned ordering rule retains these archive scores but loses one synthetic agreement and three impasses under faults compared with that fixed sort. The fix still depends on the structured vocabulary. In 144 attribute recodings that preserve the options and receipts, Myanmar reaches all 18 agreements in half of the encodings and 12 in the other half. Mali retains all certificates in both tests. Keep public package identifiers, attribute encodings and model evidence fixed or explicitly varied as separate controls. [Ordering, training and recoding replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#package-labels-and-public-field-encodings)

### Test acknowledgement and correlated delivery faults

Log intended signed reply, admitted signs, retry opportunity, and which claims arrive after the resend. A retry consumes a public slot and uses no new source lookup. Compare never retry, retry only for an immediate proof, unconditional retry, and learned retry under both independent and persistent faults with the same marginal rates. On the independent synthetic panel, the learner matches unconditional retry and recovers 42 agreements and four impasses over never retrying. A refit that sees persistent faults makes 44 fewer retries than unconditional retry on the persistent panel and adds one agreement, at the cost of seven additional total slots and 12 checks. It does not change the archived Mali or Myanmar outcomes relative to unconditional retry. [Receipt traces, channel fit and transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#retransmitting-an-acknowledged-missing-reply)

### Score retry timing as an interaction

If a delegate can disclose known evidence between attempts, compare disclosure-first and retry-first schedules with the same two nominal actions. Count the retransmission as an additional turn and the signed proposition as the same idea. Record the first successful delivery, any change in proof, and whether an intervening disclosure made the resend unnecessary. In the Myanmar replay with persistent delivery faults, the learned schedule recovers nine agreements over immediate retry while retaining all of that arm's impasse certificates. Under independent faults the same policy loses eight impasses. Keep those results separate and retain the paired transcript with exact attribution. [Timing fit, order comparison and Myanmar transcript](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#resend-timing-and-the-intervening-disclosure)

### Calibrate what counts as an idea, evidence, or a decision

Conversational Information Gain tracks atomic claims in an evolving memory and scores novelty, relevance and implication scope separately. Conversational Uptake measures how a reply builds on an interlocutor's contribution. For this benchmark, keep the sender's prior propositions and each participant's delivered evidence visible separately. Give credit for supported uptake only when the underlying claim reached the recipient. A deletion check measures whether the proof depends on one delivery with the rest of the trace fixed. A paired routing replay measures the effect when later actions can change. [Conversational Information Gain](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.2203/) · [Conversational Uptake](https://aclanthology.org/2021.acl-long.130/)

The Myanmar calibration set contains a lost resend, a recovered acceptance, a redundant delivered copy, a resend that also introduces a distinct exclusion, and a final record with invented official authority. The reference scorer verifies per-agent turns, signed and public unique ideas, the first point of agreement certification, and the result with the resend removed. The four judge labels preserve sender novelty, public delivery, dependence of the proof on the resend, and official authority as independent fields. [Runnable judge and typed anchors](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/retry_judge.py) · [Mechanical reference](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/evidence/retry-judge/anchors.json)

Qwen3.5-4B reads all five fixtures in two answer-code orders. Keeping only fields where the orders agree gives 12 correct, three wrong and five uncertain labels out of 20. It misses the invented official adoption in both orders. A retrospective pool gives 16 correct labels, including all five public-delivery labels, and still misses the authority violation. A hybrid uses the typed record for novelty, delivery and proof dependence and keeps the model's judgment for authority. It reaches 19/20 and still fails the authority test. Use the ledger to score typed proof dependencies and calibrate the authority criterion on final prose separately before using the joint score as a learning reward. [Raw judge scores and paired fixture audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/discussion/evidence/retry-judge/paired-audit.json)

### Hold final-record quality apart from the workshop certificate

The Myanmar and Mali final-record controls take the same signed replays and score official disposition, procedure, workshop status, supporting disclosures and authority at 14 points each. The bounded records of agreement, impasse and unresolved status all earn 70 on the authored reference. Changing only the authority sentence gives 56 and a failed fabrication guard in all five pairs. A faithful unresolved record can earn full writing credit while its decision remains unresolved. Log certificate success and final-record quality separately when choosing a MARL reward or comparing the single and multi-agent arms. [Quote-checked 70-point controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/evidence/authority/final-anchors.json)

### Verify the authority-bearing claim against the exact source

The isolated whole-record authority guard reaches three correct and five uncertain judgments across eight short Myanmar and Mali records and two answer orders. All four accurate records are uncertain. Pooling the logits at a zero threshold rejects every record. With an authored projection of each authority-bearing claim and a direct source-entailment prompt, the same model judges all eight correctly in both orders. Assess claim extraction and source entailment as separate stages. In a final-record score, report unsupported official claims separately from directly contradicted votes or decisions. [Matched authority probes and calibrated margins](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/evidence/authority/README.md)

The Gaza and climate archive control checks six clauses over three answer layouts, with four excerpts preserved verbatim from model outputs and two authored contradiction controls. The judge gets one correct stable three-way label, one wrong label and four uncertain labels. Collapsing contradicted and unestablished attributions into a single guard failure yields two correct failures and four uncertain guards. The result supports retaining source fragments and a distinct uncertain status when grading archived prose. [Archive calibration and guard results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/evidence/authority/archive/guard.json)

With identical archive clauses, source excerpts and system instructions, a binary prompt yields one stable correct guard and five uncertain guards. Averaging the margins from the two reversed answer orders correctly resolves all six at a zero threshold. Keep this exposed-set result separate from the three-way explanations and from fresh-case reliability. [Binary logits and matched input audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/evidence/authority/archive-binary/format-audit.json)

### Check what the sender can learn from a delivery failure

The retry learner compares mean and worst-group team-return estimates across simulated independent/persistent faults and agreement/impasse cases. The mean policy gains three held-out synthetic agreements without changing the impasse totals. It gains two persistent Myanmar agreements and loses two under independent faults in archived replay. The worst-group variant keeps immediate retry throughout. Seven of 51 exact sender observations support both a gain and a loss for the same alternative action. Report the label-blind policy, the in-sample observation table and the per-episode oracle separately, including whether a gain requires sacrificing any previously certified decision. [Risk comparison and observation limits](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/mali/README.md#learning-across-independent-and-persistent-faults)

### Score distinct decisions from the same meeting

The Venezuela meeting contains two non-adopted drafts with different reasons for failure. Both draft texts occur in the pinned UNBench archive. The U.S. draft's full roll call also matches the UNSC-Bench vetoed split. In a final record, require a separate row per instrument, with its tally, official disposition, reason and speaking capacity. For guard calibration, pair each accurate clause with a close false variant that changes one material fact. [Alignment and case report](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md) · [Official meeting record](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/3794247/files/S_PV.8476-EN.pdf)

On eight authored Venezuela clauses, the locked binary authority judge makes one correct stable decision and seven abstentions. Pooling two reversed answer orders catches all four fabrications and incorrectly rejects two of four accurate clauses. It ranks the fabrication above the accurate clause in every matched pair. The archive-fitted midpoint repeats the two false rejections. The Venezuela-fitted midpoint would clear an unsupported attribution in the Gaza archive. Report pair ranking, both guard error directions and uncertainty at the family level. Retain the original threshold for comparisons. [Frozen scores and per-clause margins](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/evidence/authority/metrics.json) · [Threshold transfer replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/evidence/authority/threshold-diagnostic.json)

### Track whether a reply resolves what the delegate asked

In the linked fictional workshop, the receiver can ask about a set of packages. Score whether the reply addresses a member of that set, uses a role-owned receipt, and adds a distinct proposition that enters the public ledger. A set query contributes no acceptance. Track receipt access separately from the selected claim and the first certificate. With exact authored receipts, permitting a delegate to select a verified compatible package or disclose verified exclusions raises fixed-policy agreement certificates from 3/30 to 30/30 and retains 20/20 impasses without extra checks. The wider fault replay gains 16–19 agreement certificates and loses 1–3 impasses per matched policy and fault condition, from 850 presentations per outcome class in each cell. Carry the stronger fixed protocol into comparisons, and keep agreement and impasse separate when judging any team-reward learner. [Turn traces, counterfactual paths and frozen policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#fixed-protocol-transfer)

### Compare a learned reply choice with a fixed compound reply

The shared selector trained on synthetic team outcomes recovers one extra independent-fault holdout impasse from 1,000 presentations and saves two slots. It adds four checks under persistent faults with identical certificates. It changes no outcomes or costs in Venezuela, Myanmar or Mali. In the successful trace, the scope-only arm admits 11 distinct signed propositions and stalls. The compound reply plus a resend resolves impasse with 10. Count distinct admitted propositions and score whether each eliminates an option that was still live. A post-hoc fixed rule that prefers the compound reply when a target is available matches the fit on outcomes and costs across the two synthetic panels and the three Venezuela conditions. [Fit, deciding trace and matched fixed rule](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#learning-which-reply-to-send)

### Separate skipping a query from buying one turn of evidence

Use a legal-route search with the fixed reply and retry rules to bound how many certificates are reachable on the same fault tapes. In Venezuela the current schedule reaches 180 agreements and 49 impasses under persistent faults. A future-tape search reaches 250 and 182, and the union of three fixed schedules covers 221 and 167. Report all three comparison levels. They give different evidence about the scheduler, the action budget and what an actor could infer from its observations.

The synthetic fit learns no protected rule for forgoing the query. A single-turn deferral fit gains 12 agreements and three impasses under independent faults. It gains seven agreements and one impasse and loses one agreement under persistent faults. The fitted rule activates in none of the three UN-linked workshops. Track activation by family and by whether the peer has a cached receipt. On Venezuela an in-sample exact-observation table can recover eight persistent-fault impasses without giving up a single certificate, even though the transferred policy makes no changes. [Search, fit and support audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#learning-when-to-query)

Training only on cold-receipt decisions activates the rule in the exposed Venezuela workshop. The matched replay loses 56 agreements for two additional impasses under independent faults, and loses 18 agreements for 47 more impasses under persistent faults. Report individual gains and losses alongside aggregate recall by regime. Matching the receipt condition closes the activation gap, while decision quality still varies between families and fault conditions. [Cold-receipt refit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/evidence/query-deferral/cold-fit.json)

### Measure the reader before crediting the router

The Venezuela Qwen3.5-4B probe reads three private cards in both answer orders. Using the cards for initial signs and later replies, the two orders give opposite failure patterns. One certifies 0/30 agreements and 20/20 impasses. The other certifies 30/30 agreements and 0/20 impasses. Retaining only stable fields certifies neither class. The frozen Mali pooling rule recovers all nine fields and 21 package signs, restoring 30/30 and 20/20 under the same router. Record which fields were disputed, which package decisions depend on them, and whether the policy starts with authored or model-read disclosures. A one-field timing repair restores the agreement with authored initial signs but leaves it unresolved with model-read initial signs. [Compound receipt probe and field-level attribution](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#reading-the-roles-mandate-from-prose)

### Train on team returns and inspect each routing change

A shared router trained on synthetic counterfactual actions gains 38 agreements and nine impasses net across clean and faulted synthetic holdouts. On the model-read Venezuela case it preserves all 50 clean results and recovers 31 agreements without losing any. It also recovers six impasses and loses nine. The same actor loses agreements on Myanmar and Mali, sometimes even with clean delivery. Compare fixed and optimized continuations as training targets. Record action changes and trace-level gains and losses alongside the scalar reward. In the Venezuela gain, the learned trace certifies agreement in five slots with seven signed propositions. The fixed trace uses eight slots and eight propositions and remains unresolved. On a Myanmar loss, two policy overrides interact even though either override alone still permits a certificate. [Sequential fit and action ablation](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#learning-routing-actions-throughout-the-workshop)

### Give the actor only the delivery evidence it can see

An acknowledgment for the actor's own prior send is too late to preserve the Venezuela routing gains. One public complete/incomplete bit after a signed ordinary send lets a frozen policy retain all 31 gains while preserving clean cases. A refit on synthetic team returns improves noisy Myanmar agreements and persistent-fault impasses, but loses nine Mali agreements in each regime. For 17 of 30 eligible observation patterns, the same deferral both rescues and breaks certificates across episodes. Log the owner and timing of each status bit, its bandwidth, and the exact observations that permit conflicting results. [Acknowledgment interventions, fit and observation bound](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#using-delivery-acknowledgments-to-choose-when-to-defer)

### Separate a successful retry from a successful decision

A learned resend of ordinary signed evidence gains five Myanmar agreement certificates under independent faults and loses seven independent-fault and ten persistent-fault Mali impasses. In the Myanmar witness the resent exclusion is still lost. The win comes after the resend hands the next turn to the peer. In the Mali witness the missing exclusion arrives and the decision still fails because the resend displaces a verification. A resend alongside a planned claim is never eligible on the three UN families when the packet must carry one acceptance and one exclusion. On Venezuela there are 492 relevant turns with one planned claim and an outstanding claim of the same polarity. Measure eligibility, occupied slots and turn ownership before attributing effects to retransmission. [Resend fit, distinguishing traces and eligibility counts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#repairing-an-ordinary-disclosure-that-failed-to-arrive)

### Isolate who gets the next turn

With two same-polarity claims allowed in a packet, the fixed repair rule recovers one Venezuela impasse while preserving the speaker schedule. Separately, a speaker-only intervention reproduces the earlier Myanmar win with the same undelivered exclusion and identical public prefix. A synthetic team-reward fit for this decision recovers two other Myanmar agreements and one Venezuela agreement with zero linked-workshop losses on the evaluated tapes. Preserve the entire matched prefix, next-role change, first certificate and per-role proposition counts. Report the synthetic and UN-linked panels separately. [Packet and speaker experiments](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#separating-packet-repair-from-turn-ownership)

### Check whether coordination needs both decisions

Evaluate four paired branches with either, both, or neither policy intervention. Use the same delivery tape and check each action against the state it would actually observe. On new Venezuela fault tapes, one impasse requires one delegate to defer a query and the other to yield the floor. Another agreement requires both decisions from the same delegate. This distinction matters for claims of cooperation. Joint and separate fitting both select a single intervention in these experiments. Report how often both decisions are available, whether both are necessary, and whether the necessary actions belong to different delegates. [Joint fits and crossed branch witnesses](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#joint-credit-for-two-interacting-decisions)

On a fourth linked family, outer space, the frozen handoff rule adds eight impasse certificates under persistent faults with authored receipts and partial initial signs. Removing the holdback restores clean agreements and removes all clean impasse certificates, even when both roles have exact authored local evidence. Log where evidence is verified, available to the actor, and actually published. A larger initial evidence set can change the policy's action schedule without supplying a complete public proof. [Fourth-family replay and model input control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/evidence/joint-repair/space-unheld.json)

### Count training examples that actually need cooperation

Of 10,200 synthetic training presentations, 17 expose both local decisions in one paired branch and zero need both decisions. Prioritizing the joint slice keeps the fixed policy. An ordinary team-return fit recovers 51 Venezuela agreements on new fault tapes, while still missing the Venezuela impasse that requires both roles. A targeted search over synthetic delivery tapes produces one verified two-role agreement example in development and zero in training or test. Report the number of eligible starts, the number that certify only with both actions, and whether both actors contribute. A selected challenge transcript gives a mechanism check. Split-level support is needed to measure whether that behavior can be learned and transferred. [Curriculum, search outcomes and paired transcript](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/venezuela/README.md#coverage-of-episodes-that-need-both-actions)

### Check whether team reward learns to deliver and use evidence

The benchmark kit gives two private holders a two-round relay to the chair. It counts a forced answer as valid if it fits the actual world and warranted if the delivered evidence rules out all worlds where the answer fails. Both sources are required for warranted decisions in all four Mali worlds and one Myanmar world. Training with only around 23% impasse exposure makes a chair without the precomputed warrant flag select a package every time on the four linked workshops, even after receiving both cards. At balanced agreement and impasse exposure, outcome-only training instead learns to stay silent and choose hold. Training with the evidence-warranted outcome reward learns to deliver both cards and gets all 16 linked cases right, including the larger Venezuela and outer-space menus. Match action correctness with a visible proof and record when a muted source changes one metric without changing the other. [Relay experiment, ablations and exact transcripts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#train-a-two-round-relay-with-explicit-private-facts)

Swap frozen teammates one seat at a time. In the balanced relay cross-play, replacing only the chair with the outcome-only policy preserves delivery of both cards in all 16 worlds and reduces warranted decisions from 16 to nine. Swapping either communication seat breaks complete delivery. Track which role chose an action, which source owned the evidence, and what the downstream actor actually received. [Frozen mixed-policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/relay-marl/crossplay.json)

### Reward-train a model-backed relay with a fixed reasoning control

Frozen Qwen3.5-4B starts at five of eight warranted greedy outcomes on the Mali and Myanmar relay fixtures. A flat action head learns to deliver every card and overuses impasse, reducing the result to four of eight. A head conditioned on whether both delivered cards are present and overlap reaches eight of eight. A fixed public overlap rule with the same learned communication matches it. Give the chair both cards as a diagnostic, independently swap the communication and chair adaptations, and report both greedy and legal-action sampling scores. The observed gains come from delivery and a decision-stage rule; this experiment uses constrained action letters. [Model-backed relay, role ablations and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#test-the-relay-with-model-scored-prose-actions)

### Check that the final memo preserves the proven decision

Qwen3.5-4B receives the same checked status and evidence used by the relay. In two evidence-withheld memos it prints UNRESOLVED and then contradicts the status by calling an impasse or inventing the missing card. The Mali partial memo also misstates why the second draft failed. Complete-input memos score 49–56 out of 70 and pass the authority guard, with all six distinct memos missing the standalone status line. Family-only instructions remove a cross-family intrusion, while none of four prompt controls clears the critical guard. An evidence-bound reference renderer satisfies the format and preserves the visible card ownership and historical facts on these cases. Score the final body, procedural fidelity, and formatted status independently of the decision certificate. [Matched writer inputs, 70-point review and reference render](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#test-the-final-prose-after-a-checked-decision)

### Check whether a memo judge penalizes justified uncertainty

The frozen two-order Qwen judge catches all six flawed memos and rejects four of eight sound ones, including both faithful unresolved reference records. A midpoint calibrated on Myanmar would release one flawed Mali memo. Keep false rejection of justified uncertainty visible before using a judge score as a team reward. The separate compiler derives status from delivered ownership and the proposed action, and renders verified source spans. It passes the exact heading and word cap in 96 recorded replays and rejects all 12 injected routing or source inconsistencies. [Judge, fallback and compiled-record controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#audit-the-memo-gate-against-sound-unresolved-records)

### Test transfer to wider decision menus

On new prompts for the seven-option Venezuela and outer-space workshops, the frozen Qwen relay warrants 1/8 decisions. Learned communication delivers both cards in all eight worlds and warrants 2/8 with the frozen chair. The learned chair alone reaches 7/8. Combining the two reaches 8/8, matched by learned communication with a fixed overlap rule. One round or either single-source mute leaves 4/8 warranted. With both sources muted, six guesses are correct and zero are warranted. Compare agreement and impasse separately and log how much of the decision was already warranted before a peer replied. These are constrained actions on four worlds per family, with seven options but only one feasible choice in either agreement world. Memo review remains a separate endpoint. [Component ablation, compiler and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#transfer-the-frozen-relay-to-seven-option-workshops)

### Distinguish a feasible package from a better compromise

For the ranked Venezuela and outer-space variants, each fictional card gives a package a score from zero to three. A zero rules it out. Among packages both roles accept, maximize the lower of the two scores, then the combined score. Accept any exact tie. This is the announced rule of the simulation. The official records supply the setting and remain the authority for historical claims. Nine private-card combinations per family include exact ties, impasses and preference reversals that leave the feasible set unchanged.

For the final judge, grade disposition against the mechanically checked set, then score reasoning separately. Require exact spans for both roles' relevant scores or ordinal reasons and the comparison that decides between the selected package and a viable alternative. Credit an accurate tie with either tied package and flag an unsupported claim of uniqueness. If the visible cards leave different winners possible, require the memo to distinguish a conditional suggestion from a verified best compromise. Count a rank disclosure as a new supported idea only if the ranking was previously unavailable and can affect a comparison. Give uptake credit when the recipient uses it in a traceable revision.

The fixed policy that takes the first feasible option makes 18 valid dispositions, 13 of them warranted optima. The typed policy trained for warranted optimality gets 18/18, matching the fixed compromise rule. The same learned architecture trained for feasibility gets 9/18 warranted optima, with all eight impasses missed despite full delivery. With either card muted, the optimality-trained policy warrants zero decisions. Report agreement and impasse alongside feasibility and compromise quality so gains on agreement cannot hide losses on deadlock. [Ranked fixtures, reward comparison and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#choose-among-several-feasible-compromises)

### Measure what the chair extracts from prose

Apply the task-authoring ceiling to the ranked cases. The zero-, one- and two-round correct-choice ceilings are 4/9, 4/9 and 9/9 for Venezuela, and 4/9, 5/9 and 9/9 for outer space under the fixed delivery route. In both cases the warrant ceilings are 0/9, 0/9 and 9/9. A better guess after one round can still leave the chair without grounds to certify any choice.

Frozen Qwen3.5-4B with both prose cards identifies four of ten best packages and six of eight impasses. Filtering its scores to feasible options produces five of ten best packages. With either or both cards absent, all 14 distinct linked views warrant only an unresolved report. Qwen supplies none. Keep three judge anchors separate in the next comparison: a feasible but inferior package, an asserted impasse with a missing card, and a justified statement that the visible cards leave more than one outcome possible. [Language chair, information ceilings and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#give-the-ranked-chair-prose-cards)

### Require both decision coverage and warranted restraint

The first joint team-return fit uses Qwen's frozen scores and grades parsed from delivered prose. It picks the best compromise in all ten agreements while missing all eight impasses. Training with balanced agreement and impasse weights plus delivery masks reaches 18/18 on complete evidence. It also handles every two-card outage and fails every one-card outage. The same coverage training using grades alone learns to stay silent and report unresolved even in clean worlds. Evaluate complete agreements, complete impasses, partial evidence and zero evidence as distinct conditions. Report completed decisions alongside justified abstentions so one cannot conceal the loss of the other. [Joint fits, role swaps and routed evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#train-communication-and-ranked-decisions-on-a-shared-return)

### Check when a missing card still permits a decision

Preserve each possible scorecard as a set of grades that travel together. With the visible card `(3,3,3)`, the two public domains `{(1,1,1),(1,2,2)}` and `{(1,1,2),(1,2,1)}` have identical per-package ranges. In the first, both B and C are optimal under every possible card. In the second, neither is optimal under both. A rubric that sees only ranges cannot distinguish the cases.

Pair incomplete views that warrant a decision with views that warrant uncertainty. In the Venezuela and outer-space controls, narrow the public set of cards for one seat while keeping the visible card fixed within each paired comparison. Include an agreement, an impasse and two still-ambiguous views per family. For the final record, credit a definite decision only when the evidence supports it across the public alternatives. For an unresolved report, require compatible completions whose sets of best choices have an empty intersection, or an equivalent argument that excludes every candidate. Accept any member of a tied set that remains optimal in every completion. [One-card comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#distinguish-missing-evidence-from-an-unresolved-decision)

### Verify the preference before scoring its use

Write private ranked mandates in terms of the clauses in the existing workshop options. An agent can misread its own mandate, decline to disclose it, or send it accurately. Record those outcomes before checking transport. A receipt confirms delivery and attribution. The mandate clauses establish whether the grade in the receipt is true. Score each choice against the actual decision and, separately, against the evidence in the receipt.

Keep each package grade separate when a disclosure mixes valid and invalid statements. Award credit for an individually corroborated grade and test whether that partial evidence still suffices for a warranted decision. Use private-truth filtering as an oracle diagnostic. A deployed verifier needs its own evidence for a grade. For order sensitivity, rotate the displayed response choices and log when agreement between orders retains a shared error or discards a correct answer. [Mandate extraction and routed decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#recover-and-disclose-a-ranked-mandate)

Putting the package terms next to their grades raises Qwen's matched mandate readings from 6/12 to 11/12 in each answer order. The same reversed preference survives both orders. It leaves all 18 decisions factually correct, with only 15 warranted by fully verified cards. A grade-by-grade oracle verifies enough evidence for the other three. Include examples where a misread preference reverses the decision and where it changes only the correctness of its explanation. [Matched prompts and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#recover-and-disclose-a-ranked-mandate)

### Score the repair caused by a clarification

Log which claim triggered the question, who answered, the exact grade and source, the prior and revised receipts, and which subsequent decision used the revision. Credit a correction only when it resolves an earlier error or uncertainty without replacing a supported claim with a new error. Keep query attempts, successful replies and accepted revisions distinct. Compare with a fixed target and no query under the same decision rule.

The lowest-margin rule asks the right Venezuela delegate about its misread preference and gets the correct grade. Replacing receipts from consistent replies alone drops warranted decisions from 15/18 to 12/18 because other replies introduce errors. A local source check admits the one real correction and reaches 18/18 on this exposed panel, matched by asking the relay every time with the same source check. The three repaired worlds reuse one mandate and one answered question. [Clarification and source-check results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#check-a-targeted-clarification-against-its-source)

### Test whether verified preferences survive the final memo

Pair memos that receive the same verdict with reported and verified receipts. Grade the corrected preference, comparison between packages, chosen action, and authority separately. In eight generated memos, all three repaired Venezuela variants preserve the corrected fallback. One chooses B in the body under a correct D heading. Another gives the correct B choice but omits the sum that breaks the tie. The incomplete and tied cases import historical procedure into the workshop outcome. Require the final-record judge to read beyond the heading and cite any contradictory body span.

None of the eight generated memos clears the exact heading, word limit and factual and authority guard together. Reference records compiled from the same packets reproduce the source claims and fit the 120-word cap. Report that control beside the free-form writing scores. [Ranked memo rubric, quotes and records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#write-a-final-memo-from-the-verified-ranked-record)

### Score the explanation and the released memo separately

Fix the heading, attributed grades, historical evidence and authority limit from the verified packet. Give the writer only the comparison or evidence gap. Test whether it preserves the decisive numbers, provisional status and correct next actor. A one-sentence prompt raises full reasoning passes from 1/8 to 4/8 on the matched cases. Both impasses, the withheld-card case and the tie still need the compiled reference. A narrow release grammar checks actual package IDs, decisive numbers and provisional wording. It keeps four unchanged model sentences, falls back on four reference records and rejects all 20 injected changes. Report coverage of accepted model prose alongside correctness of the released record. [Generation, review and release](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/README.md#keep-verified-facts-fixed-while-generating-the-explanation)

### Validity audit of the 18/18 ranked decision result

The action scores reproduce. Applying the same completion procedure to frozen Qwen changes its results from 10/18, 0/14 and 0/8 to **10/18, 12/14 and 3/8**, compared with the fitted chair's **18/18, 14/14 and 7/8**. A simpler rule that ignores the advertised priority for the worse-off delegate exactly reproduces all 40 acceptable answer sets. Keep this as a diagnostic of action choice. Seven new contrasts distinguish otherwise indistinguishable rules, including a missing-evidence case that requires three possible worlds to demonstrate uncertainty. For transcript and memo claims, use a held-out paired comparison with a fixed-transport chair and resource-matched solo/independent-draft controls. [Audit, runnable contrast generator and handoff](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md)

On the new controls, the fitted chair scores 18/22 with the shared completion procedure against 9/22 for frozen Qwen. It passes both lower-grade priority permutations, both sum tie-break permutations and all six three-completion uncertainty cases. The procedure fails four of six presentations that have a common optimum across tied completions. It intersects individual model choices instead of sets of defensible choices. Keep the full-set key and the six-presentation failure visible. For an unresolved record, a set of compatible worlds with no shared optimum is sufficient evidence. Two differing optimum sets alone are insufficient when they overlap. [Per-contrast results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#measured-chair-results-on-the-diagnostic-contrasts)

A post-test readout expands the original chosen package to other options with identical public minimum and sum before intersecting across completions. It recovers the four fitted failures and matches all 22 complete answer sets. Frozen Qwen remains at 9/22 under the same expansion. Attribute the recovered cases to the set handling in the readout. [Equivalence-class replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/relay-validity-contrasts/tie-readout.json)

### Compare actual source records under matched output ceilings

Use the same chair task and source access for pooled solo, independent role drafts and an interactive team. The team arms share byte-identical openings. Only the interactive revision receives a peer opening. Five stages provide an 896-token output ceiling per arm, with actual costs logged. The Gaza and Jerusalem records use two source event families outside the ranked workshop trials. [Frozen cases, endpoints and arm definitions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source-record-pilot.json)

Interactive versus independent final scores are 42 versus 49 in Gaza and 56 versus 56 in Jerusalem. The interactive records fail the critical guard in both families. All six records preserve the minority rationale. None of four checked role-position anchors establishes peer-specific final credit. Keep the exact record and source spans for independent review before ranking systems, and check for erroneous promotion of generated drafts into historical documents. [Paired results and process evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#measured-source-record-pilot)

For learning a particular communication action, preserve both team roles' outputs and rerun the chair with one revision replaced by its matched independent revision. Keep the source packet and all other draft text fixed. Score the changed final span and authority guard before crediting the action. This applies the controlled-alternative idea from COMA to a diagnostic intervention on text. COMA's training estimator would additionally require an action distribution and counterfactual values. VOUCH provides a separate example of verifying a predicted effect by running the intervention, with tests designed to catch a model that only confirms its own earlier claim. [Counterfactual Multi-Agent Policy Gradients](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/view/11794) · [VOUCH](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-aacl.222/)

The compact prompt control removes all eight revision truncations and the final record's citation of a generated draft ID. Both Gaza team arms score 49/70 with a clear guard. The interactive record preserves aid access but loses the UK's complement qualification, while the independent record does the reverse. Both Jerusalem arms fail the critical guard, despite a higher raw score for the interactive record. Report provenance, scope and final word count alongside any gain in supported conditions. [Compact control and exact annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#short-source-cited-revisions)

The Jerusalem chair scores 42/70 with both independent revisions, 42 with either single peer revision and 56 with both peer revisions. All four fail the factual guard. The three records containing a peer revision also exceed the word cap. This yields a 14-point raw interaction effect and zero change in release eligibility. Score the raw criteria and the release failures separately before using either as a shared reward. [One-revision swaps and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#swap-one-jerusalem-revision-at-a-time)

Frozen Qwen judges ten paired claims in both orders. A judge that can accept both or neither gets nine stable correct pairs. Checking each claim separately gets eight. The pairwise judge defers the actual Jerusalem error about which action the UK opposed. The separate checks endorse that false claim in both orders. Add this exact contrast to reward calibration. Reward factual support separately from completeness, and keep disagreements in the record. [Guard calibration](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#check-a-reward-judge-against-supported-omissions-and-factual-reversals)

On the source-frozen Sudan event, the interactive record gains one supported criterion, preserving the draft's proposed humanitarian access. It scores 49/70 against 42/70 for independent drafts, with a clear guard in both. The pooled solo scores 42/70 and misstates China's reservation. The two team chairs get identical China revisions, sources and synthesis instructions. Neither Russian revision mentions humanitarian access. This is a one-revision effect on which public fact the chair selects rather than evidence transfer of the fact from a peer. [Sudan pilot and exact evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#transfer-the-source-record-protocol-to-sudan)

### Learn source routing and score what the chair actually uses

Fit two agents to send exact source blocks under one slot each, using a verified shared reward on 192 synthetic cases. The learned policy improves on the immediate-coverage heuristic across 16 Sudan source masks and matches the simpler new-block script. When the chair already has both delegations' positions, the learned, new-block and joint-planner routes all send both qualifications and raise supported proof availability from 14 to 21 points. Keep that measure distinct from final prose scores. [Learned routing and scripted controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#fit-agents-to-route-verifiable-source-blocks)

Freeze the chair and vary which qualification blocks arrive. All four original memos score 56/70 and omit the supported consultation overlap. Asking for it first makes all four claim unsupported common ground. A bounded compiler adds the authored comparison only when both exact blocks are present. It produces a 149-word full record with 63 carried-forward and validated points. Check the factual guard, qualification coverage, and effect of withholding each source separately. Do not train the policy to maximize mentions of common ground. [Routed memos and compiled control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#require-evidence-from-both-sides-before-stating-common-ground)

### Measure whether an availability signal earns its cost

Give each delegate a private inventory and one optional truthful availability signal before its evidence slot. Score the resulting source proofs and charge for both message types. Across 256 private-inventory variants of the exposed Sudan docket, the exact decentralized planner finds a gain from signaling in four of 16 public views. Signaling whenever a qualification is available lowers mean return. Both the direct team-reward fit and the fit with a truthful-announcement warm-up match the best silent policy and send zero signals. In the cleanest contrast, the chair already has both positions and needs both qualifications to establish common ground. Signaling lets the delegates send the qualifications only when both are available, preserving proof coverage and reducing delivery cost. [Results, exact control and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#measure-whether-an-availability-signal-earns-its-cost)

### Separate proof coverage from delivery savings

Reward-only joint policy search finds a selective availability protocol. On the equal-weight Sudan views it beats optimal silence by reducing delivery costs while preserving the proofs. To test a change in verifiable content, weight the consultation comparison at seven and each disposition explanation at four. Both learned searches prove the comparison in three of 16 private inventories, up from one for the best silent policy. Each position falls from eight supported inventories to seven. Weighted coverage rises from 4.4375 to 4.8125, the exact ceiling with signals. Keep the per-claim changes visible and use a separately frozen source-faithfulness score for the final record. [Reward-only search, optimal controls and ablation](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#learn-paid-signaling-by-searching-joint-policies)

### Replay the learned route through a frozen final-record writer

Run a shared chair on every source packet induced by the learned and optimal scripted routes. The comparison-weighted fit beats silence on verifiable evidence, but authored references tie at 56.44/70 under the equal-weight benchmark. Frozen Qwen scores 45.94/70 with learned routing and 47.25/70 with optimal silence. It accurately reports both qualifications when both arrive and never explicitly draws their supported overlap. Each route yields three confirmed guard failures across 16 matched inventories. The nine unique model records expose different problems with the wording, source attribution and output cap. Record weighted proof availability, what the chair actually says, and the language-level guard separately. [Run, exact quote annotations and authored control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#score-the-records-produced-by-learned-and-scripted-routes)

### Give coordination room to improve the equal-weight rubric

The Venezuela dual-draft case gives two fictional dispatch desks potentially overlapping copies of the same evidence. The chair can see summaries of the draft texts and vote tallies. Each desk has one source slot. A truthful availability bit can steer the desks toward different evidence. Both optional cards support equally weighted final-record criteria. The learned routing policy delivers both cards in seven of 16 inventory pairs, against five for optimal silence, and raises the authored reference mean from 64.75 to 65.625/70. Check the receipt-aware 70-point record alongside the net-cost routing reward. [Fixed rubric, exact controls and rollout evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#learn-to-avoid-duplicate-evidence-on-the-venezuela-dual-draft-record)

The frozen chair scores 42.4375/70 with the learned route versus 41.5625 for optimal silence and for always announcing availability. Confirmed material errors occur in eight of 16 cases for the learned route, ten for silence and nine for always announcing. The chair earns no full credit on either private-card criterion. The higher overall score follows from the full-packet response handling the public Russian draft outcome better. Retain the per-card uptake measure so a routing success cannot conceal that writing failure. [Final records, exact review and paired scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#pass-the-four-received-packets-through-one-frozen-chair)

### Measure claim uptake independently of citation and delivery

For each required fact, compare the delivered source IDs, the exact claim in the final record, and the sources the writer cites for that claim. Tally supported claims, missing required claims, and unsupported claims separately. A reviewer can confirm that a source quote occurs in the received packet before deciding whether it supports the full claim. Handle linked facts, such as who announced the vote and in what capacity, as an explicit checklist. Withhold full credit when the record preserves only a subset of the required facts. This applies the distinction between citation correctness and completeness in ALCE and the support test in AIS to the chair’s bounded evidence view. [ALCE](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.398/) · [AIS](https://aclanthology.org/2023.cl-4.2/)

For a fixed writer, enumerate the best routing policy separately for final-record points, points on records with a clear guard, and realized national-position criteria. Keep these retrospective diagnostic ceilings apart from the routing policy’s training reward. In the original Venezuela run, the optional channel can reach 42.44 raw record points but no policy can realize either complete national-position criterion from the four archived outputs. Counting points only on records with a clear guard would favor withholding some single-card evidence because the writer inserts new errors in those prompt conditions. Inspect the affected claims and guard outcomes before changing the team reward. [Endpoint ceilings for the original writer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/duplicate-card-routes/initial-endpoint-ceilings.json)

A field-specific prompt recovers both draft dispositions in all four Venezuela packets. It recovers the complete Equatorial Guinea account in isolation and the complete Indonesia account whenever that card arrives. The packet with both cards still omits the President’s capacity. The learned route replaces two Equatorial-Guinea-only receipts with both cards, trading two realized Equatorial Guinea criteria for two Indonesian criteria. It scores 49.44/70 versus 51.19 for optimized silent evidence routing. No routing policy can make any of these four model texts realize both national criteria. Keep each national account as a separate comparison and require that adding an independent, useful source preserve previously supported facts. [Matched prompt test and exact endpoint ceilings](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#rerun-the-chair-with-explicit-evidence-specific-fields)

### Preserve each verified national account through composition

Generate national accounts separately and include only the accounts supported by received evidence. In Venezuela, the modular Qwen writer preserves both complete national criteria in seven of 16 learned-route records, compared with five under optimal silence and six under always announcing. The fully generated public paragraph omits draft symbols and invents numeric citation IDs, so its overall score remains low. Joining the two genuine model national accounts to the archived authored public reference raises the learned-route mean to 65.63/70 against 64.75 for silence. Report the authored and generated units separately and retain actual input and output costs beside the common output ceiling. [Composed records, evidence-linked scoring and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#generate-each-national-account-separately-and-preserve-it-in-the-record)

### Transfer the frozen routing policy to a new Council docket

Freeze the DPRK Panel of Experts renewal case at S/2024/255. It tests a Russian veto, a Chinese abstention, the difference between ending a Panel mandate and retaining the Committee and sanctions, and Japan’s national statement by the Council President. The source join matches the UNBench draft and all 15 votes in UNSC-Bench. Retain the two-card signaling rule, learned policy, routing costs and modular writer ceilings. Test both the original balanced inventory distribution and a new asymmetric inventory distribution. Report the balanced replay as a control for the slot mapping. The asymmetric replay changes card availability across the desks. [Frozen case and rubric](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source-record-dprk-routing.json) · [Benchmark join](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/duplicate-card-routes/dprk-sources.json) · [Official meeting record](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/4042816?ln=en)

The frozen router raises joint delivery from 31.25% to 43.75% under uniform availability and from 65% to 69% under the asymmetric availability model. Its end-to-end score ties optimal silence under both priors. The modular writer preserves Japan's Council President speaking in his national capacity. It drops China's procedural qualification about time remaining for consultations, so the strict hybrid withholds that incomplete account. Report claims supplied, claims preserved, and the best attainable endpoint with the observed writer separately. [Verified transfer, exact controls and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#transfer-the-frozen-router-and-modular-writer-to-the-dprk-panel-renewal-docket)

### Check the direction of a qualification and the effect of source order

The DPRK China checklist reverses the consultation-time qualification on the original card. With the explanation placed first, the same checklist preserves all six facts. With that clause withheld, the writer retains the other five facts and leaves the qualification unstated. Score the unchanged-source arm as primary. The reordered-source record clears the same-case review and restores the expected routing gain on the hybrid memo. Its contrast with the original ordering is an evaluation fixture for evidence order and factual polarity. [Matched responses, exact spans and final-record replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#test-checklist-instructions-and-evidence-order-on-the-incomplete-china-account)

### Optimize communication for the released record

The archive sometimes gives the empty receipt a better record than either single-card receipt. On the original Venezuela writer, an outcome-trained policy still sends rejected single-card receipts in 25% of uniform simulations. A coordinated fit on a synthetic reward and a selector that rules out rejected receipts produce 36.6625 net points under uniform inventories and 37.126 under skew, beating 35 for exact silence. The same policy matches the exact constrained ceiling under both priors. A stale DPRK calibration loses the gain after the writer changes. Freeze writer calibration with a routing benchmark and report validity and score separately from evidence coverage. [Policy fits, rejected attempts and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#fit-communication-for-the-verified-record-rather-than-card-count)

### Check receipt integrity under message loss and unfamiliar partners

The zero-rejection Venezuela protocol rejects 3.85% of records when each signal and card is independently lost with probability 10%. Replacing one desk with the older proof router causes 75% rejection even under reliable delivery. A buffer that passes both distinct cards together, or uses the validated public-only packet, eliminates those rejections in the exact replay. Under uniform inventories and 10% losses, the buffered proof router and matched fit score 39.42 versus 38.24 for optimal silent buffering. At 25% loss with asymmetric inventories, the fitted pair falls below that silent control. Training with a wider partner population does not improve the held-out partner mean. Treat receipt validity and communication gain as separate endpoints and report both seat swaps. [Exact fault, buffer and cross-play results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#stress-test-the-learned-protocol-under-message-loss-and-unfamiliar-partners)

### Verify that a language-model desk executes the learned policy

Qwen3.5-4B follows the fitted policy in 15/16 announcement views and 25/48 dispatch views after impossible actions are masked. Replaying both model-driven stages puts both dockets below optimal buffered silence. Supplying typed dispatch restores the DPRK gain and still leaves Venezuela below silence because one legal announcement is wrong. A confidence fallback to the fitted signal restores the typed result on this exposed pair and delegates only three of eight optional announcements to the model. Test availability, observed messages, and the intended protocol separately from the format of the chosen action. [Raw decisions, exact replay and interface controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#execute-the-learned-routing-protocol-through-language-model-decisions)

### Separate team-reward learning from the language model’s contribution

Optimize local signal and dispatch logits with an exact joint reward for delivering a complete pair. The full learned correction recovers the fitted controller on the DPRK logits held out from optimization. At uniform availability and 10% loss it scores 60.42 versus 57.88 for frozen Qwen and 59.24 for exact silent delivery. A table trained from zero nearly matches the result. Removing the Qwen logits after the fit leaves the greedy score unchanged. The benchmark needs both controls because a full state-indexed head can learn the entire routing rule. [Team-reward fit, held-out logits and independent rollout audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#optimize-local-action-scores-with-the-joint-record-reward)

Reducing the correction to sixteen shared biases preserves a uniform-inventory gain at 10% loss, but its greedy scores again survive removal of the model logits. The zero-logit fit is slightly better in that condition. The language model contributes to the starting scores and stochastic probabilities. These controls attribute the demonstrated greedy coordination to the public bit, legality masks and learned biases. Reusing the two-slot schema on DPRK tests held-out logits without testing a new action interface. [Shared-bias fit and ablations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#restrict-the-correction-to-16-shared-biases)

### Require the model to ground a routing role in the passage

Remove the preassigned card role from the desk’s prompt. Give the reader a historical excerpt and two exact evidence requirements drawn from the frozen final-record rubric. Include complete excerpts, sentence reorderings, near misses that retain the same speakers and votes, and explicit synthetic reversals. Rotate the three answer letters so every semantic result occurs in every menu position. Compare against assigning roles by position and by speaker mention. In the routing replay, trust an attestation only when the selected passage supports its declared role. Charge a desk for attempted transmissions even when the attestation rejects them. [Source transformations and prompt fixture](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/duplicate_card_semantic.py)

In a separate exact sensitivity check, apply independent completeness-reading errors to both desks and optimize the silent control for that same reader. At 10% link loss and symmetric 20% reading error the trained router still gains 0.745 points under uniform inventories, while 26.85% of episodes attempt at least one unsupported delivery. Under skew the gain shrinks to 0.105 points. The receipt gate discards those attempts in the score. These are simulated error rates and exclude swaps between claim types. They do not estimate observed language-model performance. [Independent-error sensitivity and controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/duplicate-card-routes/semantic-sensitivity.json)

### Calibrate communication against silence under reader and inventory shift

Fitting exact deterministic routing tables on varied reader errors reproduces the existing behavior. Including shifted inventory and delivery distributions still leaves the best fixed table 0.03969 points below scenario-specific silence in its worst training condition. An independent paired pilot can reject deployment when it cannot establish a gain. In the synthetic replay, 4,096 paired draws per condition enable communication in 2 of 20 conditions. At 262,144 they enable it in 12, with no negative-gain selections at either size. Distinguish the calibration budget and regime assumptions from language-model performance. [Fits, paired outcome audit and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#test-whether-a-single-policy-transfers-across-reader-and-inventory-shifts)

### Measure stable source-reading errors and verify known receipts

All 48 Qwen probes completed. Agreement across answer orders gets 8/8 Venezuela and 6/8 DPRK passages. The two DPRK errors are stable across orders. The model accepts an incomplete China account and misses a complete reordered Japan account. With the pooled reader, learned routing can improve net score while increasing unsupported attempts, and it loses to reader-matched silence on the reordered DPRK fixture. For known source bundles, replace the assumed receipt oracle with an executable hash registry that binds each approved passage and alias to its docket and role. This restores the typed routing gain for the reviewed set. A strict bytes-only registry discards the reordered material. Keep performance on unregistered sources separate. [Observed classifications, executable attestation and exact rollout](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/README.md#ground-local-card-roles-in-source-passages)

### Turn a stable reading error into a clause-level control

Test the two clauses implicated by the DPRK reading failures on all eight archived anonymous passages. Ask every passage both questions: whether Yamazaki spoke as Japan’s national representative and whether China cited remaining consultation time when explaining its abstention. Swap yes and no across answer letters. Grant a routing role only when both orders support its clause and both reject the other. Compare with the full-requirement prompt on the same passages and report both missed valid cards and admitted near misses. Replay learned routing and optimize silence separately for each reader, using the same inventory distributions, loss rates and archived final-record scores. The fixture varies only the two selected clauses. A broader reader check must vary the veto consequence, Russia’s views, Panel independence and sanctions review terms separately before crediting full account verification. [Probe](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/duplicate_card_atomic.py) · [Replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/duplicate_card_atomic_review.py)

FActScore supplies a precedent for verifying claims individually against sources. CheckEval supplies a precedent for expressing judge criteria as binary questions. For diplomatic records, check both directions. Verify each candidate assertion against the available evidence, and check each required rubric part for omission. Keep an explicit uncertain verdict for ambiguous attribution and retain the benchmark weights for decision, reservations and authority. [FActScore](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.741/) · [CheckEval](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.796/)

The two-clause check fixed both known errors and missed both complete China passages. Exact passage accuracy stayed at 6/8. Unsupported acceptances went from one to zero, and missed complete passages rose from one to two. At 10% loss the fitted route scored 55.80 versus 56.00 for reader-matched silence on uniform inventories, because the check never authorized a China card. Audit recall alongside precision and report the effect on valid joint delivery. [Raw probes and exact scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/duplicate-card-routes/atomic-dprk/score.json)

A three-way question recovered the reordered China account and correctly separated an explicit reversal from an omitted reason. It rejected both synthetic scope controls in all answer orders. With the earlier Japan check, strict agreement reaches 7/8 on the original passages. It withholds the complete China passage in its original sentence order because one of three answers disagrees. At 10% loss and uniform inventories, this restores a 1.179-point gain over reader-matched silence on the reordered fixture, with zero unsupported attempts. It still loses 0.200 points on the original-order fixture. A two-of-three rule chosen after seeing these outcomes reaches 8/8 and matches the ideal reader in this replay. Freeze that rule before a new family and vary the other required details before claiming reader transfer. [Scores and order-level decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/duplicate-card-routes/qualifier-dprk/score.json)

### Compare final records on a new Western Sahara docket

Use Security Council meeting S/PV.9771 to test whether interaction helps preserve a minority reservation and a split amendment vote. Join the three UNBench draft entries to UNSC-Bench's adopted row and the primary meeting. The latter counts two abstentions and one non-participation. UNSC-Bench encodes the non-participation as a third abstention even though its note records the distinction. Flag this row for native vote evaluation. Keep both failed amendment votes and the final adoption distinct, and require the final record to separate proposed rights monitoring from what the renewal authorized. [Source and release audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source-record-minurso-sources.json) · [UN meeting record](https://documents.un.org/api/symbol/access?l=en&s=S%2FPV.9771&t=pdf)

Freeze five 14-point final criteria before generation. Check the scoring contract with an authored reference and four foils. The foils change Algeria's vote classification, an amendment's disposition, a Swiss national statement's speaker capacity, and the scope of adopted authority. Each should fail the guard while preserving the unrelated rubric scores. Give the pooled analyst, independent delegates and interacting delegates the same five generation slots and 620-token ceiling. Give every final writer the same full source dossier. Share openings and vary peer delivery between the two team arms. Record all capped turns and give the final judge opaque IDs and source blocks without the conversation or condition. [Frozen dossier](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source-record-minurso.json) · [Judge anchors](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source-record-minurso-anchors.json)

The three-arm frozen Qwen pilot produced no completed final record. All 13 calls hit their caps. The interactive text preserved the Swiss split and rationale. The independent text preserved the adopted mandate's limits. Neither did both. The initial Swiss role conflated an amendment with the final draft and corrected it in both revision conditions. This calls for a completion control before attributing a score difference to deliberation. The control freezes the original drafts, dossier and chair cap and changes only the final-writing instruction. [Provisional scores and peer checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso/score.json)

The completion control finished all three records in 87–97 words with the same 250-token chair cap. All three scored 0/70 under the original compound rubric despite preserving many facts. All omitted the draft identifier, the named penholder, the Swiss first amendment vote and the call to facilitate OHCHR visits. The pooled record also introduced a false Algerian abstention. This gives a concrete reason to freeze weighted, individually scored rubric parts before a future case. Keep the 70-point final endpoint, full-contract pass and material guard visible together. [Completion-control evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso-compact/review.json)

### Score required facts separately and calibrate the judge

The first rubric refinement keeps five 14-point groups and scores 16 individual parts. It retains the material guard and requires all parts, a completed output and the length contract for a strict pass. In a retrospective exact-span review, the three completed Western Sahara records score 39/70 for pooled solo, 35/70 for independent drafts and 35/70 for interactive drafts. The pooled record still fails the guard for the invented abstention. The compound rubric previously gave all three zero. This is a different scoring resolution applied to the same outputs by the same reviewer. There is no new quality or coordination result from this rescoring. [Rubric](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/minurso-atomic-rubric.json) · [Exact-span annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso/atomic-annotations.json) · [Replayed points and guard](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso/atomic-score.json)

A judge check must separate complete support, omission and a conflicting statement. An error elsewhere in a record should leave correctly reported facts unchanged. Test this with a minimal edit and an adjacent unchanged requirement. For non-participation, remove both direct statements from the omission control and use the unchanged tally as the adjacent check. Rotate all answer meanings through all labels and report accuracy by status, including unstable answers. [Judge control generator](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/minurso_atomic_controls.py)

In the first local judge run, frozen Qwen3.5-4B agreed correctly on 30/35 record–part pairs, the same as always voting supported. It never consistently caught a contradiction and accepted four of five in both orders. The support option stayed at B, and one intended omission remained in a later sentence. Preserve the initial run and its adjudicated label. The corrected panel uses balanced answer-letter rotation and one genuine omission with a matched unchanged tally. [First-run scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso-atomic-judge/score.json)

Full label rotation preserves the failure. The frozen judge agrees correctly on all 12 supported pairs and on zero of five contradictions. It mistakenly accepts the omitted non-participation clause and four contradictions in every order. The authority foil switches verdict once. This rules out a fixed-letter shortcut as a sufficient explanation for the false acceptances in this panel. An evaluator for reward must verify what the candidate actually said before crediting the source-compatible fact. Keep the measured part-level coverage and its quote validation. Evaluate the revised judge on contradictions, omissions and unchanged nearby facts before using it to train or rank policies. [Rotated control scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso-atomic-controls/score.json)

### Extract the candidate's claim before judging it

Run the same rotated Western Sahara controls with a candidate-only extractor. Give it a neutral field and a menu of possible values, including an unstated value. Withhold the source and rubric answer during extraction. Compare unanimous extracted values to the frozen key afterward. Frozen Qwen3.5-4B verifies 11/12 supported fields and identifies 2/5 contradictions. It withholds a verdict on three other contradictions, one supported capacity statement and the omitted participation status. The joint source-and-answer judge had accepted the omission and four contradictions consistently. The new comparison uses case-specific value menus, with no quoted-span or open-text extraction guarantee. [Candidate-only score](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso-candidate-fields/score.json)

In a one-point reward audit over the six matched edits, award the point only for unanimously supported content. The joint judge rewards the faithful version more on one edit and still rewards the flawed version on five. Candidate-only extraction rewards the faithful version more on five and awards no point to any flawed version. The remaining pair ties because the correct speaker capacity is unresolved. Both methods preserve all six adjacent unchanged checks. This reuses the same predictions and evaluates a field reward rather than a complete record or a trained policy. [Paired reward audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-minurso-candidate-fields/reward-pairs.json)

With the same extraction instructions on the archived Mali and DPRK dockets, the reader gets seven of nine record and field pairs right across all answer orders. It detects all three edited errors, while withholding two judgments. The Mali omission ties in one order. The actual DPRK record says “Council President Yamazaki, speaking as Japan's representative” and the model picks the Council role in two of three orders. Majority voting would therefore misclassify the valid national statement. Evaluate statement-specific capacity with exact supporting spans before letting this verifier award or deny a full diplomatic record. [Transfer fixture and score](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/institutional-candidate-fields/score.json)

### Test whether the exact span binds a statement to the right role

Separate the office label from the capacity of a reported statement. On the DPRK candidate record, include both the exact words “Council President Yamazaki” and the exact phrase that qualifies the statement in the same choice set. Rotate every answer through every letter, and abstain when the selected span changes across rotations. Add two synthetic controls in which the national role language appears but qualifies another speaker or another agenda item. A mechanical span matcher and the model see the same proposed substrings. Verify offsets before use. An exact match can still fail attribution. [Span selector](research/unsc-marl-20261119/judge/dprk_capacity_spans.py)

Replay the learned two-desk router with those source reads and the archived China qualifier reads. Reoptimize silence for each reader. Report both gains and unsupported transmission attempts, since the replay can reject mis-scoped claims at receipt using the authored key. Treat China majority voting as a separate sensitivity analysis chosen after the original errors. [Routing replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/dprk_capacity_route_review.py)

The exact-span selector reads both complete DPRK source orders and withholds the two scope decoys. It selects the right span on two of three presentations of the actual Yamazaki memo and withholds the final judgment. The phrase matcher alone tries an unsupported delivery in 55% of episodes on either scope fixture at uniform availability and 10% loss. With the model selector, unsupported attempts fall to zero and the learned router scores 60.422 vs. 59.244 for same-reader silence. The replay uses a reference receipt gate and unchanged chair scores. [Span selections and route outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/dprk-capacity-spans/route-score.json)

A policy fitted against both standard reader regimes avoids all message costs when the reader cannot verify China. Its gain over silence is 0.000 on original-order sources and 0.465 on reordered sources at the uniform 10% loss settings. The previous router gains −0.200 and 1.179 respectively. Under skewed inventories and 25% loss, the refit loses 0.816 points to silence. The scope decoys share the reordered observation map, so their replay does not create a distinct policy generalization test. Report reader accuracy, communication benefit, transport sensitivity and final-record quality separately. [Exact joint fit and shifted controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/dprk-capacity-spans/router-fit.json)

### Measure new Western Sahara records from actual receipts

Give one frozen chair each of the four source packets formed by the public dossier and the two national source cards. The chair receives no draft revisions, inventory labels or undelivered statements. Use the same five headings, output ceiling and receipt-aware rubric for every packet. The public evidence has 42 possible points and each national card licenses 14 more. Score quote-backed claims, completion, citation use and critical errors separately. For routing, report raw points, a guarded score that zeros records with a failed or uncertain guard, and the authored availability ceiling. Compare frozen policies transferred from other routing studies with exact optimal silence at the same inventory priors, communication costs, link loss and writer.
