[Packet writer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/minurso_receipt_writer.py) · [Scoring and transport replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/minurso_receipt_scores.py)

The four fresh chair records score 27, 33, 41 and 55/70 in receipt order. The public-only record gives a wrong account of adopted OHCHR access language, so the guarded vector is 0, 33, 41, 55. The transferred learned router beats optimal silence by 1.558 guarded points at 10% loss and uniform inventories. With raw factual points it gains 1.695, so the comparison survives removing that hard penalty. A new team-reward fit from these scores reaches its exact training ceiling and ties the transferred policy across six evaluation conditions. Both lose 0.144 guarded points to silence under skew and 25% loss. The evidence and writer are fixed in each matched policy replay. [Fresh records and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-receipt-writer/score.json) · [Fitted table](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-receipt-writer/learned.json)

With receipt inputs and decoding frozen, a more explicit slot prompt recovered the main draft identifier on all four records. It dropped the amendment outcomes on all four. The both-card record fell from 55 to 33/70, and it was the only record that met the guard and receipt-citation release rule. At uniform inventories and 10% loss, guarded routing gain rose from 1.558 to 2.814 while guarded absolute net fell from 39.188 to 10.867. The raw gain reversed from +1.695 to −0.788. Keep both raw and guarded paired effects and the absolute endpoint when evaluating the learned route. A larger guarded gain can come from the comparator losing more release-eligible records. [Matched prompt control and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-receipt-fields/score.json)

### Test a natural-language exchange with a chair that has no private sources

Give two delegates the same public dossier and separate national statements. Share their initial drafts between conditions. Each drafts a brief account and asks a question about the other delegation. In one condition, both revise with access to the other's opening, while in the matched condition each sees only its own opening. Each chair receives the same public dossier and the two resulting revisions. It cannot access the original private cards. Both conditions have five calls and a 782-token output ceiling. Report actual input tokens separately because reading a peer draft adds context. Score both historical accuracy and claims traceable to a revision the chair actually saw. Use exact peer, revision and final spans to identify any added value from exchange. [Protocol and visibility replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/minurso_deliberation.py) · [Quote and mechanism scoring](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/minurso_deliberation_review.py)

In the first natural-language comparison the independent record scores 35/70 and the peer-visible record 24/70. Both fail the critical guard. Each correctly reports Algeria's non-participation under adoption and repeats a conflicting abstention from the delegate elsewhere. One opening addresses South Africa, which was absent from the exchange, and the other asks for a comparison the peer's evidence does not cover. The two sampled contacts provide no supported peer-specific contribution. The peer chair also stops at its token cap. [Final points, provenance and dialogue counts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-deliberation/score.json)

The named-counterpart follow-up corrects the question addresses and narrows them to the meeting. Switzerland fixes the amendment identifiers in both arms. The official non-participation still becomes an abstention in both national drafts and both chairs repeat it. The independent record scores 37/70 and the peer record 33/70, with an upper bound of 43 if ambiguous national claims are credited. Both fail the guard and the peer record hits its cap. The working typed-receipt route and the failed natural-language exchange exercise different interfaces. Public procedural facts need a reconciliation step before these draft messages can supply a reliable payoff for learning. [Matched dialogue and score bounds](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-deliberation-named/score.json)

### Reconcile draft claims with the public roll call before synthesis

Keep each of the four archived chair requests fixed and withhold only the sentence that says Algeria abstained on the final draft. Match the check to the country, vote and instrument. It leaves other national votes, amendment votes, questions and different meetings untouched in the controls. Do not substitute the official outcome into the draft. The chair already has the public docket, and it still has no private source access. This tests the effect of releasing cleaner draft text and exposes the collateral cost of withholding an entire sentence. Score the results against both the official sources and the text actually delivered. [Matched reconciliation experiment](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/minurso_vote_reconciliation.py) · [Final-record review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/minurso_vote_reconciliation_review.py)

The four matched chair reruns remove the erroneous draft sentence before generation. The error disappears in one record and is regenerated in the other three, even though the chair has the correct roll call in every prompt. The repaired record still reports the wrong Swiss first amendment vote, so all four fail the material guard. Regeneration after removing one bad sentence cannot enforce consistency with the official result. [Matched reruns and exact exclusions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-vote-reconciliation/score.json)

A constrained rendering control copies the public sections from the authored primary-source reference and uses exact matching only for a small set of in-scope national claims in the delivered drafts. It gives 54/70 and 48/70 for the original independent and peer conditions, and 56/70 in both named conditions. The public section supplies 42 points by construction. Neither named draft licenses a sufficiently clear Algerian explanation, so those slots remain empty. The narrow phrase rules and source-bound rendering are development controls, with no new author generated at this stage. [Protected records and independent draft spans](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-vote-reconciliation/protected-records.json)

The quote-copy recovery asks the source owner for its two missing national facts. Both model replies paraphrase and omit the explicit link between the complaint and non-participation, so neither enters the record. Letting the source owner select instead of transcribe recovers both complete spans in every answer order. With the authorized public section and witnessed Swiss claims, both named records reach 70/70 and pass the fixture contract at 100 words. The author-defined public wording and closed span choices supply part of that result. Identical records in both arms give no verified peer effect. [Free-text recovery](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-requery/score.json) · [Span selection and compiled records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/minurso-requery-select/score.json)

### Learn which missing field to request, then transfer across dockets

Give both desks private inventories drawn from three historical evidence fields. Either desk may hold any field, including duplicates. The requester can ask for one missing field and submit one available field without seeing the other desk's inventory. The responder can send one field after seeing the request or an erasure. Pay for both messages and score the verified exact-source addendum, including any bonus that needs two complementary fields. Fit two decentralized table policies by differentiating their single expected team reward on Western Sahara and DPRK. Freeze both tables before testing Mali's new field values and simulated inventory distributions. [Case packets](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source_request_cases.py) · [Training and exact controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/source_request_policy.py)

On typed Mali inputs with uniform inventories and 10% loss, the learned policy scores 14.149 net, compared with 13.669 for optimal independent submissions, 13.720 for a highest-priority request script and 14.529 for the exact optional-query ceiling. Erasing the request gives 13.249 while charging the same attempted-query fee. Under asymmetric inventories, it loses 0.571 to independent submissions. All three tests score a source addendum with a two-delivery limit. The learner uses actual gradient updates on small tabular policies. The desks see verified field identities and the chair addendum is assembled from exact source-backed text. [Held-out typed conditions and training trace](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-request-policy.json)

The direct prose reader admits only one complete original and two complete reordered fields, missing Russia's two-part policy in both orders. The routing advantage disappears under all three priors and both source variants. The learned router continues to ask and loses exactly the 0.05 request fee to same-reader silence. The gate rejects unsupported delivery using the reference proof key. [Role predictions and matched scoring](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-source-request-reader/score.json)

Selecting an exact horizon span recovers Russia's complete field in both orders. The repaired decoder restores the typed advantage on reordered Mali text at uniform inventories and 10% loss. It still withholds the original-order co-penholder field and loses 0.248 points there. In a worked reordered episode, asking for the Russia field changes a duplicate reply into new evidence and raises the addendum from 12 to 25 when links hold. Treat this as an adaptively authored scope check, with the role framing and exact phrase candidates supplied. It demonstrates a specific learned communication effect on verified text while leaving robust general reading and final-record quality open. [Term evidence and source-backed causal witness](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-request-term/score.json)

### Test whether learned requests improve a full Mali record

Generate a fresh final chair record for each of the seven possible receipts under the two-delivery protocol, plus a full-information solo control. Every chair receives the same public vote and draft blocks, and only the attested optional source blocks for its receipt. The rubric is the existing ten-part Mali historical-record contract out of 70. Score explicit source-backed claims, completion and critical errors. Track France's objection about lack of discussion separately because the routing benchmark rewards that field while the existing final rubric does not name it. Hold the trained policies fixed. Reoptimize independence for every reader using the newly measured writer payoffs, and preserve the request-erasure control. [Packet writer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/mali_request_writer.py) · [Whole-record score and exact controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/mali_request_writer_review.py)

The initial Mali packet left out the verified meeting date even though the chair prompt requested it. Two records invented distinct dates, one used a placeholder, and the other five omitted the date. Numeric or rewritten citation IDs also caused release failures. Preserve this entire run as a packet-construction error diagnostic. The matched follow-up supplies the date and meeting ID from the source-backed task to all eight receipts, with no change to the writer prompt, models, evidence cards or tokenizer limits. [Initial records and explicit scoring](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-request-writer/score.json) · [Matched date correction](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/mali_request_writer_dated.py)

With the date supplied, all eight new records state it and finish. Under uniform inventories and 10% loss, the transferred learned request scores 39.735 against 46.683 for optimized independence on raw final-record points minus costs. Exhaustive enumeration gives the same 46.683 as the optional-request ceiling. Only the full-information record passes the release gate, and the two-delivery channel cannot reach its receipt. The learned request that improved a source addendum from 12 to 25 yields a weaker complete memo, going from 42 to 35. Across the nine intact-link states where the question changes receipts, two final scores improve and five decline. Relaxing ambiguous guard judgments leaves learned requests below independence in the reordered test. [Dated full records and bounds](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-request-writer-dated/score.json) · [Sensitivity and per-world changes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-request-writer-dated/sensitivity.json)

### Protect official outcomes and test whether delivered rationale survives

In the Mali development docket, fix the official date, drafts, votes, dispositions, and limits of Council authority in a verified public section. A frozen Qwen3.5-4B writes one sentence per national source, with no access to another delegate's source. Compose each possible receipt from those three sentences, holding the underlying generations fixed across delivery policies. The public template supplies 56 of the 70 rubric points by construction. The two national rationales supply up to seven points each. France's objection about the separate proposal remains a separate retention measure. [Raw generations and annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-protected-writer/)

All three generated sentences convey the substance of their blocks. Strict model-written citation checking drops the Russia sentence because it ends in `[1]`. Moving the citation into the trusted single-source delivery envelope retains the same sentence with the correct source ID. For uniform inventories, 10% loss, and either typed or verified reordered inputs, the transferred request policy then scores 63.809 against 63.732 for optimized independent delivery and 64.039 for the exact optional-query ceiling, with message costs included. The learned request raises a matched two-delegate record from 63 to 70. Across the distribution, complete records rise from 25.31% to 29.62%. The learned policy loses to independence under the other two inventory priors and on original-order prose under all three priors. [Strict citations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-protected-writer/score-strict-citations.json) · [Trusted citations and exact controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-protected-writer/score-trusted-citations.json)

The broader benchmark needs blind grading on unseen source families. Freeze the public record schema and source citation transport before those runs. Compare the same public record across solo, independent drafts plus synthesis, and interactive teams. Keep a full-information solo ceiling and a solo control with comparable evidence acquisition and token budgets. Measure discriminating national claims and objections separately from fixed public content. Treat objection attribution and procedural dispositions as independent rubric targets. This Mali comparison uses three source-local generations, an author-verified public section, and same-assistant unblinded semantic admission. It supports one controlled evidence-routing effect on the final artifact.

The exact uniform policy also reaches the separate ceiling in each of the other typed priors. A 280-update diagnostic refit of the two tabular policies on the **actual national-component points** under the uniform prior earns 64.026, 65.013 and 63.071 net points under the uniform, asymmetric and correlated-duplicate priors. It beats each prior's optimized independent control by 0.294, 0.119 and 0.119, respectively. Erasing the fitted request reduces net scores by 0.319, 0.148 and 0.144 while retaining the request fee. The frozen writer and the annotated record bank are held fixed. This is evidence that a shared final-record reward can train evidence routing within this docket. Both test priors reuse the same source family and payoff function. A transfer experiment needs separate training and evaluation dockets and a source reader that holds up across evidence orders. [Fit, gradient trace, and exact controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/mali-protected-writer/policy-fit.json)

Use the existing 21 December 2022 Myanmar case, linked to UNBench's 15 author-test vote rows. The pinned UNSC-Bench scan found no matching row for this docket. Freeze Mali's fitted policy before generating Myanmar records. Preserve the adoption, roll call, humanitarian provisions, ASEAN role and reporting mandate in a common 42-point public section. In the opportunity inventory, the source-note inventory rises from three to six distinct concepts and the number carried into the final record rises from three to five. The blinded Qwen judge received five distinct texts, including the two authorization anchors and three distinct generated records. None returned a valid complete verdict under the ten-unit format. Surrounding wording can still change a procedural verdict when the candidate span and official ledger are identical. Use trusted per-instrument checks for explicit date and stage claims and validate any model-based judgment on minimal contextual perturbations before using it as a release gate or training reward. ### Transfer the protected router to the 2023 UNIFIL mandate renewal

Hold the Outer Space policy, loss model and message fees fixed. Bind its three field slots to Brazil, China and Russia in that order before generation. The Brazil–China pair tests whether a record can state shared support for the mission and distinguish their assessments of how well the text addressed Lebanon's concerns. Russia supplies a separate abstention rationale. These fields represent simulated copies of public records. They make no claim about private historical knowledge. Keep five seven-point Council facts in the common verified paragraph. Generate the three country notes, seven nonempty team receipts and three solo receipts. All final-body writers have 128 tokens and a 75-word requirement. Solo receives original source summaries for the matched receipts or the full pool. For exact replay, compare the frozen transfer against optimized independent delivery on the observed scoring bank and the exact query ceiling. The transferred request adds China's rationale and the supported point of overlap in the selected receipt, moving the strict material score from 35/70 to 49/70. Both team records fail release. Brazil's source says the text addressed some Lebanese concerns. Its note drops the qualifier, and the two-source writer changes that into a claim that Brazil considered the concerns resolved. The receipt-matched solo earns 49/70 and clears the guard. The full-pool solo also earns 49/70, drops Russia's rationale, and invents a disagreement over the need for enhanced direct military communication. One team receipt exceeds the 75-word cap. Across the simulated inventories, the frozen prose router beats optimized independence by 0.208 raw net points under the uniform prior, while its release-qualified deficit is 21.852. The frozen learned net gain against optimized independence is +1.232 uniform, −0.683 asymmetric and +0.492 with correlated duplicates. The uniform public-rubric exact planner attains the per-prior optional ceiling in all three. A single 280-update MARL fit on the public UNIFIL rubric under uniform inventories beats optimized independence on the verified record bank by 1.322, 0.382 and 0.582 points under the uniform, asymmetric and correlated priors. Neither of the nonuniform priors enters fitting. Erasing requests reduces the three returns by 1.435, 0.541 and 0.997 points. The fit remains within 0.086 points of each query ceiling. A single deterministic planner optimized under the uniform prior matches all three ceilings, certifying a worst-case advantage of 0.430 points for this exact rubric and set of priors. The fit still trails independence on the generated prose release endpoint, so better routing alone does not address lossy national wording. The blind pairwise judge picks the well-attributed record over a role swap and rejects false consensus under both orders. It fails order stability for two equally incomplete records and for a direct reversal of the permission clause. On the actual receipt contrast it correctly prefers the learned-request record's discussion of shared support, but it also prefers that record on the open-issue criterion even though both records omit the required qualifier and the interactive one strengthens Brazil's claim. The pairwise interface remains unsuitable as an automatic reward. [Pairs and condition-blinded decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-pairwise-judge/assessment.json)

### Select and verify UNIFIL evidence before the chair writes

Replace the free-form note with a model-selected source pointer. Each field uses the original two-sentence summary, a shorter authenticated excerpt, an altered scope distractor, and an abstention. Present both code orders. Withhold a required sentence for every field and add a Brazil test where the missing text appears only under a different source. Include the separate Outer Space May vote because paraphrasing had lost its vote-failure reason. The gate checks the selected passage against the named source and the authored proof requirement for that field. The model proposes the Brazil–China relationship only after receiving the admitted passages, and the renderer requires both receipts. Keep the original vote ledger, field weights, learned router, and message fees. [Selection and comparison code](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/unifil_span_transport.py) · [Exact policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/unifil_span_review.py)

The model finds all eight intact passages and both licensed comparisons. It abstains in only four of ten incomplete or misattributed source tests. The other six responses include four incomplete excerpts and two unsupported selections. The exact gate admits none of those six as complete. One of two missing-receipt comparison probes attempts an unsupported bridge, which is withheld. Replaying the accepted proof IDs recovers the full verified UNIFIL bank in both orders. At 10% link loss, the UNIFIL fit again gains 1.322, 0.382 and 0.582 net points over optimized independence. The existing templates supply the final wording. This is model-mediated evidence selection from an authored menu, with deterministic provenance and record assembly. [Selections, guards, and realized receipt values](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-span-transport/assessment.json)

Checking proof completeness before either actor chooses what to send improves the learned policy by 2.218, 2.744 and 1.506 net points when Brazil's qualifier is unavailable, relative to checking only after the route is chosen. The early gate restores a small gain over optimized independence under uniform and correlated-duplicate inventories. The asymmetric case remains 0.372 points behind. These are matched simulations with both desks seeing only their own admitted proof IDs. [Inventory admission replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-span-transport/inventory-gate.json)

An open sentence-ID extraction variant removes the assembled answer options and adds a relevant-looking contextual sentence to each named source. The model recovers 6/8 intact records and abstains in 4/10 incomplete or misattributed cases. Reversing sentence placement makes it select context in place of a required Brazil or Russia sentence. Requiring stable extraction preserves only the China proof in the UNIFIL bank. The final gate does not release a failed field or produce the Brazil–China bridge from a single receipt. This identifies a gap between choosing a preassembled excerpt and selecting relevant evidence directly from source text. [Sentence-ID task](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/unifil_sentence_extraction.py) · [Raw generations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-sentence-extraction/transcript.jsonl)

One 280-update calibration fit uses equal weight on intact and Brazil-missing uniform inventories after admission. It gives neither actor a global missingness flag. The revised policy leaves all three Brazil-missing returns unchanged and reduces intact returns by 0.051, 0.315 and 0.182 points. The exact benchmark still leaves some headroom on the asymmetric priors. This mixture is a training failure, and the earlier fit remains the stronger policy on the tested cases. [Training trajectory and matched returns](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-admission-fit/fit.json)

With the stable sentence-extraction gate, only China remains admissible on UNIFIL. Under early gating, the optional-query ceiling equals optimized independence on every tested prior. Both learned policies deliver the same evidence at the same delivery cost as independence and lose exactly the request fee. Evaluate evidence coverage and remaining information headroom before interpreting a lower reward as a routing failure. The common single-field case is a benchmark result with a known mask. Each actor still observes only its own admitted IDs. [Held-out availability pattern](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-sentence-extraction/china-only-early-gate.json) · [Token replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-sentence-extraction/token-replay.json)

### Retrieve one required fact at a time

Keep the same named sentence rows and the same placement reversal as the two-ID extractor. Ask a separate question for each required fact. For example, ask which sentence states Brazil's continuing support and which sentence gives its view of operating freedom and how many Lebanese concerns were addressed. Each call may select one source ID or abstain. Admit both only when each meets its separately locked predicate. Add a new condition that withholds the first required sentence while preserving the second, alongside the existing second-sentence and cross-owner controls. [Fact-specific prompts and scoring](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/unifil_slot_extraction.py)

The model recovers all eight intact proofs across both placements, up from six with the single two-ID request. It correctly handles seven of eight second-sentence omissions, both misattribution fixtures, and four of eight first-sentence omissions. Five absent-slot responses still select unsupported evidence. In particular, Brazil's or China's actual vote can stand in for an unstated position in the model output. The authored proof gate withholds all 18 incomplete or misattributed bundles. On the intact gate, reusing the licensed relationship output for the identical notes restores the verified policy bank and its prior-specific gains. Each fixture now takes two model calls. Link cost replay still excludes reading compute. [Actual replies, release checks and routing](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/unifil-slot-extraction/assessment.json)

### Learn routing from a public rubric and transfer the policy

Train the two agents in one shared expected-reward optimization using 96 synthetic reward profiles, uniform private inventories, and the existing message and loss model. The policy network receives the canonicalized public receipt score vector and outputs local-action tables. The requester looks up a row using its own inventory. The responder uses its own inventory and the received question, which can be erased. The four historical case rubrics are excluded from the training bank. Provide only their intended public weights at deployment and assess the frozen policy against independent and optional-query exact controls on the archived record scores. [Training and evaluation code](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/rubric_conditioned_routing.py)

The one 320-update fit improves the mean synthetic training objective from 8.207 to 16.335 net points. On the historical banks, it beats exact independent delivery in all four uniform cases and six of twelve case-prior cells overall. It wins on all three Outer Space priors. In the other three cases, each nonuniform comparison trails independence. Erasing the question lowers reward in all twelve comparisons. The retained records use protected fields and previously scored assemblies. The learned router transfers in this setting, with the strongest margins under its uniform training assumption. [Held-out scores, policy tables, and checkpoint](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/rubric-conditioned-routing/report.json)

| Net gain over optimized independence | Uniform | Asymmetric | Correlated duplicates |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| UNIFIL | +1.124 | −0.471 | −0.010 |
| Outer Space | +1.478 | +0.264 | +0.352 |
| Mali | +0.206 | −0.194 | −0.023 |
| Myanmar | +0.646 | −0.418 | −0.227 |

The checkpoint ablation keeps the rubric-derived role order and replaces the network's reward vector with one constant vector. This produces identical greedy tables on all four cases. The gains therefore demonstrate transfer after role canonicalization. The benchmark gives little leverage to test adaptation to numerical weights under uniform inventories. A retrospective exact planner using one shared canonical table falls just 0.043 points short of the four case-specific ceilings on average. Relabeling tied fields changes some asymmetric outcomes despite preserving the meaning of the rubric and inventory distribution. Add cases where the same canonical field order has meaningfully different best actions before crediting reward-conditioned adaptation. [Checkpoint ablation and six field relabelings](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/rubric-conditioned-routing/audit.json) · [Common-policy ceiling](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/rubric-conditioned-routing/fixed-policy-ceiling.json)

### Test numerical reward adaptation with fixed field ordering

Move a 16-point complement bonus between the first pair and the last pair, once for singleton weights 9/8/7 and once for 12/8/4. The singleton rewards, total available points, canonical field order, link loss and fees stay fixed within each pair. As a separate control, scale one complete reward profile by 0.004. Its largest possible payoff is 0.160 points, below one 0.2-point delivery fee. These are synthetic counterfactual rubrics for the same three-slot mechanism. The exact optimizer computes the best case-specific policies and the best shared policy for each pair. [Contrast definition and exact enumeration](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_reward_contrasts.py) · [Frozen router replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-reward-contrasts/panel.json)

At uniform inventories, the minimum mean regret for a shared policy is 1.041 points for the balanced complement switch, 0.596 for the steep switch and 0.154 for the change in scale. The previously frozen router matches the constant-input ablation on all five profiles. On the balanced last-pair profile it scores 12.514 against 14.365 for optimized independence and 16.017 for the exact optional policy. On the low-stakes profile its net return is −0.302 and the optimum is silence. These contrasts distinguish conditioning on numerical rewards from reusing a role order.

### Train on paired reward variations

Run one 320-update fit with the same architecture, sample count, optimizer and uniform inventory prior. The new training bank contains 24 synthetic sets with matched first-pair and last-pair bonuses, each at two scales. Normalize each task's expected net by its maximum payout with a floor of 0.25 points, so low-payout examples contribute to training. The bank and the loss scaling both change. Keep all five exact contrasts and the four historical reward profiles out of training. [Fit and held-out evaluations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-paired-reward-fit/report.json)

The revised policy sends nothing on the held-out low-stakes task, achieving the zero-point exact optimum. Feeding it a constant reward vector reduces the same endpoint to −0.308 points. On the four high-stakes contrasts, the revised and constant-input policies still produce the same scores. The revised policy beats optimized independence on eight of twelve historical case-prior cells, compared with six in the earlier fit. This is evidence of adapting the decision to communicate to the reward scale. The dependency switch remains unresolved in the network's greedy choices.

### Select among learned routing policies using public rewards

Take the mean-input action table from each synthetic checkpoint. Keep the distinct tables produced by the six field bindings of each and add an all-silent action, for 13 candidates. Score those fixed candidates on the public reward vector before either agent sees its private inventory. No new fitting occurs. One comparison always uses the uniform prior for selection. Another supplies the evaluation prior, as the exact independence comparator already has. A separate arm lets selection consider optimized independence as well. Historical realized record scores enter only at evaluation. [Portfolio code](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_learned_portfolio.py) · [Selected tables and comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-paired-reward-fit/portfolio.json)

Under uniform inventories, the selector switches field bindings when the complement bonus moves and selects silence in the low-stakes case. It beats the best policy held fixed across each contrast pair by 0.798, 0.138 and 0.064 mean points, respectively. Its balanced last-pair score is 15.709, compared with 13.115 for the revised standalone network, 14.365 for optimized independence and 16.017 for the exact query ceiling. The selector uses learned candidates and explicit planning over the announced rubric. This result demonstrates dependency adaptation at selection time.

On the historical record banks, uniform-prior selection wins seven of twelve cells. When given the evaluation prior, the learned portfolio wins eleven and loses Mali with correlated duplicates by 0.023. Allowing optimized independence as a candidate turns that loss into a tie, leaving eleven wins. The known-prior condition and the archive-based record scoring bound this result. Current evidence supports a small planning layer over trained policies. It leaves learning which prior applies, robust routing with a misspecified prior, and free-prose evidence use open.

### Route with uncertainty about private inventory distributions

Choose one shared policy mixture per public rubric. Fit the mixture to maximize its worst gain over optimized independence across the three declared inventory distributions. Use the 13 frozen portfolio candidates plus one exact independent table from each candidate distribution. Draw the table publicly before either agent sees its local inventory. The selector uses the public intended payoff and the candidate distributions. The actual receipt bank is reserved for scoring. Verify the finite game using both a feasible mixture and a matching adversarial weighting. Within the convex hull of the declared distributions, a per-distribution guarantee is measured against an independence planner that knows the distribution. Exact independence on an interior mixture cannot exceed the mixture of the individual independence optima.

Reserve four synthetic stress distributions from selection. Swap the two desks in the asymmetric case, change the outer field positions, increase duplication, and favor complementary inventories. Preserve the declared message and link costs. Compare the robust mix to the best single robust candidate, the prior portfolio's nominal uniform choice, and exact independent and optional-query policies given each evaluation distribution. The historical inventory distributions remain simulation assumptions. [Selection and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_prior_robustness.py)

On the historical banks, robust selection wins nine of twelve anchor comparisons with exact independence and ties three. The nominal uniform choice wins seven. The optimizer chooses one table per case, so mixing tables supplies no additional benefit on these inputs. Outside the anchor set, robust selection wins seven of sixteen, ties three and loses six. The nominal choice wins eight and loses eight, with a smaller worst deficit. The robust selector loses on all four desk swaps. Its worst stress deficit is 2.139 points on Outer Space. [Anchor and stress results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/report.json)

An exact optional-query oracle prices candidate tables against the adversarial weighting of the three anchors. Adding its candidates yields matching primal and dual bounds for all four historical public rubrics. The gaps between the learned library's worst-case planned gain and the unrestricted optimum range from 0.075 to 0.131 points. The unrestricted anchor-optimized policies improve the realized historical anchor record in every case-prior cell, and still lose on all four desk swaps. This separates a modest library capacity gap from sensitivity to a changed role distribution. [Exact unrestricted certificates](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/ceiling.json)

### Separate desk symmetry from information about the inventory prior

Assign the requester with a public fair coin before either desk sees its inventory. The value of a fixed table is then the mean under the original and transposed inventory distributions. Select the table from the original anchors using that objective. The desk swap remains outside selection. The largest swap deficit falls from 2.139 to 0.093 points, and Outer Space beats independence by 0.193 after either orientation. Three cases still lose narrowly on the swapped distribution. In Mali, even the exact symmetric query policy falls 0.029 short of optimized independence with the evaluation distribution supplied. This is a limit of the symmetric protocol under that information comparison. [Role assignment and exact ceilings](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/symmetry.json)

Test an additional-information condition using 32 previous synthetic joint inventory pairs drawn independently from the distribution of future episodes. Add 16 uniform pseudocounts to the histogram and select one table and requester orientation before any future inventory is seen. Repeat the pilot 128 times under each prior and reuse those counts across cases. Give an exact independent planner the same estimated distribution. Evaluate selected policies on future inventory draws in expectation, using the realized record banks only for scoring. The historical boards supply the outcome scores and the observations of inventories remain synthetic. [Pilot method](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_prior_pilot.py)

The pilot hybrid beats the independent oracle in mean future net on 10 of 12 anchor cells and 11 of 16 stress cells. Its mean gains on the four desk swaps are 0.394, 0.621, 0.004 and 0.230 points for UNIFIL, Outer Space, Mali and Myanmar, respectively. Mali wins 78 of 128 pilots, and three cases have negative tenth-percentile gains on the swap. Three control arms reveal separate costs: the prior-agnostic selector has no pilot, the symmetric selector pays for randomizing the requester in expected opportunity, and the pilot selector uses extra information. The table charges normal future episode communication fees. It excludes the cost of collecting the pilot. Validate on observed inventory variation and record acquisition costs before treating the pilot gains as a deployment result. [Pilot distributions and comparator scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/pilot.json) · [Replay checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/verification.json)

### Charge for stale and fresh pilot evidence

Test five inventory transitions with the frozen routing library and existing historical record banks. Compare 32 past observations, 24 past plus 8 recent, 8 recent, and 32 recent. Draw the old and recent samples from the two distributions assigned to the transition. All policies choose before any evaluated inventory is drawn. Each pilot observation carries two hypothetical disclosures at the existing delivery fee, for a 0.4-point collection cost. Retain exact future scores under the new distribution and assess 16-, 64-, and 256-episode horizons. These acquisition prices and transitions are simulation assumptions. [Matched pilot windows and acquisition accounting](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/pilot-drift.json)

After desk reassignment, the 32 stale samples lose an average 0.614 future net points per episode relative to the symmetric no-pilot policy, before paying for the pilot. Keeping 24 stale observations and adding 8 fresh still loses 0.375. Eight fresh observations yield positive mean gain on all four cases and cover the assumed collection cost within 64 future episodes on three. The strict break-even counts against symmetric routing are 21 for UNIFIL, 14 for Outer Space, 177 for Mali and 29 for Myanmar. The range from 14 to 177 episodes makes the proposed collection cost and operating horizon important benchmark inputs.

With access to 32 archived samples and eight recent samples, a recency selector scores the old table, the recent table and the no-pilot default on recent predictive performance. Each recent score uses a table selected without that observation. The selector improves on recent-only in all four stationary and all four field-reassignment cases. It underperforms recent-only on all four desk reassignments. Even with archived counts available for free, a small validation window can retain a stale strategy. [Recency checks and cost regimes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/pilot-recency.json)

### Test whether ordinary receipt logs can identify the better routing strategy

For each historical bank, compute the exact channel from private inventory pairs to public role assignments and receipt masks under the symmetric incumbent. The channel has rank 11 over 64 latent inventory pairs. Construct two positive inventory distributions in its observational equivalence class, using the public rubric to choose the perturbation. They produce the same public observation probabilities to within 1.6e−16. Under independent episodes and fixed logging, even an unlimited passive sample cannot discriminate these two distributions. Exact policies with the prior and requester orientation known outperform the best policy without that information by 0.146 to 0.572 mean net points per episode on the four observed record banks. The result is specific to the incumbent channel. It allows for richer instrumentation and deliberate probe policies. [Channel, witness priors and exact upper bounds](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/receipt-identifiability.json)

For those known two-hypothesis contrasts, choose a learned table and role assignment to use for eight in-task probe episodes, then select the table for 64 more episodes using the receipt histogram. Enumerate every possible count histogram. Compare with applying the best fixed portfolio strategy for all 72 episodes. Charge the probe episodes their usual message fees and account for any lost reward relative to the fixed portfolio. The planner adopts a probe in UNIFIL, Mali and Myanmar and continues with the fixed portfolio in Outer Space. On the observed banks, the total gains over the fixed portfolio are 8.645, 2.370 and 5.252 points in the three chosen cases. The two hypotheses are constructed benchmark inputs, and no joint inventory disclosures are used. [Exact active-probe replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/active-receipt-probe.json) · [Replay verification](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/pilot-followup-verification.json)

### Probe under uncertainty about which distributions are plausible

Use the same eight probe episodes plus 64 subsequent episodes, but plan against the three original inventory anchors, with equal weight on each. The agent observes the union of delivered fields in each pilot episode. It chooses an oriented table from the frozen portfolio using the likelihood of the exact histogram. Compare against the best anchor-average fixed table over all 72 episodes, charging both strategies for their messages and crediting the records produced during the pilot. Score once under each anchor, each of four excluded stress distributions and each of two case-specific passive aliases. The observed banks are used only for scoring. [Selection and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_population_probe.py)

The planned rule probes in Mali only, with an expected public-rubric gain of 0.004 points total. The other banks already use the same best observed-bank portfolio table under all three anchors. In Mali the mean realized gain across the anchors is 0.003 points. The largest held-out gain is 0.535 under more duplicates. The two passive aliases lose 0.009 and 0.035. Across the four swapped-desk cells, the fixed table leaves 25.0–104.9 points available relative to the best portfolio table given the true prior, and the selector recovers none of that gap. The large gains in the earlier pairwise experiment depend on supplying plausible alternatives that change the best decision. Include shift families that change that decision when evaluating a learned routing policy or its value of information. [Matched-horizon results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/population-probe.json)

### Attribute delivered fields to the sending desk

Record each desk's delivered field ID, or zero if nothing arrived, on the same fixed symmetric routes. The ordered delivery log retains the policy draw and the public requester assignment. It leaves task fees and private inventory access unchanged. Summing over all ordered results that produce each union mask recovers the original channel exactly. The richer channel increases rank from 11 to 25 of 64 inventory pairs. For the four original aliases, one episode distinguishes the hypotheses with 57.1–58.0% optimal accuracy at equal odds. Upper bounds after eight ordinary episodes lie between 75.8% and 79.2%, depending on the case. [Ordered delivery-channel audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-prior-robustness/ordered-receipts.json)

The richer channel still admits decision-relevant aliases. Construct new pairs with the public rubric that match the distribution of ordered deliveries to within 5.1e−16 in total variation. With eight episodes or any larger passive sample, those pairs have the same likelihood under the fixed protocol. Revealing which prior applies and letting the supervisor choose the requester is worth 0.057–0.694 realized net points per episode across the four banks. This establishes an information limit for this logging setup and synthetic inventory class. Include a pair of priors requiring different actions after identical passive observations when testing robust adapters. Hold the bank and the benchmark scoring rules fixed across logging conditions. [Construction and exact policy values](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_ordered_receipts.py)

### Separate new peer evidence from different use of an existing dossier

For each improved final criterion, locate the exact final span and source span, then record where the chair could first see the supporting proposition. Credit new evidence delivered by a peer only when the proposition was available in the peer's delivered words or direct source receipt and absent from the chair's matched independent input. A peer-conditioned draft can also change which part of an already shared dossier the chair uses. Score that change on the common final rubric and require a matched replay before attributing it to the draft. Count it separately from new evidence, and retain both a dossier-only chair and an independent-drafts chair for the comparison. This availability distinction adapts the shared-versus-unshared evidence design in the original hidden-profile experiments. [Stasser and Titus](https://www.uni-muenster.de/imperia/md/content/psyifp/aeechterhoff/vorlesungkommunikation/stasser_titus_unsharedinfogroupdisc_jpsp1985.pdf)

Use two archived development anchors. In the outer-space opportunity, the interactive chair receives China's explanation in a transmitted note, which the independent chair lacks, and retains three concepts from it. The records tie at 14/70 and both fail the guard. In Sudan the archived interactive chair alone includes humanitarian access. The claim comes from the dossier already present in all arms, and neither delivered draft supplies it. One draft wording differs and no new peer evidence reaches the chair. These are separate outcomes for a process judge, even if both records contain examples of successful source use. [Outer-space trace and process scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/space-final-routing-transfer/process-review.json) · [Sudan paired provenance](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-sudan/process.json)

For Sudan draft S/2024/826, the accession check finds the text in UNBench and all 15 votes in UNSC-Bench. The UNBench author vote tables have no matching row for the draft's ID and 2024 date. The 15 same-ID training rows refer to a 2014 vote. Keep the document join and vote join distinct, and retain the official roll call as the reference. [Pinned-release join](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/sudan-accession.json) · [Official Council report and roll call](https://www.un.org/pga/wp-content/uploads/sites/109/2024/11/241121-PSC-letter-to-PGA-Special-Rpt-UNSC-9786th-mtg-SIGNED57.pdf)

Replay six Sudan chair calls with frozen Qwen3.5-4B. Give every chair the same six evidence blocks and 256-token final cap. Cross three draft conditions with the original evidence order and its reversal. The independent and peer arms reuse their archived drafts and reproduce both original outputs token for token. The dossier-only chair provides a same-cap control and consumes fewer input tokens. It has no upstream drafting calls. Review the raw records under the existing ten-part, 70-point rubric and require a clear guard, the word limit, and a completed generation for release eligibility. [Source-linked scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-sudan-chair-control/scores.json)

| Dossier order | Independent drafts | Peer-visible drafts | No drafts |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Original | 42, eligible | 49, capped | 49, capped |
| Reversed | 49, eligible | 42, eligible | 42, guard failed |

The raw peer-minus-independent contrast changes from +7 to −7. Humanitarian access appears in the peer record and the draft-free record in the original order. After reversal it appears in the independent and draft-free records. The reversed draft-free text also implies joint support before reporting Russia's sole negative vote. All conditions omit the explicit 14–1–0 tally and the shared consultation comparison. These are exact-span, same-assistant ratings on one previously inspected docket. The result shows sensitivity to source order under frozen generation. Treat final score gains, completion, guard failures, and peer-supplied evidence as distinct outcomes, and include paired presentation-order controls on fresh families. [All six unedited records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/source-record-sudan-chair-control/final-records.json)

### Calibrate the judge on the chair's actual evidence access

Give the judge one final claim, its dated official reference, and three distinctly marked locations: material in the starting dossier, a note delivered to the chair, and a note never delivered. Keep the official reference available to the judge regardless of what the chair received. Require one label for a supported claim already available, supported new evidence delivered by a peer, supported evidence the chair never received, or a claim the reference does not support. If the dossier and peer both establish the proposition, label it already available. If a peer exactly repeats a historically false claim, the reference takes precedence. This measures availability of a supporting proposition. Attribution of a downstream behavior change still needs a matched replay.

Use three development anchors from the archived Sudan, outer-space and Western Sahara records. Hold each supported claim fixed and move its evidence across locations, including a repeated-evidence control. Add a false claim repeated by an actually delivered note. Rotate each label through every answer position. A useful training reward would have to credit all newly delivered anchors and avoid granting new-peer credit to every control, visible or not. Check both individual verdicts and unanimous decisions. [Executable controls and judge instructions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/peer_evidence_judge.py)

On frozen Qwen3.5-4B, all 60 four-way answers parse, 30 are correct, and none of the 15 cases is correct in all four orders. It selects D in 37 calls and never selects A. At least one rotation awards new-peer credit to an unsent Outer Space note and to a false claim repeated by a delivered note. The compound Outer Space claim includes amendment support, which its reference excerpt conveys only implicitly. Excluding its four supported-claim conditions still leaves zero stable cases and 21/44 correct calls. The results motivate requiring order controls in addition to valid formatting. [All verdicts and rotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/peer-evidence-judge/scores.json) · [Pezeshkpour and Hruschka, option-order sensitivity](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-naacl.130/)

The same three dockets supply exact-span examples from unedited traces. The Sudan chair uses a fact in its starting dossier. The outer-space chair retains new information about China's preferred broader measures, and a matched independent condition with the same receipt gives the same output. The Western Sahara chair repeats a delivered misclassification of Algeria's non-participation even though the correct value is public. Preserve these traces as separate controls for the interaction score and the historical guard. [Verified receipt and source spans](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/archived-peer-provenance.json)

A second version asks the judge whether one excerpt establishes one claim. The harness separately checks the official reference, the starting dossier, and the delivered note. It applies the evidence precedence in code and never passes unsent text into these checks. Deduplicating identical claim–excerpt pairs yields 17 pairs. The affirmative question produces the correct final class on 14/15 controls and misses the new Sudan evidence stated in different words. The inverted question produces 0/15. The judge gives the same raw YES or NO to both wordings on all six official-reference checks, so requiring agreement leaves all 15 controls unresolved. This prevents false peer credit and yields zero usable reward coverage under that rule. [Binary judgments, receipt scoring and abstentions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/peer-evidence-factored/scores.json)

A follow-up retains the original affirmative call and checks the same claims with a second affirmative formulation. It also adds five UNIFIL conditions from the joined case, including a peer that turns Brazil's “some” into “all.” The two wordings agree correctly on 10/15 development conditions. They agree on one error, missing the paraphrased Sudan delivery, and abstain on the four compound Outer Space conditions. Both correctly resolve all five UNIFIL conditions, including rejecting the false scope expansion and recognizing the narrowly paraphrased new peer message. Neither wording grants false new-peer credit on this panel. Use one atomic proposition per claim, verify the official source separately, and enforce delivery access in code. This is a development calibration with one extension docket. It leaves independent review and the calibration of whole-record rewards on unseen families open. [Affirmative wording and UNIFIL results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/peer-evidence-affirmative/scores.json) · [UNIFIL release and source join](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unifil-reserve.json)

### Check each proposition against the source that establishes it

Split the Outer Space claim into China's supported vote on S/2024/323 and its reason for seeking early work on binding multilateral agreements. The two questions agree on all six atomic cases. They credit the dossier for the vote, credit the delivered note for the rationale only when it supplies new information, and reject a false vote claim about the final draft. Providing the two official excerpts to the combined-claim control still leaves its verdict unresolved. The combined claim requires evidence from two locations. The one-excerpt verifier should be applied to each proposition with its own evidence and receipt history.

Narrow the Sudan claim to the draft's humanitarian-access provision. Leave the original paraphrased delivery unchanged in one control. Both questions now recognize the original paraphrase and a shorter paraphrase, withhold peer credit for a generic humanitarian note, and reject a false delivery that reverses the access provision. The ten atomic cases are same-assistant development controls on exposed dockets. They supply evidence about the verifier given preselected facts and do not test extraction of those facts from a full record. [Atomic controls and all 40 model decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/peer-evidence-atomic/scores.json)

### Assign credit when a request changes the reply

Compare two sampled MARL fits with the same initialization, 160 updates per arm, 384 simulated episodes per update, the same random draws, and the existing paired synthetic training rubrics. The shared-reward arm subtracts a running mean for the current rubric. The counterfactual arm averages over the current agent's legal actions as a baseline. For the requester, changing a query can change the responder's observed message and policy action, so the baseline replays the responder under each alternative query with the same exogenous draws. For the responder, the received question and the requester's contribution are fixed. Use typed receipts and source-backed reward banks, and leave the language-model judge out of optimization. This is an exact-model variant of action-marginalized credit assignment in a short sequential game. [Foerster et al., counterfactual multi-agent policy gradients](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/download/11794/11653) · [Paired fit and policies](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-sampled-credit/report.json)

At update 40, normalized exact training return is 0.212 with counterfactual credit and 0.201 with the shared reward. At update 160 it is 0.212 and 0.241. The normalized advantage RMS is 0.046 and 0.190 across the two training trajectories. Counterfactual credit therefore helps the early objective and loses on the final training objective in this single paired run. Exact scoring on the four archived final-record banks under uniform inventories gives the following net scores. [Training trace and fixed-policy replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-sampled-credit/report.json)

| Docket | Shared reward | Counterfactual credit | Optimized independent delivery | Counterfactual with requests erased |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| UNIFIL | 47.788 | 48.798 | 47.713 | 47.681 |
| Outer Space | 37.998 | 38.601 | 37.729 | 37.698 |
| Mali | 63.403 | 63.952 | 63.733 | 63.651 |
| Myanmar | 53.193 | 53.707 | 53.118 | 52.910 |

The counterfactual policies beat the shared-reward policies on all 12 anchor and 16 stress cells. They beat optimized independent delivery on 8/12 anchors and 6/16 stress cells. Their mean gain over optimized independence is 0.112 on the anchors and their mean deficit is 0.453 under stress. The reloaded tensors and deterministic refit are exact. On an ordered two-agent test, the action-marginalized score-function gradient agrees with the exact expected reward gradient to within 3.4e−16. These outcomes use simulated private inventories, archived records and one paired initialization. They show transfer of a structured request policy across the measured banks and give no estimate of improved free-text negotiation. [Seven-prior stress replay and estimator verification](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-sampled-credit/verification.json)

### Train against inventory shift without revealing the distribution

In the earlier counterfactual fit, request erasure lowers the score in 12 of the 14 cells that lose to prior-informed independent delivery. The full policy can fail even where its questions help. A public coin choosing which desk becomes requester reduces the worst loss on the desk swap from 2.316 to 1.071 points, while leaving a 1.638-point loss on the mostly complementary Outer Space inventories. This checks desk assignment and question value separately. [Frozen policy diagnosis](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-regret-training/verification.json)

Fit two teams with the same 160 batches of 384 sampled episodes, counterfactual credit, and seven synthetic inventory groups. The groups include uniform, two previously unused asymmetric profiles and their desk swaps, and milder duplicate and complement mixtures. Both teams act from the announced rubric and local inventories. The first weights training groups equally. The second adjusts group weights every eight updates using the exact normalized score gap against optimized independence for that training group. The nonuniform historical test distributions are absent from training. [Matched curricula, weights and final policies](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-regret-training/report.json)

| Training | Anchor wins | Stress wins | Mean stress gain | Worst stress gain |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Uniform only | 8/12 | 6/16 | −0.453 | −2.316 |
| Varied inventories, equal weights | 7/12 | 6/16 | −0.401 | −1.893 |
| Varied inventories, weakest groups weighted | 7/12 | 6/16 | −0.403 | −1.893 |

Scores are net points relative to independent policies that can use the true test distribution. Both varied-inventory fits improve the worst stress result and both still lose in ten stress cells. The two group-weighting rules choose identical tables on three of the four dockets. Their only policy difference is on Mali. This comparison covers one matched initialization on typed receipt tasks, with the same previously reported historical record banks. [Deterministic refit and receipt replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-regret-training/verification.json)

### Bound what a policy can guarantee with hidden inventories

Run an exact pricing oracle on the seven evaluation distributions and the realized record banks. A policy can react to local inventories and a received question, and can publicly randomize its table and requester assignment. It chooses without knowing the current distribution. To price the upper bound, weight the seven priors using a finite game over available strategies, then price the optimal table on that weighted prior for each requester orientation. This requires enumerating 746,496 requester maps per orientation. Any distribution over priors gives a valid upper bound after exact pricing. A feasible public policy mixture supplies a lower bound. This is a hindsight capacity check, with the realized scores available to the oracle. [Policy mixtures, certificates and exact bounds](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-regret-training/blind-limit.json)

| Docket | Feasible worst-case gain | Upper bound on best worst-case gain |
|---|---:|---:|
| UNIFIL | −0.242 | −0.043 |
| Outer Space | +0.182 | +0.254 |
| Mali | −0.163 | −0.045 |
| Myanmar | −0.220 | −0.008 |

Gains use optimized, prior-informed independent delivery as the per-prior reference. The three negative upper bounds rule out matching that reference on every tested prior in those dockets, even with a public policy lottery. The positive Outer Space lower bound exhibits a mixture beating that reference in every tested condition. For a comparison with equal information, optimize independent delivery under the same hidden-prior constraint and measure worst-case gain against the same informed reference. The feasible interactive mixtures improve that robust score by at least 1.115 points on UNIFIL, 1.343 on Outer Space, 0.239 on Mali and 0.852 on Myanmar. These are gains in worst-case scores across policies. They establish value from communication under matched prior information for the typed protocol, with retrospective calibration and archived final records. [Matched-information independent bound](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-regret-training/fair-control.json)

### Freeze Outer Space selection before archived record scoring

Use the announced rubric to choose a policy mixture while hiding which inventory distribution is active. Keep realized receipt scores out of the optimization and persist the mixture before replay. The announced and realized payoff vectors disagree by seven points on receipts containing both April positions, after removing the fixed 21-point public record. The seven-prior plan nevertheless beats prior-informed independence on all seven distributions. Its smallest gain is 0.140 points, compared with the retrospective feasible gain of 0.182. The worst-case advantage over any prior-blind independent policy is at least 1.302 points. Question erasure reduces net scores on all seven distributions. [Frozen plan and artifact locks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-announced-selection/plan.json) · [Archived scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-announced-selection/scores.json)

Plan again with just the three original anchor distributions. This wins on six of seven and loses by 0.217 points when the desks swap. Both selections use the same announced rubric and realized record bank. The full-set plan is robust within the disclosed stress set. The anchor-only run supplies a genuine withheld-prior control within this exposed docket. [Three-anchor plan and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-announced-selection/anchor-scores.json)

The full-set mixture assigns 96.0% of its probability to added exact-search routing tables. Restricting its candidates to the frozen portfolio and original controls changes the realized worst-case gain to −0.529. Using only the added columns and the silent controls gives +0.168, with the mixture weights still selected on the announced rubric. Those columns were generated using the full portfolio as a warm start. This menu ablation credits the positive result mainly to exact search. It does not establish an advantage from the learned warm start. [Candidate-menu ablation](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/routing-announced-selection/ablation.json)

### Transfer to the August 2020 written vote

Freeze a new retrospective docket for Indonesia's draft S/2020/852. The Security Council used its pandemic-era written procedure. The draft received 14 votes in favour and one against, with no abstentions. The U.S. negative vote prevented adoption. The result letter and full compilation have distinct document symbols, S/2020/865 and S/2020/870. Both Indonesia's national explanation and its representative's separate procedural role are in scope. The two benchmark releases verify the draft and all 15 votes. The UNBench author vote tables have no match on both the draft ID and date. An ID-only lookup returns a 2009 vote. [Accession and file hashes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/accession.json) · [Official vote result](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/3878826) · [Full written record and national statements](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/3885224/files/S_2020_870-EN.pdf)

Protect the five public criteria using a common 35-point record. Deliver optional source summaries for the United States, Russian Federation and Indonesia. The U.S. and Russian explanations give separate national reasons. Together they support a comparison that preserves the shared concern over repatriation and the opposite votes. The Indonesian source distinguishes advocacy of national strategies from notification of the Council's vote. The announced payoff structure matches UNIFIL, allowing its frozen public-profile candidates to be reused. Freeze both the policy mixture and a prior-blind independent mixture before reading the fresh records. Keep the exact independent benchmark that can use the true prior as an additional control. [Task and frozen rubric](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/task.json) · [Frozen routing plan](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-record-transfer/plan.json)

Frozen Qwen3.5-4B wrote seven supplements covering every nonempty receipt. All finished within 75 words. The routing mixture contains frozen learned candidates and pre-existing controls, with no newly priced table. Its raw record score beat the announced-only independent mixture in all seven inventory conditions, by 0.120 to 1.758 points. It beat independently optimized delivery with the true prior and realized scores in three conditions. Erasing requests lowered all seven raw scores.

Under the frozen release rule, two supplements failed and two were uncertain. A U.S.-only record denied that a national explanation had been provided after reporting it. A Russian and Indonesian record treated the negative vote as procedural. Both speakers' shared concern was correctly expressed when both explanations arrived, but the two-source record ambiguously called the failed draft a Council act. None of the supplements completed the full U.S. geographic qualifier or identified Indonesia's representative in both capacities. Under the strict review only one of seven matched-information wins survives release gating. Treating the two uncertain records as eligible restores six wins, with the confirmed errors still excluded. The guard sensitivity is larger than the raw routing gains. Preserve this docket as development evidence and adjudicate the ambiguous wording independently before using the release score for optimization. [Assembled records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-record-transfer/assembled-records.json) · [Exact-span annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-record-transfer/annotations.json) · [Matched-information replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-record-transfer/same-information-scores.json) · [Guard sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-record-transfer/sensitivity.json)

### Test whether writing instructions preserve the evidence routing gains

Keep the written-vote docket, evidence blocks, vote paragraph, seven possible receipts, two frozen policy mixtures, token ceiling, and scoring rubric fixed. Replace only the writer's system instruction. Ask for source-by-source explanations, with the national and presidential capacities identified explicitly and a single comparison when the U.S. and Russian explanations both arrive. The first instruction includes guidance for all three roles in every prompt. [Paired prompts and outputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-bounded-writer/transcript.jsonl)

The U.S.-only receipt now preserves the full motive and geographic qualifier. The U.S.–Russia receipt rises from 56 raw points and uncertain eligibility to 63 and clear eligibility. Three prompts lacking the U.S. explanation produce a false claim that the U.S. opposed prosecution strategies. The release result against same-information independent delivery improves from one to five wins across seven priors. Wins on the raw score stay at seven. The team still loses all seven strict comparisons to independent delivery given the true prior and realized record scores. [Exact-span score and paired net changes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-bounded-writer/comparison.json)

Because the policies and costs are fixed, multiply the score change for each receipt by the difference in receipt probabilities between team and control. The improved joint U.S.–Russia record contributes +0.49 to +5.57 points to the shift in the release margin, depending on the prior. The repaired U.S.-only record can help independence more, since that control often delivers it. Keep absolute record score and the relative team margin visible together. [Per-receipt decomposition](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-bounded-writer/effects.json)

Next, remove the writing instructions for roles whose annexes were never delivered, and remove the comparison request unless both U.S. and Russian annexes are present. Keep the actual user evidence unchanged. Generate six new supplements. The full-receipt prompt and its output are reused. The three invented U.S. explanations disappear. Indonesia's single-annex output now names the representative and gives both capacities with the full national proposal. All seven supplements finish within the word limit and meet the release guard. Some multi-source versions still omit a qualifier or part of Indonesia's rationale. [Assembled records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-visible-writer/assembled-records.json) · [Exact-span annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-visible-writer/annotations.json)

| Writer instruction | Eligible receipts | Wins over matched-information independence | Wins over prior-informed independence |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Original prose | 3/7 | 1/7 | 0/7 |
| Source-by-source with all roles named | 3/7 | 5/7 | 0/7 |
| Source-by-source with delivered roles only | 7/7 | 7/7 | 4/7 |

The receipt-specific instruction gives gains of 0.085 to 1.212 points against matched-information independence. Erasing questions lowers the release score in every condition. A post-run comparator selects from twelve hand-scripted requester variants and the original independent controls. It uses only the announced rubric to set weights and has the same delivery limits and fees. The frozen learned mixture leads that scripted mixture by 0.668 to 1.344 points in all seven conditions. This is evidence that the learned routing mixture can improve release-qualified text on the adapted docket. The chair still uses a fixed 35-point public paragraph, the routed evidence is access-controlled, and review is by the same assistant. Test the delivery-specific instructions and scripted comparator on a separate family before treating the effect as general. [Seven-prior results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-visible-writer/comparison.json) · [Scripted requester control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/ftf2020-visible-writer/script-control.json)

### Transfer scoped writing to the 2021 climate-security vote

Test the delivery-specific instruction on a different Council instrument. In meeting S/PV.8926, Ireland and Niger led the draft that proposed incorporating climate-related security risk into UN conflict prevention. India and Russia voted against it, China abstained, and Russia's negative vote prevented adoption. Link the UNBench draft to all 15 UNSC-Bench votes. Use the primary meeting for the separate national statements. The three optional fields are Russia's objection to an automatic climate-security link and preference for a Sahel-specific alternative, India's concern for developing-country participation and support for that alternative, and China's country-specific approach and emphasis on differentiated responsibilities. The Russia–India receipt can support the additional comparison on both the UNFCCC and the Sahel. [Task and fixed 70-point rubric](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/climate2021/task.json) · [Accession](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/climate2021/accession.json) · [Official meeting record](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/3951616/files/S_PV.8926-EN.pdf) · [Draft text](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/3951276/files/S_2021_990-EN.pdf)

Transfer the written-ballot routing policy, including its frozen same-information independent mixture and scripted control, because the announced receipt payoff vector is identical. Freeze all three before writing. Use the same 35-point public procedure scaffold, 160-token and 75-word supplement limits, and delivered source text in both conditions. In one condition provide the instructions for all roles. In the other give only instructions for delivered roles and request the comparison only when both sources arrive. The full-receipt prompt is identical in both conditions and is evaluated once. [Frozen plan](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-writer-transfer/plan.json)

The all-role instruction produces explanations for an absent delegation on four receipts. Some of those explanations are historically accurate and earn no credit because the cited delegation's statement was never delivered. The delivery-specific prompts remove all four off-receipt explanations and increase the team's absolute release score by 0.569–0.862 points across the seven fixed distributions. Both writer conditions release six of seven records under the frozen guard. In the delivery-specific condition, the India-only supplement incorrectly narrows the alternative to climate-driven conflict in the Sahel, whereas the source describes a broader security draft focused geographically on the Sahel. The receipt-specific team beats blind independent delivery in all seven cases and the scripted mixture in six. It loses to informed independence on release in all seven.
