[Paired scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-writer-transfer/scores.json) · [Evidence and scope adjudication](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-writer-transfer/annotations.json)

Add a general instruction to distinguish geographic and policy scope to the four receipts that include India's explanation. The India-only output still limits the alternative to climate-induced conflict prevention, and one previously clear receipt now describes China's position in a way that makes the alternative's scope uncertain. Eligible receipts fall from six to five. The learned route's absolute release scores decrease on all seven priors. The result preserves the failure of the additional prompt instruction. [Follow-up generations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-scope-writer/transcript.jsonl) · [Fixed-policy scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-scope-writer/scores.json)

To isolate the India-only error, keep the original transfer bank and edit that one receipt in two ways. Withhold the faulty sentence and release the remaining forum rationale, or replace only the unsupported limit with the geographic scope confirmed in India's source. The first change gives a 35-point eligible record and the second gives 42. Both alternatives make all seven receipts eligible, with the learned route ahead of the independent and scripted mixtures on all seven priors. The source-bound correction restores four wins against informed independence. These edits are reviewed interventions on the archived text and supply a measured target for a future automatic scope check. [Before and after text, source, and exact replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-scope-writer/source-edit.json)

### Check the scope of a proposed alternative before release

The Sahel error joins two independent assertions. The alternative focused on a particular region, and the writer added that its subject matter was limited to climate-related conflict. Test two automatic checks using the same candidate sentences. The source-support check compares each full sentence with the delivered statement. The candidate-only check asks what the restriction modifies and applies the docket's known boundary in code. Neither judge supplies new record prose. Use two question phrasings per check and record disagreements separately. The twelve-item panel includes actual model wording, the reviewed correction, a neighboring two-source record, and matched synthetic claims with different uses of “only” and “limited.” [Executable scope calibration](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_climate_scope_gate.py)

For the routing replay, apply the resulting scope decisions to the offending India-only record and the neighboring two-source control. A blocked or unresolved sentence is withheld. Preserve the rest verbatim apart from whitespace, withhold any rubric points that depended on the removed text, and keep the other guards under the earlier exact-span annotation. This tests a source-specific safeguard with the existing plans and recorded answers. It leaves whole-record judgment outside the automation. [Receipt replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/validity-audit/routing_climate_gate_replay.py)

The source-support check classifies 9/12 sentences correctly. It abstains twice and its one false acceptance is the actual India-only error. The candidate-only check classifies 6/12 correctly, abstains four times and falsely clears two synthetic restrictions. It abstains on both actual versions of the India-only error. Withholding the unresolved sentence keeps the rest of the original record and leaves the correct two-source record intact. On that localized replay the learned mixture leads the scripted control in all seven priors, by 0.628–1.270 points. The ordinary support gate leaves the original error in the bank. These are outcomes under existing review labels for the rest of each record. [Calibration and exact decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-scope-gate/scores.json) · [Unedited remainders and fixed-policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-scope-gate/replay.json)

Require both checks to clear the sentence. On the development panel this admits five of six supported sentences and none of the six unsupported restrictions. Freeze that rule, then evaluate six new Sahel sentences and six new Brazil–UNIFIL sentences. The UNIFIL extractor asks whether the sentence promotes “some” Lebanese concerns to “all.” The combined rule again admits five of six supported sentences and none of the six unsupported restrictions. The source-support-only check produces the same totals on the new panel, while the candidate-only check misses the new variant that puts non-weather concerns outside the proposal's remit. The combined rule matters on the archived India-only error that source support cleared. It withholds the bad sentence and preserves the neighboring record, so the fixed-policy replay matches the candidate-only replay. These are authored contrast checks on two previously exposed dockets. [New case-by-case judgments](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/scope-gate-transfer/scores.json)

The demonstrated replay invokes four judgments when either of the two calibrated receipts occurs. Account for the difference in that receipt probability between the learned and scripted mixtures. The smallest break-even price across the seven priors is 2.030 score points per extra judgment. This is a hypothetical price in rubric units for the scoped intervention. It excludes checking the other receipts and does not estimate runtime cost in dollars or seconds. [Expected judge-call counts and break-even values](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-scope-gate/cost.json)

### Route generated national notes through the climate-security comparison

Give each frozen role writer just its national source summary. Generate a natural-language note for each of the three delegation slots, then run the receipt-specific chair on every nonempty subset of those exact unedited notes. Freeze the learned, scripted and independent routing plans, chair instructions, message fees and evidence masks. The chair sees generated notes in place of the approved source summaries. The simulator caches one generated note per role. Questions change which note arrives, and the wording of each note is fixed across query paths. For each awarded private criterion, record the exact chair span and its support in each relevant note. [Role notes and chair inputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-channel/transcript.jsonl) · [Source and receipt annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-channel/annotations.json)

The three notes preserve the required national positions. The chair still narrows India's Sahel alternative to climate pressures, despite the India note retaining the geographic distinction. The release-qualified team beats matched-information independence in seven of seven inventory conditions and the scripted control in six. Its absolute release score falls by 0.569–0.862 points versus routing the approved summaries, because it now omits China's explicit Council role when both the India and China notes arrive. The learned and scripted routes differ by at most 1.33 expected attempted language tokens per episode in this replay, excluding the chair and query text. [Frozen-policy scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-channel/scores.json) · [Absolute effects and attempted token volume](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-channel/effects.json)

All notes finish within the hard 88-token generation limit. The India and China notes use 58 and 61 words, exceeding the requested 55. A strict admission replay discards both notes and uses the actual chair record for each remaining subset. The learned team then beats the scripted control in two of seven conditions. This separates the raw language channel from a budget-compliant channel. [Word-budget admission replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-channel/strict-budget.json)

Enforce the word budget by forwarding the longest leading sequence of complete sentences within 55 words. It preserves the 48-word Russian note and shortens the Indian and Chinese notes to 41 and 40 words. The two omitted sentences were restatements. All remaining credited claims are still witnessed by the admitted notes. Reuse the Russian-only record and regenerate the six affected chair receipts. The India-only supplement is byte-identical to its earlier text and retains the scope error. In this climate replay the joint Russia–India record loses its explicit UNFCCC comparison, while the India–China record gains credit for China's country-specific Council role. All three notes pass the hard admission limits. At release the learned route beats independence on all seven priors and the script on five. This recovers three of the five scripted losses under dropping the long notes, and leaves the final writer as a separate source of error. [Admitted note text and affected receipts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-note-budget-writer/preflight.json) · [Word-compliant channel scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-note-budget-writer/scores.json)

Run the already calibrated scope rule on three actual sentences in the word-compliant bank. Check the offending India-only sentence against the admitted India note. Check the two valid Sahel sentences in the Russia–India record against their respective notes. The source-support questions alone clear the bad sentence. The restriction checks disagree on it, triggering the combined rule to withhold it. Both neighboring control sentences remain. The resulting eligible score vector is `[35, 42, 35, 49, 42, 42, 42, 56]` in receipt order. [All twelve judgments and final released text](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-gate/scores.json)

The word-compliant and scope-checked learned route beats its fixed scripted comparison by 0.233–0.754 points and its fixed independent comparison by 0.383–0.589 points across all seven priors. It wins two of seven comparisons to prior-informed optimized independence. Request erasure reduces all seven scores. The routes attempt nearly the same volume of note text. The learned route would call the targeted gate more often than the script. The smallest break-even charge is 0.436 score points per additional judgment, compared with 2.030 in the earlier approved-summary replay that checked fewer sentences. Treat these as rubric-unit sensitivities. This is a retrospective replay on one adapted docket with other assertions reviewed manually and one cached note per role. [Word and judge-cost replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-language-gate/cost.json)

### Deliver a live question and trace it into the chair's record

The highest-weight frozen climate routing component asks for India when the requesting desk holds only Russia. The smallest responsive inventory holds both. Generate the Russian note and the question once with frozen Qwen3.5-4B. Give the responder the same two annexes and the same instruction in both conditions. In one condition it reads the actual question. In the other it follows the declared first-annex default. It never receives a separate typed answer target. Both conditions attempt a question and a reply under the same 392-token total generation ceiling. Two annexes reach the chair when the question arrives. When it is erased, the chair instead reads two Russian notes. [Plan and exact token transcript](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-exchange/transcript.jsonl)

The delivered reply adds four attributable Indian points under the local idea ledger, including India's developing-country representation concern. The duplicate Russian reply adds none. The joint record earns 49 raw points versus 42 after erasure, owing to the explicit shared Sahel position. It fails release because it assigns Russia's objection to an automatic climate-security link to both delegations. India’s representation concern also drops out. An attribution-focused prompt replay uses the identical evidence and changes only the chair instruction. It again yields 49 raw points but fails release, now by ascribing a position on expanding Council climate authority to both. The erased condition retains 42 eligible points under both versions. The fixed public paragraph contributes 35 points. Review uses exact spans from the admitted notes and final text and was done by the same assistant. [Live scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-exchange/scores.json) · [Attribution replay and annotations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-attribution/annotations.json)

The language reply instruction implements a limited form of the trained rule. Exact enumeration gives four of 64 worlds where its prescribed action differs from the highest-weight routing component. When the requested Russian or Indian source is absent, the learned policy can send China instead of repeating a source the requester already has. Across seven frozen priors the literal guide gives up 0.064–1.132 expected announced rubric points for that component. This is a projected protocol loss from a typed replay. It makes unavailable-request handling a separate test from successful direct retrieval. [World-level projection](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-exchange/literal-guide-projection.json)

Writing each national sentence from one distinct annex removes the false shared attribution. The first isolated writer earns 35 eligible points in both conditions because it drops “automatic” for Russia and “consensus-based” for India. Giving each isolated call its existing single-annex rubric rule restores India's consensus and representation details, for 42 eligible points with the request and 35 without. Russia's wording remains incomplete. This equals the strongest 42-point erased control from the joint writers. Neither isolated writer supplies a cross-delegation comparison. Caps are 80 tokens per distinct annex and the assembler concatenates the sentences verbatim. [Isolated writer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-isolated/scores.json) · [Coverage-conditioned writer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-coverage/scores.json)

Keep the evidence gain, the chair score, and the guard visible as separate results. This selected docket gives a direct request-to-reply effect and a release-qualified gain within one writer configuration. A convincing deliberation result still requires comparisons with the strongest solo and independent-draft systems, exact coverage of different national positions and shared positions, and independent review on unseen event families. [Run protocol and reproduction](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-exchange/README.md)

### Test what the learned unavailable-request reply adds

Test two worlds selected from the four discrepancies above. In one, the request asks for India's annex and the responder holds Russia and China. In the other, it asks for Russia and the responder holds India and China. Both learned-table replies deliver China. The ordinary fallback and the query-erased conditions each repeat the source the requester already has. An available-India control selects India. All seven reply selections match their respective planned tables under identical 96-token generation caps. The prose is a separate result. One no-question Russian reply attributes China's financing and technology positions to Russia. The syntax and word budget admit that unsupported text. [Matched live replies](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-fallback/live-summary.json)

A simple rule can reproduce the learned fallback. Send the requested annex when held. For an unavailable request send a different held annex from the no-question default, if possible. Combine this with a requester that asks for the missing member only when exactly one source from a preferred pair is held. This reproduces the highest-weight learned component across all 64 inventory pairs on the reachable questions. To compare whole mixtures, enumerate both desk orientations and all field permutations of both rules. Add the resulting 24 candidates to the original 20. Optimize the mixtures on the announced data and score both archived language banks with the same weights. Relative to the expanded script, the learned mixture trails by 0.029–0.126 points on the announced rubric and by 0.016–0.066 on the August 2020 written-vote release records. On the climate release records its margin ranges from −0.015 to +0.048. The earlier advantage depended heavily on which scripts the comparison included. This is a retrospective comparator audit on exposed cases. [Exact equality and mixture certificates](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-fallback/scripted-audit.json)

Separate routing from prose. The selector reads the question, table and annex identifiers. It returns a local ID under a 16-token cap. The writer receives only that source with an 80-token cap. Across the seven replies this preserves every routing choice and removes the observed cross-annex attribution. The full reply caps match, and the split uses two calls per reply with one shared draft per distinct source in this pilot. The isolated India note still omits the consensus-based qualifier. Keep routing accuracy, note integrity and final-record coverage as separate measures. No new end-to-end chair score was produced for these worlds. [Factored replies](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-factored-reply/factored-summary.json) · [Exact attribution review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-fallback/review.json)

For the stronger benchmark, freeze the expanded scripted control before evaluation, including field ordering and request-dependent backups. A held-out case should have more than one plausible backup and distinct conditions in which different backups are source-relevant. Verify a separation between the best allowed script and the attainable interactive score under the same information and costs before running writers. Then require that the additional evidence appear in a source-faithful final record alongside the procedural outcome and dissent. [Protocol and reproduction](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/evidence/climate2021-live-fallback/README.md)

### Stress-test a request-dependent backup on a new Council docket

Use the 18 October 2023 Council meeting as one event family. UNBench contains Brazil’s S/2023/773 and both Russian amendments. UNSC-Bench matches all 15 votes on the main draft. Verify the two amendment tallies against the official meeting. Keep the 6–1–8 and 7–1–7 failures to reach the vote threshold distinct from the 12–1–2 main-draft veto. Four focal slips identify the record section to reconstruct. The responder holds two of four substantive backups. The requester can send the same generic certified-file question or remain silent. The certified file is always unavailable. The question can still change which backup the responder supplies. Historical sources are real and the evidence distribution is simulated. [Task and accession](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/README.md) · [Official meeting](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/4025062/files/S_PV.9442-EN.pdf)

Enumerate all 65,536 joint deterministic policies. The strongest rank-script control jointly optimizes any of 16 requester policies and two independently chosen priority orders, using the true prior. An unrestricted Bayesian script reaches the same ceiling as exact interaction. Three deliberately selected correlated priors produce gaps of 0.573–0.722 points over the best priority script. The uniform prior and six unselected draws tie that script. Across all ten distributions, exact interaction exceeds optimized silent delivery by 2.165–4.751 points. The designed cases diagnose a limitation of priority orders under correlated availability. The unselected results keep the prevalence of that limitation visible. [Exact bounds and baselines](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/routing-results.json)

Fit two local policies with the expected shared evidence reward for 96 CPU updates on one named correlated prior. The trained pair scores 55.746 from an exact ceiling of 55.781 against 55.058 for optimized priorities. It gives up 4.912 points when the request is erased. The trained pair beats exact scripts frozen from the same development prior on four of nine out-of-training priors when the script is forced to use priority orders, and falls behind the prior-informed rank comparator on eight of nine. The exact and fitted pairs use opposite signal meanings. Crossing the partners on the training prior scores 43.989 and 46.086. A known-codebook alignment of receiver labels raises those scores to 55.514 and 55.781. That alignment leaves the channel’s physical erasure mapped to silence. [Fit and transfer scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/routing-fit.json) · [Partner cross-play](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/crossplay-results.json)

Use frozen Qwen3.5-4B to generate eight final-record supplements, one per focal slip with and without its matching backup. The chair exposes only matched evidence, and the policies all replay the same outputs. Same-assistant exact-span review confirms two guard failures and two uncertain records. Without its backup, the S/2023/775 supplement incorrectly adds a veto effect. With its backup, the U.S. explanation reports the supplied rationale accurately and then falsely claims exclusivity and lack of abstention explanations. S/2023/776 wording is ambiguous about a proposed versus implemented insertion or threshold versus veto. The humanitarian access supplement drops implementing partners and other aid-delivery initiatives. [Exact writer transcripts and labels](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/writer-evidence/annotations.json) · [Case-specific judge instructions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/judge.md)

The learned route exceeds the frozen rank script on strict release in all ten priors by 1.146–6.602 points. This margin largely reflects writer errors. Routing the veto explanation less often avoids a bad supplemented record, and routing S/2023/775 more often avoids a bad slip-only record. With two exact manual deletions of the unsupported sentences, preserving every already credited span and leaving both uncertain rows untouched, the margin ranges from −0.972 to +1.102 and is positive in five of ten priors. The raw margin is also positive in five of ten. Keep this release score out of the training reward until an independent guard audit and source-preserving writer both pass. [Original final-record scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/writer-evidence/scores.json) · [Per-focus decomposition and deletion sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/writer-evidence/release-audit.json)

### Separate prior uncertainty from final-record failures

Keep the Gaza evidence, eight receipt conditions, Qwen3.5-4B parameters, caps, and routing tables fixed. Replace only the final writer's system prompt. Require the writer to use public text for references, report the focal disposition from its own evidence, keep proposed language conditional, and avoid claims of exclusive national explanations. The writer removes the invented veto effect from the S/2023/775 slip-only output and removes the unsupported claims of exclusive rationale and unexplained abstentions from the matched U.S. output. It then assigns the first amendment tally to the main draft, invents a U.S. rationale with no annex, and introduces a nonexistent amendment referring to Article 51. Strict eligibility declines from four of eight to three of eight. The same-assistant exact-span review shows why a more restrictive instruction does not stabilize the release reward. [Paired scoped bank and scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/scoped-evidence/scores.json)

Render a separate extractive control from the same delivered evidence. Fixed docket labels identify the author and instrument. Slip statements and matching backup text retain their wording. All eight records clear the guard in same-assistant review. The aid record earns 63 instead of 70 because its explicit ceasefire comparison is missing from the packet. Recalculate the flexible and rank-policy ceilings using these attainable receipt scores. On the exposed correlated priors the flexible advantage is 0.476–0.722 points. It remains zero on uniform and all six unselected priors. The fitted route beats the frozen rank script on five of ten priors, with margins from −1.018 to +1.185. This controls prose contamination at the cost of docket-specific labels. [Rendered records, proofs and calibrated routing ceilings](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/compiled-results.json)

Give both routing methods the same four development distributions and withhold the realized prior at execution. Optimize a public mixture over 65,536 flexible policies or 9,216 priority policies using worst regret to the prior-informed interaction ceiling. Independently verify each primal and dual certificate. Both methods select the same two-component mixture. Their worst development regret is 1.264 points and their scores tie on every unselected distribution. On this docket a larger prior-blind policy search yields no advantage over the stronger scripted control. Prior-specific headroom remains measurable when the actual distribution is known. [Prior-blind mixture certificates](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/prior-blind-results.json)

Calibrate a blinded frozen Qwen3.5-4B guard on 16 generated records and four authored controls, with clear and failed codes swapped for every candidate. All 40 calls use a valid code. The model judges all four authored controls correctly in both orders but agrees with the same-assistant reference in both orders on only eight of sixteen generated records. Two verdicts change with the code assignment. Even when release requires both orders to return clear, the judge releases two of five reference failures. Both concern the U.S. explanation. It also clears three of four uncertain records. Authored probes alone overstate this judge’s ability to gate real outputs. [All judgments and reference labels](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/guard-evidence/results.json)

### Recover cross-play through public calibration

Keep the Gaza router channel, compiled record scores and losses fixed. A public probe names a dummy focal slip. The unfamiliar requester uses its usual send-or-silence policy for that slip. The receiver sees only the delivered result, including possible erasure. One probe costs 0.05 points and an attempted request costs a further 0.05. Each responder uses the mean of four development priors. All 16 deterministic requester schedules are tested. The exact probe policy may stop early or make up to six probes. Compare a frozen learned receiver, the best blind decoder, a fixed four-probe deck, calibration that can only flip the two existing label meanings, adaptive calibration with a flexible decoder, and an oracle given the partner's schedule. [Protocol and costs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/calibration-results.json)

On the development availability prior, at eight missions per calibration and averaging the 14 schedules other than the two archived senders, the frozen receiver scores 50.188 and the blind decoder 51.004. Four fixed probes raise this to 52.476. Adaptive probes raise it to 52.705 using 5.834 probes on average, below the known-schedule oracle's 52.789 by 0.084 points in the mean and 0.118 in the worst case. A simple label flip fixes much of the original opposite-codebook example but scores 51.831 across the broader schedule set. Calibration slightly reduces the already matched learned pair's net score. Charge overhead and report the familiar and unfamiliar partners separately.

Train two receivers on the same fixed four-probe deck with the exact shared record reward. Use 96 CPU updates per fit and the same eight even-parity schedules. Exclude every odd-parity schedule, including the two archived partners. A generic network scores 51.073 over the eight excluded schedules on the development prior, 1.592 below exact decoding with the same probes. A factored receiver scores a candidate annex using its own probe outcome, the incoming mission request and a learned background score for that annex and inventory. It recovers 52.664 and agrees with all 192 exact decoding actions. The archived replay confirms exact score agreement for each excluded partner across all ten availability priors. Rotating the topic labels of the delivered probe outcomes while preserving their count, loss and fees costs 2.850–3.729 points across those priors. This measures learned decoder interoperability in one finite replay. The symbolic comparison ties, the calibration prompts are simulated and the records use the compiled source control. [Generic receiver](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/learned-calibration-results.json) · [Factored receiver](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/factored-calibration-results.json) · [Probe-binding replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/gaza2023/calibration-behavior-results.json)

### Transfer calibrated cross-play to the Jerusalem vote

Accession Egypt's S/2017/1060 and all 15 votes from the pinned UNBench and UNSC-Bench releases. The 18 December 2017 record distinguishes the failed draft from earlier Council resolutions and preserves the role of the United States as the sole negative vote. Build four evidence focuses for operative paragraph 1 and the U.S., UK and Swedish explanations. The docket did not enter the Gaza receiver fit. It shares the same Council agenda and the routing simulation retains its earlier channel and ten inventory priors. [Accession, cases and reproduction](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/README.md) · [Official meeting](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/1327435/files/S_PV.8139-EN.pdf)

Pair the receiver with two frozen Qwen3.5-4B requesters. One is asked to request a draft provision, the other a national explanation. Give each a public probe and a differently worded mission prompt for each focus. All 16 outputs use the permitted question or PASS. The draft desk keeps its schedule. The statement desk asks for all three statements in calibration and drops every request in the mission. The receiver pays for calibration on a schedule the requester drops during the mission. The compiled record score is 49.801 versus 50.869 for the receiver without probes. The typed forecast for the intended statement schedule was 52.753. [Original language actions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/language-evidence/actions.json) · [Measured language replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/language-transfer.json)

Classify the requested source as draft or national statement in a separate model call. Apply the declared desk rule to the observed category. All eight categories match the focus across probe and mission, recovering both intended schedules. Receivers still get only the request bit. Replay the frozen receiver against exact decoding with the same development prior and new announced scores. In the compiled bank the frozen calibrated receiver leads its uncalibrated predecessor in 216 of 240 prior-and-slot conditions for the draft desk and 186 of 240 for the statement desk. Exact decoding under the new rubric changes 32 of 192 decisions because the third slot now has a 28-point backup instead of 21. Across the slot assignments the frozen calibrated receiver sometimes leads and sometimes trails that exact decoder. The 240 conditions per desk represent one simulated docket. [Role classification](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/factored-evidence/actions.json) · [Policy and reward-shift comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/comparison.json)

Generate a new bank of eight receipt-conditioned records with the original frozen writer. Six pass strict release in same-assistant exact-span review. The draft-only record blurs Egypt's submitting role, and the matched draft record says proposed wording mandated action. Both are marked uncertain. The U.S. and UK matched records earn all five focal criteria. The Swedish record leaves the effect on earlier resolutions implicit. In canonical order under strict release the calibrated receiver trails its uncalibrated predecessor for the draft desk by 0.443 points. It leads by 1.257 for the statement desk and trails the exact decoder by 0.505. These replays use the policies selected against the announced values and apply generated record scores afterward. [Generated records and review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/record-evidence/scores.json)

### Give the calibrated receiver the current rubric

Fit a receiver that adds the log of the announced marginal source credit to its source-local evidence score. Use 96 CPU updates with the joint team reward, eight simulated Gaza requester schedules, and the 24 permutations of the four synthetic weights 7, 14, 21 and 28. The Jerusalem uniform reward and five other evaluation vectors are excluded. It recovers all 192 exact Jerusalem actions, up from 160 for the original frozen decoder. On the unchanged Jerusalem generated evidence bank, exact agreement holds after admission and reranking across all 24 topic orders and ten priors. In canonical order on the development prior, strict release improves from 39.232 to 39.806 for the draft desk and from 38.829 to 39.334 for the statement desk. Those scores beat their respective uncalibrated receivers by 0.131 and 1.762. This setting-specific improvement does not hold in every coordinate. The fitted receiver recovers 176 of 192 decisions on the original Gaza weights and 160 on a separate unequal-weight case. [Reward-conditioned fit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/reward-receiver.json) · [Unchanged Jerusalem record replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/reward-replay.json)

Estimate the inventory-conditioned focus distribution and shared probe-to-request likelihood from one 4,096-episode simulated training sample. Multiply the two estimates by the public rubric weight at execution. On the six declared vectors, replacing only the estimated channel with its known value leaves every measured disagreement count unchanged. Using the declared mean development inventory distribution removes all those disagreements even with the estimated channel. The declared prior is available to the earlier exact controls and differs from the evaluation priors. This identifies the important finite-sample error in this run. [Estimation and factor swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/probability-audit.json)

For 512 unselected public rubrics, compare the frozen Gaza decoder, the team-reward fit, the two-factor estimated model, and the model that carries the declared development inventory prior but estimates the signal likelihood. Expected missed team credit against the same-information exact decoder averages 0.539, 0.070, 0.0032 and 0.000014 points respectively, on the development mixture and excluded odd-parity partners. The last variant agrees on 98,212 of 98,304 local decisions and its worst expected regret is below 0.001 points. This quantified comparison favors the probability-based model for this small known protocol. It uses centralized labels during sample collection and a synthetic availability model. Its numeric stability does not validate prose generation, independent judging or a live dialogue policy on an unseen event family. [All 512 weight profiles and results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/reward-stress-results.json)

### Run the calibrated receiver with fresh notes and chair records

Run four preselected Jerusalem worlds with intact delivery in both arms. Rerun all eight public-probe and private-mission classifications, then use either the receiver with the estimated channel and the declared development inventory distribution or the original uncalibrated receiver. Give each responder only its selected archival source for a fresh 65-word note. The chair gets the fixed public paragraph, focal slip and the note only if the task ID matches and the note passes the format gate. Both arms use identical limits and one responder and chair call per case. They share the classifications. This is a live sequence of fresh outputs under a typed routing and admission protocol, with intact delivery and no estimated frequency claim. [Frozen live plan](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/live-plan.json) · [Paired records, messages and source spans](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/live-evidence/scores.json)

All eight source classifications match the intended protocol and all eight sent notes pass the syntax and length gate. The calibrated receiver selects the matching source in three cases and the uncalibrated receiver in one. The U.S. and UK cases present the same inventory and silence to the responder. Each policy correctly serves one of those two cases. The exact blind decoder shares the calibrated choice for that pair. Calibration changes both positive-request cases relative to the exact blind decoder and agrees with the known-sender comparator on all four. Reuse the observed record for each counterfactual selection as both legal source choices were tested. Label the both-inventories control as a routing ceiling. Two fresh notes contain a date placeholder, and a note about the U.S. position that was blocked as unrelated also calls it the source of the draft proposal. Those notes passed the format gate, so keep source fidelity distinct from admission.

The paired raw margins are +28 for the U.S. focus, −28 for the UK focus, +21 for Sweden and +21 for the draft. The UK record preserves all five criteria but uses 87 words and fails the 85-word release cap. This reverses its strict score comparison. Rerun only the chair calls, removing the fixed public paragraph from the prompt while retaining it in the final assembly. The same UK evidence now yields a 74-word fully credited record. All four matched receipts pass and score 70 in the new run. Among the four slip-only receipts, the writer gives the U.S. explanation unsupported exclusivity and assigns a Council draft's rejection to the General Assembly. A Swedish record is uncertain about its language on adoption. Both reruns show the sensitivity of the release margin to language failures. Record the matching-source result and raw coverage separately, and retain all failed outputs. [Fixed-message writer intervention](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/slip-evidence/scores.json)

The live score artifacts show the request, the actual note and the unedited supplement. The per-case ledger counts peer turns and distinct rubric ideas in the note, delivered to the chair, and retained in the supplement. The estimates use same-assistant semantic review with mechanically checked spans. A blinded packet strips the arm labels and holds the key separately for independent review. It is a prepared handoff with no independent verdict yet. [Packets](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/live-evidence/review-packets.json) · [Reviewer instructions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/live-evidence/reviewer-instructions.md)

### Select supported passages instead of rewriting the chair record

Use the same live Jerusalem notes and admission decisions. The public record and focal slip are attached unchanged. Split each matching note into numbered sentences. Remove the literal leading `On [date], ` placeholder when present, without filling in a date. The four matching notes go to frozen Qwen3.5-4B with their original archival excerpt. It selects sentence IDs under a 12-token cap. Copy only the selected substrings after the focal slip. When no matching note reaches the chair, the system emits the focal slip directly. The archival excerpt lets the selector check the note, and cannot add text the sender did not transmit. [Extractive records and traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/extractive-evidence/scores.json)

All four genuine matching notes retain all four additional criteria and release at 70 points. All four slip-only records release at 42. This preserves the matching-source benefit without rewarding a receiver for sending the chair less information when a free-form output would fail. Four authored contrasts alter one sentence each to claim a settled status, an embassy relocation, revocation of earlier resolutions, or adoption of the draft. The selector excludes all four altered sentences. Surrounding supported material can still be selected. The four positive notes and four negative contrasts are same-docket evidence. Grounding is separately checked against the archival excerpts and the exact reviewed transport spans. This does not show the selector will catch realistic errors in another docket.

Apply the unchanged selector to eight matched receipts from the older Gaza writer banks. These inputs contain actual model-generated errors. The selector removes the unsupported sole-rationale sentence and preserves the U.S. explanation at 70 points in one case. It also retains an invented amendment and two ambiguous scope statements. One generated selector list ends with a comma and falls back to the slip. Two otherwise clear assemblies repeat the tally and exceed the word cap after the fixed slip is attached. Only three of eight assemblies pass strict release, including the fallback. The authored Jerusalem foils gave a better result than these older generated cases. Keep exact copying and semantic acceptance as separate requirements. [Cross-docket selector outputs and source review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/extractive-transfer-evidence/scores.json)

Replay the same four live Jerusalem choices using exact approved archival passages. Each source fits within 65 words and each assembly fits within 85. Keep the protected slip and verify the source's membership, topic and bytes against a pinned registry. The four matched records receive the extractive control's 70 points and the remaining four receive 42. All 20 rejected payload trials are caught across four cases, including unrelated, unavailable, modified, rehashed and relabelled passages. This is a deterministic transport control on the same choices, with no new live model outputs. It gives a source-preserving record comparison when trusted passages fit the budget. Richer paraphrase still requires independent fidelity evidence. [Verified archival relay control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/verified-relay-results.json)

### Enumerate verified relays with loss and phase drift

Extend the four live Jerusalem worlds to every focal slip, two-source inventory and legal action. Verify all 48 receipt choices against the archival registry and both word caps before scoring. Replay the measured stable draft and statement desk schedules over the ten synthetic availability priors and 24 topic assignments, with independent 10% probe, request and reply erasure. Charge the four-probe setup over one, eight and 32 missions. Both calibrated and exact no-probe receivers see the same delivered mission bit. The no-probe control knows the development inventory prior and an equal mixture over all 16 possible sender schedules. It gets no probe outcomes. [Full physical receipts, scores and costs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/relay-population-results.json)

At eight missions, the stable calibrated receiver beats exact no-probe in all 480 coordinates. The mean gains are 2.642 for the draft desk and 2.568 for the statement desk. It ties exact calibration in every coordinate. It beats the silent pair that knows each evaluation prior in 160 of 240 draft-desk and 147 of 240 statement-desk coordinates. Erasing mission requests while leaving the probe results and sending fees unchanged lowers every stable result. A separate script given the two possible stable schedules can use one probe. On average it leads the general four-probe receiver by 0.022 and 0.034 points, with wins and losses for both desks across the topic assignments. The controls share the same delivery and cost model. The one-probe script receives the stated sender-family information. The coordinates measure sensitivity within one docket. [One-probe scripted comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/two-family-results.json)

The original direct language desk changes from requests for all three statements to silence in the mission. Calibrating on its earlier probe profile loses 0.351 points on average. Generate another four public probes using new mission-style wording. The same direct policy passes on all four. This predicts the observed mission silence but just recovers the no-probe decoder's behavior, with a remaining 0.025-point amortized cost. On the same new wording the factored classifier gives the expected categories on all four prompts. Using that speaker's stable mission profile restores the 2.568-point mean gain. Keep semantic classification, channel loss and policy stability visible as separate controls. [Fresh phase probes and replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/jerusalem2017/phase-probe-evidence/results.json)

### Transfer to corroborating evidence in the 2020 written vote

Use the separate S/2020/852 written vote on foreign terrorist fighters. Retain the pinned UNBench draft and all 15 UNSC-Bench votes. Use the original five private criteria. Split Indonesia's national statement and the Council President's procedural transmission into separate archived passages. Russia's and the U.S. explanations together substantiate both the common repatriation objection and the divergent votes. A second copy of a held source adds no coverage. The verified assembly now has a non-diagonal decision payoff and reaches at most 63 points with two sources. Every possible supplement fits under 85 words. [Source accession and corroborating record bank](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/corroboration-records.json)

Transfer the frozen calibrated likelihood and supply the new source-pair reward matrix. Hold all protocol costs fixed. For the intended schedules this ties exact decoding in all 480 prior-and-placement conditions and beats the old match-only receiver in 478. The receivers share the same development prior and delivered mission bit. The four paid probes add mean gains of 0.149 for the national desk and 0.167 for the procedural desk. This is much smaller than the earlier Jerusalem relay gain. The prior-informed silent pair wins most coordinates against the calibrated receiver. The fresh Qwen source classifier treats both presidential transmission prompts as national, giving six correct classifications out of eight and constant sender schedules. With those measured bits, calibration loses 0.050 and 0.025 points on average against exact no-probe. [Intended schedule preflight](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/corroboration-preflight.json) · [Measured language schedules](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/role-evidence/actions.json) · [Measured replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/corroboration-measured.json)

Fit the requester and responder jointly for 96 CPU updates on the existing mean development prior with the verified record score as team reward. The agents read their local authenticated card or inventory and the delivered request bit. The learned pair's greedy score is 45.406, against 45.084 for optimized blind silence and 45.697 for the exact pair using the same development prior. Erasing the request reduces the learned score to 44.692. Neither a new requester table alone nor a new responder table alone raises the learned score. Change one focal request and one corresponding responder action together. A single accepted pair of changes lifts the score to 45.658. It raises scores on all six unselected priors by 0.020–0.244 points. The fitted and repaired pair loses to the exact design rule on some priors. This is centralized model-based refinement over a small known protocol. The language classifier error is kept separate. [Joint fit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/joint-fit-results.json) · [Paired policy refinement](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/joint-repair-results.json)

Execute both policies through 192 explicit loss branches, using the actual verified excerpts and record text. With the presidential card already at the chair and both Indonesia cards in the responder's inventory, the first fit delivers a duplicate. The repaired policy's intact request and reply deliver the national statement. The record can then distinguish the two capacities and rises from 35 to 42 points. Losing either packet removes the extra criterion. The scalar execution reproduces the expected-score implementation on every prior. [Executed written-vote protocol](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/written-vote-protocol-results.json)

### Route written-vote language through a separate scope check

Freeze the repaired team policy and the authenticated passage registry. First ask frozen Qwen3.5-4B to retrieve one archival card from four role-specific descriptions and an ambiguous option. Check two permutations of the answer labels. This recovers both mission prompts for Djani's two roles and all four fresh role paraphrases. Three determinate lookups vary across the two orders. Both underspecified prompts get a stable but unwarranted card. Agreement between label orders is alone insufficient for a safe dispatch. [Initial role lookup and labels](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/lookup-evidence/results.json)

Run a separate scope check on assignment text alone. It identifies all 18 authored cases, including four underspecified requests. Then run both label orders of a four-card lookup for the 12 determinate routing examples that pass this check. All 12 select the correct card in both orders and both underspecified routing examples abstain. The four extra scope controls test only the first stage. The model has authentic card descriptions but no answer label from the simulation. These are same-docket development examples. [Scope judgments](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/scope-evidence/results.json) · [Gated archive lookup](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/scoped-lookup-evidence/results.json)

Use the measured archive identities to dispatch the existing repaired protocol. A lookup must identify a source actually held by the requester. For every admitted assignment, evaluate six responder inventories, request loss or delivery, and reply loss or delivery. All 288 emitted branches match the typed reference, and both underspecified requests produce clarification with neither a request nor a record. The expected mission score remains 45.658 on the design mixture. In the same-person contrast, the national statement sends no request, while the procedural transmission sends a request that obtains the national excerpt. Both records keep the national rationale distinct from reporting the vote. This tests language lookup followed by typed communication and assembly. It keeps the role prompts, simulator loss, and archival prose boundaries visible. [Language-dispatched record and branch traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/language-dispatch-results.json)

Apply the unchanged scope prompt to ten additional same-docket requests. Five are correctly classified. The scope check accepts explicit requests for both Djani records and for both national vote explanations, even though this runtime accepts one focal source. It also accepts a vague question about what Djani said about the result. It incorrectly rejects two single-record formulations. Run these only as scope judgments. They never enter the measured dispatch panel. The natural-language interface still needs a reliable distinction between one requested record, several requested records and an unspecified request. [Fresh scope challenge](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/scope-reserve-evidence/results.json)

### Separate record selection from request operators

Test one required record, every listed record, any listed alternative, and an unspecified request. Give frozen Qwen3.5-4B the same four role cards under two label orders. A compact set decoder recovers all eight single-record requests but achieves exact agreement on 12 of 20 full intents. It returns a single record for two vague requests. A second decoder uses stable semantic card identifiers and names the operator. It reaches only four exact intents on 24 cases, including three on the same 20. In particular, it frequently reads a conjunction as an alternative. Preserve both traces and the gaps between card identity, operator, and wording. [Compact set outputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/intent-evidence/results.json) · [Semantic identifier outputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/semantic-intent-evidence/results.json)

Use a verified relay that can ask for explicit card IDs and can append at most one authenticated reply. Mark a conjunctive request complete only if every requested card is present. Mark an alternative complete when an allowed card is present. Clarify an unresolved prompt. Test all four protected anchor identities, all six responder inventories and both link losses. With known intent this satisfies 20.25% of the two-card requests in the uniform panel, where many worlds cannot supply both. The semantic decoder's all-to-any mistakes produce claimed completion with about 69.8% probability on the conjunctive subset while satisfying none of their full requirements. Typed assembly alone cannot repair a misread goal. [Scope-aware relay and completion audits](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/scoped-relay-results.json)

An independent operator check reads only the assignment, with two different label orders. It identifies 14 of 24 operators correctly in both calls. Withholding dispatch on disagreement removes the observed false completion for the semantic decoder in the conjunction and vague subsets. It leaves another false completion in the compact decoder's vague subset, roughly half the original rate. It also cuts valid completion for the compact decoder's conjunctions from 12.15% to 4.05%. The check trades recall for fewer unsupported completions and does not recover a reliable open language policy. [Operator outputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/operator-evidence/results.json) · [Gated dispatch replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/operator-replay-results.json)

For a separate matched-reward test, condition on the requester already holding one of the two required cards. The one missing card is private. Fit one one-bit requester and responder for 96 CPU updates on the development inventory mixture with a reward of 28 points for completing the request and the established link fees. The learned pair matches the exact fixed one-bit script on all evaluated priors. On the design mixture it completes 40.59%, versus 28.37% for optimized silence. An explicit named request completes 43.58%. The named message uses a larger alphabet and sends no reply when the request is lost or the card is unavailable. With some shifted priors the one-bit policy can gain from its fallback when no request arrives. With neither required card protected, completion requires at least two replies and is infeasible here. This keeps intent inference, bandwidth, and optimized coordination as separate empirical questions. [Equal-reward routing fit and named control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/target-bandwidth-results.json)

### Ground requested scope in quoted action phrases

Ask frozen Qwen3.5-4B to quote the words naming each requested record. Check literal, contiguous spans and their local actor and action vocabulary. Resolve explicit conjunctions, alternatives, exclusions and unspecified requests before the verified relay. The model and validator get 13 of 32 full intents exactly right, with no wrong dispatch on these measured prompts. Against the previous letter decoder on their shared 20 prompts, agreement drops from 12 to seven while both of the letter decoder's false single-record selections are withheld. Over the full panel, only about 18.5% of singleton requests and 16.7% of alternatives resolve after delivery loss. Treat withheld determinate requests as missing credit. [Quoted actions and decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/witness-evidence/results.json) · [Loss replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/witness-replay-results.json)

Lexical guards cannot establish scope from substring membership alone. Deliberately supply quotes from explicit negation or from a record already held. The original check admits both. Add checks for explicit pre-object exclusion and possession. They catch those two failures but still miss the selected object followed by a phrase saying it is unnecessary or already held. Adding those specific post-object patterns passes all 12 authored quote controls without increasing deployment recall. On ten measured failures, exact spans written by the reviewer recover six. The other four still fail because the grammar does not resolve shared wording or a role phrase. These substitutions isolate bottlenecks but do not represent independent adjudication or model outputs. [Context checks and manual witness substitution](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/witness-context-results.json)

Build an explicit clarification handoff that leaves the language route first. It requests a confirmed operator and validated archive card IDs only when no grounded dispatch exists. It requires distinct approved IDs and preserves larger conjunctions as constraints. A request for three required cards remains incomplete under a one-reply limit. Across the 32 measured prompts, 23 route to clarification. Replay the 16 determinate clarifications with authored typed answers, and keep the other seven unresolved. Under those simulated answers the policy reaches the same completion probabilities as known intent, at the cost of one additional confirmation on each of the 16 cases. This is executable coverage of the clarification path and an upper test on supplied confirmations. Real users' responses and open-language recall remain unmeasured. [Clarification contract, invalid reply tests and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/explicit-resolution-results.json)

### Give the chair a third contact and match the stopping rule

On the Indonesia written-vote docket, reuse the four source-checked cards and give two archive desks synthetic, private two-card rosters. The chair starts with one of three required cards and can request a verified missing card from either desk. Compare an optimized fixed contact order with optimized receipt-dependent routing. Both can stop on the same evidence, incur the same message costs and use the same verified compiler. Score confirmed scope completion and exact-span final-record quality separately. The same transport becomes a different task when a faithful partial record earns credit.

With two contacts, both routing policies tie under either objective. With three contacts and the 70-point final-record objective less message cost, exact adaptive routing raises complete scope from 30.36% to 33.47% and the final record from 48.54 to 48.84. The same completion gain is 8.68 percentage points on complementary desks and zero on identical desks. The result is an exact expectation over synthetic rosters and link failures. Under a completion-only reward, the adaptive policy instead reaches 42.66% completion and 47.89 final-record points. This is a concrete calibration case for reward design before applying an LLM judge.

A tabular policy trained jointly for the chair and shared desk policy reaches 33.39% completion and 48.82 final-record points. Swapping in the canonical fixed response rule reduces it to 30.37% and 48.51. The exact adaptive chair with that canonical rule still scores slightly higher than the jointly fitted pair. Use the policy swap to identify dependence on learned replies and the strong scripted chair to test whether learning adds value beyond a designed protocol. All policies were compared on the same source docket. For a broader benchmark, preserve this controlled transport diagnostic and measure free-form discussion gains on disjoint UN event families using the final-record rubric and the resource-matched solo and independent-drafts conditions.

Frozen Qwen selected the intended option on 8/12 authored clarification replies in two label orders. It resolved 4/7 actionable replies, and none of the remaining five examples reached a dispatchable action. The other actionable replies include both single-record requests and a self-correction. Continue treating typed confirmation as a separate ingress requirement in the transport experiment. [Policies, exact replay, and raw clarification traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/README.md#receipt-dependent-routing)

### Test source-faithful discussion when the recorded vote is unanimous

The Iraq mandate-renewal case joins UNBench author-test event 2484 to UNSC-Bench adopted row 256 with all 15 votes matched. The archived meeting contains unanimous adoption and no national explanations of vote. The resolution sets distinct mandate and review deadlines and a separate four-month reporting requirement. This complements the veto and abstention cases. A good record preserves the documented coordinates of agreement while leaving the individual reasons for voting unassigned. The preceding fictional workshop exposed the docket, so the new matched run is a fresh task on an existing source family. [Case, protocol and scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/README.md)

Use two analyst desks with disjoint original source summaries, shared openings between the team arms, and the same full packet for every final writer. Five calls and a 688-token output ceiling per arm gave the pooled solo 28/70 and both teams 35/70 in same-assistant exact-span review. All fail the factual guard by changing consideration of civil-society and youth input into an explicit full participation requirement. The interactive final also hits its token cap. The interactive chair introduces the draft symbol directly from its own dossier. The traced revisions show no peer-specific uptake in a bounded source-idea inventory. Keep the placement of each fact visible alongside the total points. [Raw records and source-idea trace](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/evidence/process-results.json)

The two teams remain tied at 49/70 in a retrospective partial-credit check, with the pooled analyst at 45.5/70. The same participation guard fails in every arm. The two-order Qwen judge gets 17/20 authored checks, then labels all 30 actual record criteria supported in both orders. The exact-span review supports 14, with 13 omissions and three contradictions. Use source-linked negative examples drawn from actual generated text, including short partial clauses and fluent scope errors, when calibrating any judge for an RL reward. The current judge cannot distinguish these submissions. [Judge logits and classifications](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/judge-evidence/results.json)

### Require candidate evidence before comparing with the source

Separate what the candidate asserts from whether it matches the archive. Give the same frozen judge exact candidate windows and alternative field values in two orders, then map the selected value to the source criterion offline. On 30 generated Iraq criteria, stable correctness rises from 14 to 27. The new judge catches all three participation scope errors. It still credits two incomplete records, including seven points that would rank independent drafts above the interactive record even though they tie in reviewed scores. On an expanded authored panel it grants false credit to a review addressed to Iraq instead of the Council. [Witness judgments and reward distortion](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/witness-evidence/reward-audit.json)

A narrow prerequisite can direct those three false credits to review, but its controls expose an accepted counterfactual and a rejected consultation paraphrase. Keep it out of automatic MARL reward. Judge prompt compression needs its own audit. Two ranked sentences retain 17 of 17 known Iraq witnesses and 58 of 59 in Western Sahara. The lost span is a material attribution error. Three sentences retain all 76 known witnesses in these archived outputs. Future judge tests should retain contradictory witness recall, include actual generated negatives, and hold out entire event families before scoring an adaptive policy with the judge. [Gate controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/witness-evidence/gate-results.json) · [Retrieval replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/evidence/retrieval-audit.json)

### Align source slots by what an additional receipt can prove

Transfer the frozen written-vote chair and desk policy to the retrospective UNAMI and UNIFIL source records, with the same three-contact limit and synthetic loss. Both targets have four optional source cards and a protected local card from a confirmed set of three. Replan exact fixed and adaptive scripts for each target. Across all aliases, direct frozen transfer averages 12.680 and 16.366 net points above the corresponding protected-anchor records. Map each case to a written-vote view using the public gains from either single receipt and from both. With frozen weights this gives 13.671 and 17.693, ahead of the best fixed routes at 13.508 and 17.562. The best adaptive scripts still lead at 13.885 and 18.114. The result uses authored evidence-linked records and one shared input mapping. The two source families are excluded from the fitted policy and already exposed elsewhere in the project. [Transfer, alias controls and exact replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/README.md#transfer-by-public-receipt-value)

Audit the score of every verified subset before fitting. The 85-word written-vote supplement loses 14 points when the national statement is added to the U.S. and Russia pair. The ceiling allows the required new statement but drops the supported comparison. A 92-word counterfactual restores monotonic coverage. A single fit on the counterfactual leaves UNAMI transfer essentially unchanged and lowers UNIFIL transfer by about 0.11 points. A mapping-only control accounts for nearly all of that shift. Retain the original word-budget endpoint and report the tradeoff between scope completion and preservation of earlier criteria. [Counterfactual and one refit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/ftf2020/adaptive-cap-fit-results.json)

### Check whether routed evidence survives a frozen final writer

Generate an Iraq UNAMI supplement for every reachable nonempty receipt set with the frozen Qwen checkpoint. Use the same authored public paragraph and 106-word supplement cap for the learned route, scripted routes and pooled writer. Keep policies and the scripted route choices frozen on authored scores before writing. Exact-span review confirms all licensed facts across the 14 generated supplements. The aligned MARL route yields 52.61/70 actual record points, above the fixed script at 52.43 and below the adaptive script at 52.82. The full-target solo has all three requested cards and scores 59.50. The writer largely preserves exact source wording. [Recorded generations and policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/unami2023/README.md#replay-frozen-routes-through-the-final-writer)

Their receipt probabilities reduce pre-format release to 87.70% for the aligned route, 87.78% for fixed, and 88.43% for adaptive. A posthoc formatter preserves all non-citation words and repairs all three reviewed records. Keep pre-format results primary and score formatting, evidence acquisition and semantic source fidelity separately. ### Test national attribution and conditional authority on a fresh Colombia docket

Freeze the 30 October 2023 adoption of resolution 2704 as a separate event family. The UNBench author-test rows match the primary record for all 15 votes. The pinned UNSC-Bench release has no row for this docket, so this extends the union of the two sources. The case tests three different statements of authority. The Council renewed the existing mission, had previously authorized verification of the Government–ELN ceasefire, and expressed willingness to consider a Government–EMC role under stated conditions. Keep the A3 speaker's represented group and Brazil's national capacity separate from the Council's action. [Accession, case and protocol](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md)

At a matched 716-token output ceiling the pooled solo scores 14/70 and both teams score 21/70. Both teams reach 6/10 on key points and 6/10 on arguments, with no tracked peer-specific contribution in the interactive revision. All ten working records reach the cap and the solo final truncates. Both team finals reverse Ghana's relationship to the African members. This error first appears in synthesis. A higher quantity of authorized evidence and continued interaction cannot be measured as success when the final record reverses the speaker and represented delegation. [Transcript rubric and reviewed outputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/evidence/results.json)

Apply the Iraq source-blind excerpt judging procedure with new Colombia field menus written before inspecting the final responses. It passes four of seven authored checks and none of nine actual authority and role checks. It accepts both A3 speaker reversals and the authored foil that turns possible later consideration into authorization. Use explicit speaker and represented party fields and separate proposal, consideration, conditional authorization and current authority in benchmark proofs. A single pick over a fluent excerpt can still credit reversed relationships. [Judge replay and verdicts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/judge-evidence/results.json)

### Separate concise evidence notes from peer exposure

On the same Colombia docket, keep the 716-token ceiling, role sources and final prompt fixed. Replace the working instructions with 25–40 words, no headings, and explicit priority for who spoke for whom and with what qualification. Note completion rises from zero of ten to ten of ten. The solo gains 14 points, and the independent and interactive arms gain seven each. All tie at 28/70 with the compact notes. Each correctly attributes the A3 statement to Ghana speaking for the African members. The independent Brazil note wrongly says Brazil spoke for Colombia, and its chair repeats the error. The team chairs receive identical A3 revisions, and their only differing input is the Brazil note. No tracked idea is uniquely taken from a peer opening. All three compact records still fail the factual guard for overstated common ground or incorrect attribution. [Compact note control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#compact-notes-with-the-same-final-prompt)

In a chair-only counterfactual, reverse just the Ghana–A3 relation in the compact interactive note and leave the official source correct. The chair copies the reversal and truncates. The raw 28/70 stays flat because an Ethnic Chapter criterion replaces the lost attribution criterion. Without either working note, the same chair finishes at 35/70, correctly attributes the A3 statement and still overstates the conditions shared with Brazil. Use a full-dossier single-chair control alongside the budget-matched pooled analyst. Keep material attribution errors and completion outside any compensatory sum. [Receipt of a reversed note and no-note control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/intervention-evidence/results.json)

### Separate documentary bindings from provisional claims

Against the same reversed Ghana–A3 note, strengthening the source-priority instruction alone still yields the reversal at 35/70. Supplying a source-linked table of speaker and represented party corrects the reversal at the same score. The chair also preserves both national attributions when both provisional notes contain errors. It still merges distinct conditions into shared agreement. The clean ledger reaches 42/70 and clears the semantic guard, but truncates. Track attribution, unsupported consensus and completion independently. [Matched chair interventions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/provenance-evidence/results.json)

Compile a separate authorized record bank on the same Colombia case. All 16 source subsets fit under the word cap and adding a verified card never lowers the score. Both national statements and the resolution's authority clause are needed for the shared conclusion. Under simulated holdings and three contacts, public payoff alignment gives the frozen written-vote MARL team 19.117 net points, against 18.606 for the best fixed schedule and 19.161 for the best adaptive script. Keep this transferred routing result separate from end-to-end release of prose. [Verified bank and exact transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/verified-transfer.json)

With both erroneous notes present, a shorter word target alone yields an incomplete 35/70 and repeats the Brazil error. Giving the decision, authority and each statement a separate place raises the score to 49/70 in 134 complete words. It preserves both speaker relationships and correctly limits the supported common ground, yet it says the decision was left to the Secretary-General's update and thereby loses the complete authority guard. A useful reward has to bind each factual prerequisite to its own legal stage and agency. [Matched short synthesis comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/structured-evidence/results.json)

### Stress-test learned coordination with specialized archive desks

The frozen written-vote team underperforms a fixed schedule on UNAMI, UNIFIL and Colombia when one desk is strongly more likely to hold the requested records. Fit one joint policy on the written-vote case with a specialist, then freeze it for transfer. On all three dockets it improves over the original frozen team with a correct role hint. A script that also optimizes its reply order closes the advantage on the first two dockets. The learned Colombia team reaches 23.333 net points against 23.142 for the stronger fixed script and 23.486 for the stronger adaptive script. Mislabeling the desk drops the learned team below the original policy, and the new policy loses some return in symmetric and split-specialty conditions. [Held-out-to-fit transfer and matched controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#specialist-desks-and-an-unreliable-desk-label)

Calibrate metadata-dependent policy selection separately from communication. Under strong specialization, the desk label needs about 75–76% accuracy to beat the original frozen team in these three dockets. A threshold of 70.49% derived only from the written-vote fit is too low on all three. The result uses exact simulated source holdings and source-verified assembly. It supplies a test of cross-agent dependence and deployment robustness without an open-text judge in the reward. [Reliability sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/specialist-hint-results.json)

Freeze the learned and scripted routes before replaying them through 14 actual Qwen-written Colombia supplements. Each receipt carries its own source summaries and, for the national statements, observed working notes. All routers share an authored roll call and the same writer output per delivered packet. The specialist with a correct desk label reaches 34.95/70, against 34.90 for the fixed script and 34.92 for the adaptive script. On expected releasable points the two scripts lead at 17.62 and 17.78 versus 17.52. Both A3 criteria are retained in every packet that carries the A3 statement. [All packets and composition control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#replay-the-specialist-team-through-a-generated-record)

Two source-specific retry prompts show why constraining scope and preserving facts need separate checks. The A3 singleton clears the release gate after dropping an invented authorization, but omits which ceasefire its support covered. The three-source retry still assigns the Secretary-General's condition to both delegations and drops further source detail. Both complete. A retrospective substitution of just these two outputs lowers the specialist's raw score by 3.41 and improves release by 0.54 percentage points. ### Compare training on unreliable hints with a calibrated adaptive script

One written-vote-only fit encounters a wrong desk hint a quarter of the time under the paid negative-receipt protocol. With an incorrect hint it improves over the previous learner by 0.21–0.31 net points across UNAMI, UNIFIL, Colombia and DRC. With a correct hint it loses 0.25–0.37. At 75% reliability the new fit trails the earlier learner on all four dockets, and a script that knows only the reliability and simulated roster distribution leads both. The DRC generated endpoint yields 16.170 for the new learner, 16.310 for the earlier learner and 16.718 for the calibrated script. Keep both pure conditions visible. On DRC, replacing only the chair's opening with the new fit removes about 0.075 net points at 75% reliability.
