These swaps use frozen policies and the same materials and protocol. [Component controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/evidence/receipt-hint-swaps.json)

### Check whether generated peer discussion adds a correction to the final record

Give every writer the same DRC Experts source and the earlier paragraph with two material omissions. Compare two-pass solo revision, independent mandate and reporting drafts, and a mandate-to-reporting peer exchange. Each uses two notes and the same final prompt and output ceiling. All three, plus the one-call direct revision, restore both missing facts and score 14/14. The interactive reporting desk repeats the incoming proposal for the possible further extension, while the independent reporter supplies the missing Council recipient. The independent notes cover two distinct corrections and the interactive notes cover one. The interactive final has no exclusive factual improvement over either matched arm or the direct revision. The direct revision uses 98 output tokens against 196–206 for the other conditions. This is a frozen-model test of a generated interaction on an exposed source card. It uses same-assistant exact-span review. [Matched final records, turns and idea ledger](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/deliberation-evidence/results.json)

The one-call direct output is 68 words against a 67-word instruction, while the multi-call outputs are 64–65 words. This is a factual-coverage tie with a direct-arm length failure. Crossing note visibility with an explicit instruction to respond shows that both reporters who see the mandate memo echo its correction. Both who lack the memo identify the Council recipient instead. The effect appears at the reporting note and the follow-up has no generated chair decision. The follow-up conditions use a separate generation batch. Count unique corrections and repetition alongside final recovery before rewarding a visible response as good deliberation. [Controlled reporter comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/deliberation-control-evidence/results.json)

### Hide the source passages from the chair and verify what actually reaches it

Run two matched cases with the actual DRC and UNAMI source passages separately visible to two desks. Both archives agree with the official roll calls for these events. Count this as a paired change in reporting, with no peer introduction credit for those facts. Five working notes hit their caps, and none of the eight final records meets the complete contract. The pooled UNAMI revision truncates and changes four-month reporting to quarterly. Keep partial coverage, evidence visibility, completion and contradictions separate. This uses frozen inference on two exposed events and same-assistant review. ### Give the reporting desk more room and add a verbatim relay

Raise the reporter cap from 62 to 104 tokens on DRC and UNAMI, with the original prompts, frozen first notes and same 112-token chair cap. All four reporter notes finish. DRC preserves both original 62-token prefixes. Its interactive chair gains the Committee update cadence and now gets 5/8 facts to the independent chair's 3/8. The UNAMI interaction remains at 4/8 versus 2/8. Its independent chair now truncates. The longer UNAMI notes diverge before the original cap, and the generation batches changed. The extension is a paired replication with the same prompts and explicit budgets. [Facts, conflicts and prefix checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/private-deliberation-cap-evidence/results.json)

Forward both accepted source groups to the same chair verbatim and skip generated notes. The resulting records retain 7/8 and 6/8 with no marked contradictions, and both finish within the word limit. They use one model call and 88 or 104 output tokens. The interactive episodes use three calls and 241 or 234 output tokens. Neither verbatim record is fully complete. A model-generated exchange improves over independent summaries in this setup, and a simpler forwarding path retains more when sending the source is feasible. Track communication payload alongside inference cost when testing whether learned communication adds value under constrained source delivery. [Protocol and matched records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#raise-the-reporting-cap-and-forward-the-source-verbatim)

### Compare source excerpts and peer notes at 48 tokens per desk

At a common 48-token chair-facing cap per desk, compare raw token prefixes, complete source sentences, independent generated notes and an interactive pair. DRC final coverage is 6/8, 7/8, 4/8 and 1/8 respectively. The independent reporter fits four supported facts in 44 tokens. Its interactive counterpart echoes the first note's mandate and reaches the limit before completing a reporting fact. The source-prefix chair also makes an error, completing a dangling update clause with the wrong recipient. The complete-sentence arm keeps seven facts and has no marked contradiction. This result follows a joint change in channel budget and instructions from the earlier long-note experiment. The within-run comparisons hold the chair-facing limits fixed. [Measured payload and reviewed outputs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#compare-generated-notes-and-exact-extracts-under-a-common-payload-cap)

UNAMI ties at 4/8 across all four arms. The source extracts transmit six supported facts, and the chair omits both Council recipients. The two generated-note finals add unsupported claims. Keep three measurements distinct for future joint training: each sender's distinct supported information, the total traffic including the peer hop, and the facts preserved downstream. Under the same chair-facing cap a peer hop can consume additional tokens and displace complementary evidence. The final release gate remains separate from partial coverage. [Scored visibility and payloads](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/private-payload-evidence/results.json)

### Fit verified excerpt selection and replay it through the final writer

Train both tabular agents for 180 exact updates on the written-vote source slots. Each desk privately holds two excerpts and sends one. The second desk receives the first selection with probability 0.8, and each selection reaches the chair independently with probability 0.9. Reward distinct delivered excerpts, public priorities and their joint coverage. Under held-out overlapping source inventories, the new policy duplicates 8.27% of attempted sends versus 10.26% for greedy. Net delivery reward is 24.039 for learned, 23.774 for greedy and 24.065 for the exhaustive decentralized script with a known prior. Hiding the peer observation and waiving its charge lowers learned reward to 21.922. [Fit and exact script ceiling](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/evidence/payload-policy.json)

Pass every reachable DRC excerpt set through one frozen chair and review the resulting 11 records. Under the overlap prior, the learned policy licenses 3.480 assertions per record and preserves 2.976, versus 3.382 and 2.945 for greedy. On the original priority reward recalculated from fully preserved fields, greedy leads 19.955 to 19.767. Two review combinations yield contradictory legal modality even though the review excerpt alone is faithfully paraphrased. Only five of 11 terminal outputs clear the record guard. This is one held-out-to-fit docket, random simulated holdings and same-assistant exact-span review. Keep packet delivery, partial assertion coverage, requested-field survival and guard outcomes separately measurable. [Synthesis replay and per-excerpt evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/payload-policy-evidence/results.json)

### Preserve the legal force of each delivered source clause

Freeze the DRC excerpt packets and routing policies. In the final prompt, keep each source clause separate and require the original legal verb, actor, dates and recipients. The frozen writer now preserves every delivered fact and raises factual guard passes from 5/11 to 11/11. The two prior prospective-review errors disappear. The learned route's final score recovers its delivery advantage over greedy at 24.039 versus 23.774 under overlapping holdings. The optimized script scores 24.065. A deterministic renderer copying the admitted extracts reaches the same factual scores without writer inference. All six two-excerpt Qwen records still miss the requested paragraph count, and one singleton repeats the public vote. This is factual improvement on the same DRC packet bank. [Exact fixed-policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/payload-writer-contract-evidence/results.json)

Transfer the unchanged prompt to UNAMI and rerun both writers at the same cap. Factual coverage ties at 6/8, 4/5 and 4/4 across full, recurring-and-review and review-only views. The new prompt removes the two marked legal errors, and the full view still drops both Council recipients. Keep omissions, force and actor errors, layout and delivery separate. The review is same-assistant on two exposed diplomatic dockets, and the experiment adds no model training. [UNAMI comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/payload-writer-transfer-evidence/results.json)

### Make the peer hop optional and charge for sending

In one exact joint fit on the written-vote slots, let the first desk choose whether to pay for showing its selected excerpt to the second desk. The second desk cannot tell a skipped message from a lost one. At fees of 0.25, 1.5 and 4 points the learned team sends in 83.0%, 37.6% and 32.7% of cases. It beats the earlier always-send policy on the fixed DRC end records at every evaluated price and prior. Under shared holdings at the highest fee, it reaches 22.133 net versus 21.370 when random sends preserve the per-request rate, the same excerpt marginals and the same recipient policy. Conditional sending adds value beyond lowering overall traffic. [Joint fit and same-rate intervention](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/evidence/optional-peer-controls.json)

The learned team beats a script frozen on the uniform training prior in split holdings at the two higher prices. The exact script that sees the shifted prior leads everywhere and sends nothing in split holdings at the highest price. The fit has price-specific local states and never sees the shifted inventory prior. Keep the rate, send timing or selection, receipt, delivery and final-record scoring visible separately. This is a verified-excerpt protocol with simulated holdings and archived DRC synthesis. [Per-price results and decentralized script ceiling](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/evidence/optional-peer-policy.json)

### Make a peer receipt change a warranted compromise

Use three outer-space workshop packages with two private grade cards and a public third card. The two emitting roles choose which truthful package grade to disclose under the same one-grade limit. The first can pay to show its choice to the second before the latter sends. Grade a recommendation only when it wins under every completion of the chair's evidence. In one exact joint fit, warranted agreement on balanced worlds rises from 57.78% with the learned peer signal erased to 66.99% with it visible. Impasse recall stays near 81%. The fit reaches 0.733 net against 0.694 for the best independent script and 0.734 for the exact decentralized peer script. No single first-seat grade warrants a decision, and one of the nine worlds cannot be certified with one truthful grade per seat even when both arrive. Record structural limits separately from agent failures. This is a simulated three-seat decision and a fit of two tabular emitters. [Workshop, certificates and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#learn-selective-disclosure-for-a-three-seat-compromise)

Give frozen Qwen six paired evidence views including the sum-vs-worst-off contrast, certified impasse, a provisional choice and a genuinely unresolved two-receipt case. It emits zero exact correct actions from the grade tables and textual receipts. Supplying the deterministic decision and its compatible worlds raises exact actions to five of six. Only two explanations give sufficient reasons with a correct action. The impasse memo invents a total-grade threshold, and the correct maximin choice D appears inside prose instead of in the required action field. Keep the certified decision and proof separate from free-text rendering. This is one displayed order, six related views and same-assistant exact-span review. [Paired chair records and reasons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/three-seat-proof-evidence/comparison.json)

### Test the decision value of one paid follow-up

Give the chair one optional request for an additional grade after the initial two disclosures, and stop whenever those disclosures already certify an outcome. The request costs 0.08 and the two legs deliver with joint probability 0.81. The balanced joint fit raises agreement recall from 66.99% under the earlier protocol to 89.49% and impasse recall from 80.92% to 95.50%. It scores 0.8984 net and asks in 25.12% of episodes. The earlier emitters with an optimal querying chair score 0.8992, and the alternating script scores 0.8996. The additional authenticated grade explains the gain on this simulation. Jointly refitting the emitters adds no observed benefit over that chair retrofit. [Three-policy fit and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-followup.json)

The previously impossible world reaches 65.49% certification versus its exact 65.61% delivery ceiling. Pair identical opening receipts with different verified replies. A second-delegate grade of one on A warrants D, while its grade of three warrants A. A lost reply warrants continued deferral. The records give each possible competitor a verified counterexample when no common outcome exists. This measures evidence-based changes in the disposition across the same fictional space workshop. [Paired records and structural ceiling](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-followup-records.json)

### Test shared disclosure policies on different public scorecard grids

Fit three linear policy heads using features of each proposed grade, the visible evidence and public scorecard completions. Train only on the outer-space grid. Transfer the frozen 27 weights to new fictional Mali and Myanmar grids of 12 worlds each, and replay all six package orders. The follow-up raises net from 0.435 to 0.685 on Mali and from 0.668 to 0.867 on Myanmar relative to the same emitters with queries removed. The fully scripted public-model team leads at 0.915 and 0.937. Its emitters choose grades by how much they narrow the public domain, and its chair chooses a question by the fraction of compatible worlds it could certify less cost. [Training and exact transfer suites](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-transfer.json)

Replacing only the learned emitters with the public-model rule raises Mali to 0.906 and Myanmar to 0.935. Replacing only the learned chair yields 0.700 and 0.872. The Mali emitter swap lifts initial certifiable outcomes from 43.55% to 77.25%. Masking the rarely sent peer message changes net by less than 0.0001 on either test. The result distinguishes transfer of question selection from useful initial disclosure. It uses two new invented grade grids based on previously exposed UN workshop menus. [Swaps and authoring notes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#transfer-a-shared-disclosure-policy-to-new-workshop-grids)

### Measure room for a cooperative gain before comparing learners

Fit one bounded feature policy around the public-information rule on outer space. Hold out new Venezuela and climate grade grids before fitting, and report the two previously evaluated Mali and Myanmar grids separately. Anchoring improves over the earlier learned policy on all four targets. Training improves over the anchored initialization on two of four. The public rule still wins every comparison. On the two new grids the fit reaches 0.9273 and 0.9373 against 0.9414 on each. [Frozen grids and component replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-residual.json)

Exact enumeration finds the public rule already reaches the best independent score on all four targets. An optimistic planner that knows the hidden world before choosing disclosures and a question still cannot beat it on Venezuela or climate. Mali and Myanmar leave at most 0.0078 and 0.0128 net points. For comparison the earlier outer-space fixture attains a gain of 0.0102 with a peer hop over the exact independent optimum. A new benchmark needs a demonstrated coordination advantage or a measured open interval between its independent performance and feasible coordinated performance. Easier cases remain useful for checking decisions and explanations. [Bounds, exact attaining policies and positive reference](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-headroom.json)

### Freeze positive controls using an exact peer advantage

Under one truthful grade per seat and no chair follow-up, 160 unstructured draws produce no grid with a peer advantage of at least 0.025 net points. Mutating the earlier outer-space prototype yields 19 distinct certificate-count signatures above that threshold. Freeze four development and four reserved grids using exact independent and peer-enabled scripts alone, before evaluating the learner. The reserved gaps are 0.0399–0.0797. The grids are related mutations of one fictional prototype. [Registry and exact script witnesses](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-positive.json)

A single joint policy fit beats the exact independent optimum on three of four reserved grids. The mean scores are 0.7381 learned, 0.7232 independent and 0.7730 exact peer-enabled. Masking learned peer content while keeping its send fee gives 0.6648. The learned policy sends in 99.67% of cases versus 26.79% for the exact peer-enabled policy. This benchmark can detect causal use of peer messages and inefficient communication separately. The finding applies to the fixed-receipt budget and simulated grade setting. [Exact replay and all option orders](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/evidence/three-seat-positive-fit.json)

### Check whether the learned disclosure survives prose transmission

Freeze the learned selectors from the positive controls and generate all 12 package-grade statements with Qwen, then read each back with a separate Qwen call. The decoder is numerically right on 11/12 and misreads C=0 as A=0. It also accepts a two-grade message and misattributes a correction. A strict same-assistant review counts only one generated sentence as an unequivocal current grade. The others ask to adjust, review or assign one. This distinguishes recovering a label from preserving its diplomatic force. [Exact messages and controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/three-seat-language-evidence/results.json)

Replaying the observed Qwen map on the reserved grids lowers mean net from 0.738 typed to 0.304 with exact tuple binding. Binding drops the misread grade. Without it the verifier certifies false outcomes in about 8% of episodes on three grids. A one-sentence exact declaration and deterministic parser preserve the typed results. This tests a fixed language realization of the learned disclosure action. It keeps sender and reader errors, factual judgment, proof and cost separately observable. [Transport experiment and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#realize-the-learned-disclosures-in-prose-and-read-them-back)

### Repair the message writer and audit quoted evidence separately

Prompt frozen Qwen to express a confirmed numerical grade for a named package ID. All 12 outputs carry their intended current assessment under same-assistant review. The original reader prompt now reads them all correctly, and adding a reader quote check yields the same 12/12. The fixed learned selectors recover their typed score of 0.738 on the same four reserved grids. This separates the writer repair from the additional reader constraint. It reuses the same finite vocabulary, model, and simulated disclosure policies. [Repaired roundtrips and exact decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/three-seat-language-contract-evidence/results.json)

Seven free-form development controls produce the intended host action, with several abstentions recovered by rejecting malformed output. A separately authored reader can submit a literal quote that the gate accepts from a multi-grade statement, a peer attribution, or a withdrawn grade. Require the quote and separately evaluate whether the whole message states a unique current commitment by the authenticated speaker. The quote grammar was amended after seeing how the model abbreviates package references. [Provenance and whole-message controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#preserve-grade-commitment-and-check-what-a-quote-can-prove)

### Judge whether a quoted grade is a live commitment

Hold the exact grade quote constant across 16 authored controls and vary the surrounding speech act. Use Qwen with direct and checklist instructions and require agreement over two answer orders. Both get 6/16 right and defer on nine. The direct judge admits another delegation's grade when spoken by the chair. The checklist admits a proposed grade. Quote-only admission accepts all eight invalid statements by construction. Keep the separate labels on eight exposed generated controls and report deferral alongside false admission. [Paired statements and model verdicts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/three-seat-commitment-evidence/results.json)

Substitute each nonbinding statement for the same truthful package-grade tuple in the four frozen policy grids. Quote-only admission produces an unwarranted certificate in 15.48% of episodes averaged across these counterfactuals, even though every proposed option remains correct on the hidden numbers. The checklist reduces that rate to 2.62% and the direct prompt to zero in these specific trajectories. Their low control accuracy still precludes relying on either as the entire gate. Score truth of the decision and whether the visible evidence licenses it separately. [Exact replay under nonbinding speech](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/three-seat-commitment-evidence/decision-replay.json)

### Elicit an authorized commitment when it would complete the proof

Keep provisional grades outside the evidence until the authenticated speaker confirms them with authority. The frozen delegate reports the exact grade in 12/12 granted cases and declines in 12/12 withheld cases. Replay the measured replies through the four previous synthetic grids. At 50% grant probability, dropping all provisional evidence scores 0.5833 net. Asking only when the reply could complete the proof scores 0.6302 with requests in 14.45% of episodes. Asking about any pending grade in an unresolved view finds the same certificates and scores 0.6273 with requests in 18.06%. If all authority is withheld, the selective rule incurs costs and scores 0.5717. Its break-even grant probability is 9.88% under these prices and link assumptions. [Scripted reference and model replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#ask-for-an-explicitly-authorized-confirmation)

The experiment gives the responder a private simulated authorization bit and enforces it independently at admission. The price, authorization probabilities and statement substitutions are controlled inputs. Hold the learned disclosure policy fixed and score refusal and missing replies as absence of new evidence. This tests the transition from a proposal to an authorized declaration under a structured reply contract. [Actual replies and paired proof changes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/three-seat-confirmation-evidence/results.json)

### Test source-grounded confirmation and separate institutional roles

Replace the supplied authorization bit with a local documentary view from the DRC sanctions and UNAMI cases. Across 24 claim and source controls, the direct Qwen prompt gets status and passage right in 16 and the checklist in eight. Both confirm all eight valid claims. The direct prompt gets seven of eight source-withholding pairs right while still confirming the wrong institutional role on a recipient reversal. Relabel three authoring foils unverified before inspecting model outputs, because missing support in a local summary does not establish contradiction. Keep original labels and decisions available for audit. [Local-source controls and scored adjudication](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/sourced-confirmation-evidence/adjudicated.json)

Extract four role fields from the source and statement in separate prompts, then compare values. This detects three out of four altered fields and defers on a fourth, with all four faithful comparisons matching. The DRC recipient reversal is caught by reading Council and Committee in the relevant roles independently. A posthoc gate on the fields selected from this pilot suppresses the one direct and two checklist false confirmations and preserves all eight correct admissions. This is a targeted component test on exposed cases. Reuse the extraction design on independent documentary pairs before assigning it a reward role. [Role reads and gate replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/sourced-role-binding-evidence/gated-replay.json)

### Check whether separate role extraction transfers across real dockets

Freeze six fields on UNIFIL and Colombia before inference. Compare a direct confirmation with independent extraction from the source and claim, using the prior DRC and UNAMI prompt unchanged. Under two answer-code orders, the separate method gets 23/24 field reads right. It admits all six faithful claims, catches all six altered relations and withholds all six claims whose establishing passage is missing. The direct method admits five faithful claims, four altered ones and one unsupported claim whose source is withheld. Requiring both methods retains five faithful claims and no unsupported claim. One case where the extractors defer still correctly blocks admission. [Frozen prompts and exact outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/sourced-role-transfer-evidence/results.json)

The controls include vote versus mission support, coordination versus permission, institutional versus national speech, record of representation, and prospective versus immediate authority. Report the number of valid actions preserved rather than only counting vetoes. The six fields and allowed values are provided, and the cases are same-assistant-authored. This evaluates field verification beyond the original pilot, with whole-record judgment and learned negotiation still separate. [Benchmark protocol and source scope](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/space/README.md#transfer-the-role-checks-to-unifil-and-colombia)

### Check whether better partial records carry through to the full target

Transfer the first-pass separate documentary writer from Colombia to the UNIFIL docket. For every reachable packet of up to three cards, hold the source set and generation ceiling constant across single-call synthesis and separate calls. Freeze learned and scripted routing on the original authored UNIFIL rubric. The separate paragraph preserves all three operative criteria, while the single-call drafts better preserve the qualified Brazil–China contrast. Both approaches miss Brazil's compound criterion on every packet containing its statement. The comparison paragraph in the separate arm also drops its citations. [Transfer protocol and scorer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#transfer-separate-documentary-drafting-to-unifil)

The learned route rises from 38.01 to 39.40 final-record points and from 16.30% to 35.12% release against the received packet. Release with the full target remains 14.69%. The prior-aware adaptive script still leads the learned team on raw net points under either writer, 15.416 to 14.785 and 16.757 to 16.171. The canonical responder swap produces fewer raw points and more expected released points under separate drafting. Gate an incomplete delivery against what it was supposed to obtain as well as what it actually received. Score cross-source comparisons explicitly when testing whether separately authored fields preserve disagreement. This is frozen inference on one exposed UN event with simulated rosters and same-assistant exact-span review. [All generated records and exact frozen-policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/unifil-field-transfer-evidence/results.json)

### Train for complete delivery and measure the writer ceiling

Under the three-contact UNIFIL protocol, exact prior-aware planning can deliver all three cards 65.12% of the time. Either frozen writer releases only one of four target sets when every card is supplied. Strict release therefore tops out at 16.28%. The previous adaptive script reaches that rate. A planner with advance knowledge of the writer's failures can skip those targets and keep the rate at a lower cost. Report its advantage separately from communication success. [Exact upper bound and independent tree replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/full-target-headroom.json)

Change the sole shared terminal award to 28 points for complete evidence delivery, with the same three-contact budget and fees. One fit on written voting raises complete delivery there from 41.65% to 55.62%. With the correct specialist label, frozen transfer raises full-target release from 14.69% to 16.20% on UNIFIL and from 64.28% to 64.78% on Colombia. The learned requester with a canonical responder drops to 11.66% and 47.12%. With the incorrect label, Colombia falls below the prior fit by 2.53 percentage points. Track full release, partial-source utility, and reliability of role metadata as separate outcomes. The writer banks are archived UN outputs with same-assistant review and simulated source holdings. [Fit and exact transfer results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#train-routing-for-full-target-delivery)

### Stress the metadata before crediting learned coordination

Fit the same complete-delivery policy on written voting with an equal mixture of correct and reversed desk labels. At 50% label accuracy, full-target release rises from 14.50% to 15.62% on UNIFIL and from 58.00% to 62.50% on Colombia versus the earlier full-delivery fit. Canonical replies lower the new rates to 12.11% and 48.82%. The original raw-score fit gives 13.49% and 59.01%. The new joint policy also improves both release rates with split specialization. When the specialist label is always correct, the earlier fit still leads. Use the same physical rosters for correct and reversed labels, and report the full reliability curve. [Frozen policies, writer banks and evaluation](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#train-complete-delivery-with-unreliable-desk-labels)

An oracle that knows aggregate label reliability must plan over the mixture of correct- and wrong-label worlds. It cannot condition its actions on which side of the mixture generated an episode. Give the benchmark this bound separately from the ceiling that knows the realized label status. At 50% reliability on Colombia, the balanced policy scores 17.028 net against 17.068 for the mixed-prior planner. On UNIFIL, the writer still limits achievable full release. These are structured routing results under simulated holdings and archived generated records. [Reliability curves and mixed-prior bounds](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/noisy-desk-fit.json)

### Check if the receiving delegate can answer the question

Run matched UNIFIL and Haiti protocols with the same opening and local documentary cards. In the interactive arm only, show the generated opening and its question to the second desk. Give the same opening and corresponding reply to the final writer in both arms. Both opening questions ask about China's own position, which the other desk's source cannot establish. No new supported peer-specific answer reaches either final. UNIFIL ties at 35/70. Haiti falls from 21/70 to 14/70 when the answer and final lose Mozambique's capacity as speaker for the three African members. The pooled analyst scores 49 and 21. All eight records fail the full release checks. [Matched protocols and exact-span review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#check-whether-a-peer-question-changes-the-final-record)

Record question answerability from the recipient's source, response support, and visible retention as three distinct events. Log the first note's extra peer hop even with matched generation ceilings. Separately mark cross-source consensus inflation and whether a qualified position survives in the final. This is a frozen-model, same-assistant-reviewed probe on two exposed dockets. Visibility changes the entire incoming note. A precise question tied to a fact the other desk can establish is a distinct intervention. [Output tokens, unique ideas, spans and all final records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/peer-question-evidence/results.json)

### Compare an answerable question with deleting the question

On UNIFIL and Haiti, change only the question shown to the second desk. The chair always sees the original opening and the resulting reply. Deleting the question and replacing it with a reply-side question both recover all four requested propositions in the replies. The chair preserves one of the four after deletion and all four after the replacement. The two same-batch original-question records preserve one. The replacement raises record scores from 35 to 42 on UNIFIL and from 14 to 21 on Haiti. No record clears all release checks. [Matched prompts, results and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#change-only-the-question-the-second-desk-sees)

Credit the answerable-question intervention for final survival in this comparison. Against the question-deletion control it elicits no extra supported requested proposition. Keep those effects distinct in the process rubric. The two replacements were authored after inspecting the earlier development cases. Both original-question replies replay exactly, but the Haiti chair varies across batches, so use the original-question condition from this batch to estimate the contrast. [Every output and source-linked score](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/peer-question-repair-evidence/results.json)

### Test whether agents can generate source-answerable questions

On Colombia and UNAMI, give the question writer the receiving desk's source IDs and topic descriptions, without the source answers. Compare an indexed question, an unindexed question and no question. Hold the first note and chair inputs fixed apart from the generated reply. Both unindexed questions are unanswerable. The index yields one partially answerable question and one unanswerable question. No indexed reply establishes an answer to its question from the recipient's own cards. Final scores for the indexed arm are 21/70 on both dockets, against 28 and 35 with no question. [Run and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#generate-questions-from-the-receiving-desks-source-index)

The index can identify a subject without identifying the claim a desk can establish. On Colombia the indexed exchange drops Ghana's capacity as speaker for the African members. On UNAMI it turns lack of a provision in the receiving desk's local cards into a claim that the official record lacks it. Annotate unsupported premises, source answerability, supported replies and final retention independently. Penalize an assertion about a whole dossier when the speaker only has evidence of absence from its own cards. This is a two-docket frozen generation test with same-assistant review. [Scored questions, replies and final records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/peer-index-transfer-evidence/results.json)

### Evaluate the handover by what its next user can do

A fresh frozen reader saw the delivered Haiti and UNIFIL records, including their common vote line. Across three answer orders it recovered 0/4, 1/4 and 3/4 of the question-targeted facts from the original, deleted and answerable-question records respectively. All three carried 4/4 other sampled facts. The fourth targeted item was unstable even on its faithful calibration sentence. Each Haiti record still omitted China's welcome for Kenya's offer, leaving a facilitator unable to establish that specific overlap with the three African members. On the later UNAMI comparison every arm preserved both one-time deadlines and failed to supply the correct four-month reporting cycle. These development measurements distinguish a successful handover to a particular recipient from a change in aggregate rubric points. [Results, controls and downstream benchmark data](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md)

Use the actual recipient's job to define the evaluation before inspecting candidate records. The new corpus includes 24 facilitator briefs on release of documented common ground and 32 calendar handovers requiring confirmation, correction or verification. Keep the source and institutional history fixed while changing access or the mistaken working draft. Score preserving a legitimate consensus statement and rejecting a false joint attribution separately. Always withholding earns 18/24 dispositions and misses every releasable statement. Compare solo and team systems on the same successor task and record attributable changes in the final product.

### Measure what each source-owned question adds over passing

Enumerate two authored, source-answerable questions and a no-question action on Colombia and UNAMI. Freeze the opening and let the selected question affect the chair only through the receiving desk's generated reply. All eight requested propositions appear in the replies and seven survive synthesis. Six are already stated without questions. Colombia ties at 28/70 across the actions. Asking for UNAMI's review actor yields 42/70 versus 35/70 when passing and 28/70 when asking for consultations. The improved reply names the Council as the recipient of the review, while the final loses the recipient and gains regional cooperation. Every UNAMI output retains the same incorrect reporting cadence from the opening. [Menu, scored records and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#measure-the-effect-of-specific-question-actions)

Score a question against silence on the same visible facts. Rewarding the number of requested facts alone would credit six already disclosed facts in these two cases. Preserve the requested fact's survival, any unrelated gains or regressions, and the 70-point record assessment separately. This is a development estimate of action value from fixed generated outcomes. The menus were authored and no learned question selector was used. [All twelve calls, fields, and action contrasts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/field-question-action-evidence/results.json)

### Separate calendar usability from a complete documentary handoff

Freeze the UNAMI opening and the no-question and review-actor replies. Cross visibility of the original REPORT card with instructions to prioritize official sources and preserve each obligation's actor, recipient and time. The card alone corrects the reporting interval for one reply. With the instructions, it corrects the interval and preserves the full recurring-report relation for both. The no-question control previously cited the REPORT card for the interval that it contradicts. The best record gains seven points and supplies all three calendar entries. No record preserves both the review actor and recipient, including the all-source pooled control. [Matched inputs, controls and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#verify-the-reporting-source-at-the-unami-handoff)

The material distinguishes fixing a corrupt upstream note, preserving a newly available actor-recipient relation, and supplying a calendar. Keep those endpoints separate in the score. In the actor reply the Council recipient is present before writing. A final that drops this recipient or moves it to a different responsibility has lost part of the contribution. A source ID on a sentence is insufficient when the source contradicts the claim. These are nine outputs on one exposed docket with same-assistant review. [All records and exact-span scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/unami-handoff-evidence/results.json)

### Use verbatim assembly as a control for evidence lost in synthesis

Preserve the authentic UNAMI reply's complete review sentence when the peer receipt arrives. Preserve the official reporting clause only when its own card arrives. Remove the old false cadence and hold the generated remainder fixed. With both receipts, the writer retains two complete obligations, all three calendar entries, and 56/70 on the richer remainder. Each receipt independently contributes seven points. This gives a baseline for where the quality loss occurs and establishes a conjunction for complete handoff, without evidence of an interaction bonus in the raw rubric. No record clears full release. [Pinned clause control and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/README.md#preserve-the-reviewed-unami-handoff-clauses-verbatim)

The registry rejects missing receipts, slot and source substitutions, actor and date errors, and a faithful paraphrase. Treat this as exact transport for pre-reviewed text. It leaves semantic intake and whole-record quality as separate gates. Pair the outcome with the generated chair's results and hold the remainder fixed to distinguish the value of a delivered peer sentence from a change to the rest of the prose. [Assembled records and all admission controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/colombia2023/unami-verbatim.json)

### Check whether peer observation improves complete handoff

Select one excerpt per desk and require both independently delivered UNAMI obligations for complete handoff. Hold the receipt assembler fixed. Under split holdings the learned pair scores 37.48% with the first desk's acknowledgment visible and 34.17% with it erased. The exact known-prior scripts get 37.59% with peer observation and 34.27% without. Both scripts average 44.05 ordinary rubric points. Track the full conjunction because equal partial scores can hide a real coordination effect. The previously fitted routing policy already reaches 37.46% under planned messages. Credit the information channel and measure the much smaller change from the new training objective separately. [Protocol, training and exact comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#compare-a-receipt-acknowledgment-with-a-planned-send)

For a strict two-delivery objective, a planned-send message and a delivery acknowledgment coincide on episodes that can still succeed. Under the fixed learned policy the acknowledgment preserves completion while sending 10% fewer messages at the design link and 30% fewer at the weaker link. The distinction can still change partial records. This result uses simulated source holdings and no further chair generation. [Metrics across priors and link strengths](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/receipt-ack.json)

### Measure whether receipt prose preserves the routing gain

Use the actual UNAMI review reply and documentary excerpts with minimally contrasted confirmed and planned envelopes. Run a frozen Qwen decoder and a direct action chooser on all local two-card holdings and two option orders. On split inventories the learned typed router completes 37.48%. It falls to 34.17% through the decoder, at the erased-message baseline. Direct language action reaches 29.04%, against 33.19% with the confirmation erased. The decoder recognizes REVIEW consistently and withholds all five negative messages, while three of four valid receipts are misread or deferred. Correcting REPORT alone restores the typed learned result in replay. [Exact prompts, results and counterfactual](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#turn-receipt-prose-into-a-routing-action)

Measure whether a received clause changes the next action in the complementary direction. Accuracy on planned-send rejection cannot establish this. Report read failures and action failures separately. The intervention uses real clause text inside authored envelopes and a known target pair. It tests one link between language and an already trained router, with no new policy fit. [Per-order decisions and delivery replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/receipt-ack-language-evidence/results.json)

### Verify receipt status before reading the clause

Use descriptive field labels with a single read and with separate reads for current receipt status and the excerpt. On UNAMI and DRC the separate status read handles all 20 confirmed, planned, silent and withdrawn messages in both label orders. The separate field read identifies six of eight confirmed clauses. The single read identifies five and mislabels DRC mandate expiry as prospective review. Both identify both target clauses on both dockets. UNAMI completion returns to the typed router's 28.17% and 37.48% in the overlap and split replays. The descriptive one-call read reaches the same rate, so distinguish clearer field labels and the separable status gate from the source of improved completion. [Receipt controls and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#read-receipt-status-and-clause-identity-separately)

The excerpt boundary is supplied by the harness. The DRC decoding transfer is applied to the abstract delivery game and has no generated DRC record outcome. Preserve failures on ancillary clauses as well as the two required receipts. This prevents a target-only completion score from certifying the whole message reader. [Per-message decisions and comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/receipt-ack-binding-evidence/results.json)

### Promote ancillary receipts to required fields

Replay the DRC readers through all six requested two-clause packets and the archived terminal writers. The reporting pair preserves the Council deadlines and monthly Committee exception and retains the 37.48% completion rate on split holdings. When the target includes mandate expiry, the single reader's expiry-to-review confusion lowers delivery to 29.21–30.87%. The separate reader's deferral holds 34.17%, near the erased baseline. The typed router remains at 37.48%. Repeat target roles before treating a decoder's success on one pair as a general result. [Scored DRC text and per-pair replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#score-drc-receipt-decisions-through-the-actual-writer-bank)

The earlier original writer fails four complete packets on material accuracy. With the same typed routing, average safe two-clause handoff improves from 12.49% to 37.48% under the reviewed separate-clause writer. Its single-read and separate-read scores are 33.90% and 35.83%. Measure delivered clauses, downstream fidelity and packet guard separately. This uses archived generated DRC supplements with same-assistant review and simulated delivery. [All six target requests, eligible clauses and writer failures](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/receipt-ack-drc-replay.json)

### Test expiry and prospective review under date and polarity changes

Test the authentic DRC and Colombia records plus swapped dates, negation, paraphrases and clauses with both acts. A four-way force classifier gets 5/14 with nine deferrals. Checking enacted extension and prospective review independently gets 3/14 with ten deferrals and one wrong stable decision. The direct choice restores the DRC expiry, including with a swapped date, and defers on the authentic prospective-review receipt. In split-holdings replay it raises both simple expiry pairs from 34.17% to 37.48% and lowers the corresponding prospective-review pairs from 37.48% to 34.17%. [Prompts, results and writer-bank replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/README.md#distinguish-an-enacted-expiry-from-a-prospective-review)

Test every affected field when changing a decoder. Exact proof on the earlier expiry error alone would miss the newly lost review receipt. Record abstention and misclassification separately and keep authentic received text distinct from synthetic controls. [All 14 cases and paired outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/crossbench/drc2023/receipt-force-evidence/results.json)

### Score what the next clerk can certify when a source is missing

Run the 32 UNAMI and DRC calendar handovers with identical archival excerpts and answer choices. Cards alone produce 42/64 correct field decisions. Adding the field's expected source ID and delivery status raises it to 49/64. Both presentations already get all 32 sourced decisions right. The remaining errors concern evidence absence. Enforcing referral when the host knows the specific source is absent brings this closed fixture to 64/64 and removes all unsupported certifications. The source map and two alternative dates are supplied. [Prompts, exact grading and all cases](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#test-a-structured-receipt-on-the-calendar-successor-task)

Weight those same downstream decisions by the learned router's receipt patterns. On split holdings the acknowledgment raises the chance that both dates can be used from 34.17% to 37.48%. Text-only reading also slightly increases unsupported certifications, because the model handles the changed mix of missing sources poorly. The host gate removes those unsupported actions in this controlled design. Score justified referral and actual delivery of both dates independently. [All receipt masks, decisions and replay outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/receipt-handover-evidence/results.json)

### Remove the date menu from the clerk handover

Generate both ISO dates, actions and source IDs on all 32 UNAMI and DRC handovers. The raw field-receipt condition has 11/64 exact decisions under the strict two-line format. Inspecting each line independently and normalizing blank verification columns gives 52/64, against 51/64 for text cards. The same audit plus forced referral gives 54/64. A post-hoc adapter compares the generated date to the draft and corrects six action labels without changing any date, reaching 60/64 and 29/32 handovers. The remaining errors include confusing a DRC prospective review date with the enacted mandate expiry. [Open-date generations and verification](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#generate-the-handover-without-a-date-choice-menu)

Only five of eight fully sourced handovers succeed in the unadapted generation. On split holdings the acknowledgement raises both dates usable from 21.36% to 23.42%, below the previous date-menu rates of 34.17% and 37.48%. Split the endpoint into format, field-specific date extraction, the action determined by the draft, and whether a needed source arrives. A syntactically correct receipt with the right source ID cannot establish that the chosen date belongs to the right act. [Strict outputs and diagnostic normalization](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/open-handover-evidence/line-audit.json)

### Bind each generated date to one documentary field

Replace the two-line generator with one open date read per field from its bound card. Let the host derive confirm or correct from a date comparison and infer referral when the expected card did not arrive. On the 32 historical UNAMI and DRC fixtures this recovers 64/64 fields and 32/32 handovers, against 60/64 and 29/32 after the previous post-hoc action repair. The learned acknowledgment again raises complete two-date usability from 34.17% to 37.48% on split holdings. The change bundles source isolation, call count and output design. [Exact reads, comparison and routing replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#recover-one-calendar-field-from-its-own-source)

The method gets 10/12 additional controls right. It confuses Colombia's existing expiry with future authorization in one case and withholds the authentic DRC expiry when the paragraph reference is absent. Both hypothetical DRC date swaps are read correctly. Benchmark source formatting and fields that lack a value even when their card arrives. A gate that knows whether a card arrived cannot distinguish an operative expiry from a future action mentioned on the same card. [Control inputs and raw generations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/isolated-date-evidence/results.json)

### Score publishable common ground and its component claims

Run the Haiti and Mali facilitator cases through a direct disposition judge and a separate check of each actor's claim. Composing the latter gives 24/24 dispositions against 17/24 direct, with zero versus three unsupported publications. The same component classifier gets only 8/12 source readings right. Its three false supports are masked by a contradiction or a missing positive on the other side. Score upstream relations as well as the final joint decision. [72 reads, contrasts and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#route-evidence-for-publishable-common-ground)

With split holdings, the learned acknowledgment raises correct publication across the equally weighted proposals from 11.39% to 12.49%. The per-snapshot disposition accuracy remains 100% because it also gives credit for correct withholding. Keep how often the facilitator can act separate from how correctly it handles its current evidence. The test releases existing sentences. Replacement prose, human review, and transfer beyond these two dockets remain open. [Per-source decisions and weighted outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/routed-common-ground-evidence/results.json)

### Require a usable replacement as well as the right disposition

Generate facilitator replacements for all 24 Haiti and Mali evidence views. The direct writer gets 15 dispositions and three complete briefs right. A writer given the previously checked disposition gets all 24 dispositions and eight complete briefs right. It preserves the actual contrary position in all four revisions and fails the citation requirement in each. Both writers preserve the Haiti overlap and omit required card IDs. Nine staged briefs have citation as their only outstanding release condition under same-assistant review. [48 actual briefs, review and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#draft-a-replacement-that-preserves-the-status-of-each-side)

On split holdings, peer observation increases valid publication of newly drafted briefs from 5.70% to 6.25%, half the earlier result that released the original sentences directly. The difference comes from the writing contract and supporting evidence. Keep source-bounded dispositions, exact dated support, and usable revision text as distinct endpoints. The source packets and case keys are exposed development data. [Full evaluation table](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-replacement-evidence/results.json)

### Test whether citation repair makes a frozen brief usable

Attach source IDs to the staged Haiti and Mali replacement briefs without rewriting their text. A separate frozen reader raises usable briefs from 8/24 to 10/24 and adds no excess citations on this sample. Appending every received card reaches 15/24 and adds excess IDs to four. Review-approved IDs reach 17/24, with all four contradictions publishable. The selective reader recovers one of four contradictions and both supported retain briefs. It sometimes treats an embedded claim being rejected as the writer's own assertion. [Exact outputs and citation evaluation](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#attach-citations-to-fixed-facilitator-prose)

Score the added citations separately from whether the brief's factual claims are licensed. Under split holdings, learned peer observation raises usable briefs from 27.23% to 32.22% with the selective reader, and valid publication from 11.39% to 12.49%. The additional source checks use the same docket texts and inherit the prior same-assistant factual review. This is an output repair, with no new fit or rewritten claims. [Per-brief citations and routing replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-citation-evidence/results.json)

### Train for both positive publication and contradiction repair

In the facilitator game a revision may need one contradiction while a published common-ground line needs both supports. Let each desk choose a clause or pass, with a send fee and an acknowledgment channel. A uniform fit scores above the scripted control on its own synthetic training mix while publishing only 5.41% of support-pair cases, compared with 32.40% for the script. Match the publication, revision and withholding classes by sampling equally, without putting the global disposition in either actor's prompt or state. [Training and replay protocols](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#learn-when-to-send-a-source-or-pass-on-common-ground-claims)

In the split-holdings Haiti and Mali replay with frozen model reads, the balanced fit raises usable actionable briefs from 13.76% to 20.14% versus the uniform fit. Erasing acknowledgments reduces the balanced policy to 11.19%. Greedy reaches 21.54% and incurs greater cost. At 0.10 per send the balanced fit leads greedy on net with model reads, while the order reverses under exact relations. Score cost, each disposition and strict usable output along with the change from erasing peer evidence. The replays score existing prose under simulated delivery. [Both policies and the archived writer endpoints](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-policy-balanced.json)

### Stress publication on new one-sided source panels

Transfer the frozen facilitator policies from Haiti and Mali to eight UNIFIL and Colombia proposals. The reader gets 11/16 source relations right and falsely retains two fully received proposals. On split holdings, revealing the peer acknowledgment raises valid retain decisions from 1.19% to 7.90% and false actions from 10.46% to 17.18%. Net falls from −0.007 to −0.015 at the tested fee. The earlier uniform fit remains positive because it sends less and rarely attempts publication. [Transfer cases and exact routed outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#transfer-the-frozen-facilitator-policy-to-unifil-and-colombia)

Holding the balanced weights fixed and supplying correct local reads raises split net to 0.216 with the acknowledgment and 0.148 without it. This separates the value of coordinating delivery from amplifying erroneous local attributions. Evaluate false actions against the sources that actually arrived. Include one-sided support and unknowns as explicit strata. A fully sourced packet can still be unsafe to publish. [Perfect-reading intervention](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-policy-transfer-evidence/perfect-reader.json)

### Separate quote provenance from entailment

Ask frozen Qwen for an exact documentary witness for each local relation. On the eight UNIFIL and Colombia proposals, the exact-witness reader gets 12/16 local relations correct and 7/8 full-packet dispositions. The earlier label-only protocol gets 11/16 and 5/8. Both earlier false retentions disappear. An exact passage saying a text fell short of fully addressing concerns still leads to a false revision of the weaker claim that it addressed some concerns. On the twelve legacy Haiti and Mali relations the reader gets 9/12, including two wrong claims backed by real quotes. [All generations, admission checks and relation keys](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#require-a-documentary-span-for-each-source-reading)

Replay the very same raw labels with and without the exact quote gate. In split holdings, the gate leaves false actions at 14.79% and lowers valid revision from 18.20% to 15.63%. The learned router scores 0.007 net with the gate, against −0.030 with acknowledgment erased and −0.026 for the local script. Replacing only erroneous admitted labels with unknown using the authored key eliminates false actions and reaches 0.199 net, compared with 0.216 with every relation corrected. This is a diagnostic oracle on exposed cases. It isolates the opportunity for a separate claim-to-source entailment check. Grade that check on scope, qualification, and attribution, and track true claims lost at the gate. [Per-policy outcomes under both inventories](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-witness-evidence/routing-replay.json)

### Verify a cited relation before forwarding it

Judge the 19 surviving quotes with a separate call to the same frozen Qwen. Ask whether the cited words establish the proposed relation for the exact actor, act, and qualification. Require agreement when the option order reverses. The judge keeps 12/15 valid readings, blocks 2/4 invalid ones and discards all three valid contradictions that reached it. Five results depend on option order. The two false readings that survive concern strength of wording and treating support for adoption as a recorded vote. Full-packet accuracy falls from 7/8 to 5/8. [Judge instructions, captured decisions and grading](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#judge-whether-the-cited-words-establish-the-proposed-relation)

Under split delivery the gate cuts false actions from 14.79% to 9.28%, while valid revisions fall from 15.63% to zero. The frozen learned router falls from 0.007 to −0.069 net. The first order alone does better on this set, at 0.013 net. The authored semantic veto still reaches 0.199. Keep reference relations indisputably true, false and unresolved under minimal alternations of actor and scope. Record judge consistency and valid-action recall alongside false-action rate. This replay isolates a failure of the semantic judge on existing receipts. It adds no evidence about the quality of final diplomatic prose. [Both option orders and all control replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-entailment-evidence/results.json)

### Read cited wording before assigning claim polarity

Give the frozen judge the quotation, its context, and the assertion without the first reader's label. The judge recovers all three valid contradictions lost by the earlier judge and scores 15/19 on the archived reads and 8/8 on new authored controls. Seven of 19 archived readings still change across the two option orders. Use the judge to corroborate the original relation before publication. In split UNIFIL and Colombia replay, the agreement gate raises valid revision from 15.63% to 18.56% and lowers false actions from 14.79% to zero. It also halves valid retention from 7.90% to 3.95%. The learned router earns 0.171 at the fixed send fee, versus 0.137 with acknowledgment erased and 0.149 for the local script. These are simulated communication costs and exclude the judge's inference. [Judge instructions and all outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#judge-the-source-to-claim-relation-directly)

On archived Haiti and Mali briefs, the agreement gate raises the rate of usable revisions and reduces usable retentions. Directly replacing the earlier reading introduces 12.37% false actions after a polarity mistake on Russia's final-extension statement. Score preservation of warranted statements and objections under matched transmission budgets before using a judge as reward. Keep the agreement gate as a measured comparison for a fresh record test with neutral presentation, source-family holdouts and actual chair generation. [Archived-brief replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-source-relation-evidence/brief-replay.json)

### Score fresh final briefs under identical receipt masks

Generate 35 fresh briefs over four UNIFIL and Colombia proposals and all 16 receipt masks. Hold the writer and sources fixed, compare a pooled writer with staged quote and agreement dispositions, and reuse identical prompts. Grade a 70-point final record across disposition, each speaker's supported position or explicit uncertainty, citation binding, factual and authority fidelity, and completion. The reviewer sees the source cards and unedited output without the arm label. [Prompts, rubric and reproducible exact-span grading](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#generate-a-fresh-chair-brief-for-the-routed-receipts)

On split holdings the agreement arm scores 62.44/70 and 46.24% strict versus 45.76/70 and 28.75% strict for the pooled writer operating on the same receipts. The agreement arm has zero false actionable headings and ties its acknowledgment-erased control to two decimals. Every strict pass is a withhold. Across all 11 generated prompts whose evidence warrants retention or revision, three lose only the provenance criterion, and none passes strict. Keep useful publication and correction separate from valid abstention. The result tests fresh prose on exposed events and a controlled receipt path. It provides no measured interaction gain on the final record. [All generated briefs, rubric scores and receipt-weighted outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-fresh-brief-evidence/results.json)

### Bind generated chair fields to delivered speakers and sources

Generate one source-local field per speaker. Bind each received field to its source ID and render an explicit uncertainty sentence for an absent source. Keep the original 70-point rubric and the same word budget, allowing two short sentences on one line. Seven of eight fields preserve the decisive limit or position. The remaining field drops “some” from Brazil's description of Lebanon's concerns. Four assembled retain/revise briefs now clear the strict rubric, versus zero with the unstructured chair. [Generated fields, assembled records and review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#bind-each-speakers-wording-to-the-card-the-chair-actually-receives)

In split replay, the acknowledgment raises usable actionable briefs from 19.74% to 26.45%, driven by one UNIFIL common-ground proposal. The local greedy script delivers 27.95%. After the simulated send cost, learned routing scores 0.195 against the script's 0.177 and the acknowledgment-erased router's 0.130. Keep raw usable records and cost-adjusted utility separate. Move the frozen renderer, gates and policies to previously unused event families and test the same rubric against an evidence- and resource-matched solo and a separate independent-draft synthesis arm. [One-case effects and both receipt models](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-bound-field-evidence/results.json)

### Transfer the facilitator benchmark to Abyei and MINUSCA

Freeze two fresh event families and four proposals about common ground, vote polarity, and conditional mandate support. Use the same 70-point rubric, frozen evidence router and speaker-bound renderer. On the same delivered cards, neither the pooled writer nor independent-drafts synthesis produces a strict actionable brief. With quote-only reading and bound fields, the learned router produces a usable retain or revise in 22.31% of split cases versus 15.59% with acknowledgment erased and 22.97% for greedy routing. The learned router leads after the simulated send fee, 0.100 versus 0.087. This comparison matches final answer caps and receipt evidence. Total inference costs differ. [Case cards, model outputs and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#transfer-the-source-bound-facilitator-to-abyei-and-the-central-african-republic)

The quote reader gets all eight relations right. Requiring a second judge suppresses both valid joint statements and reduces usable actions to 14.41% despite a higher raw score out of 70. Both usable-action improvements come from MINUSCA. On Abyei, the speaker fields miss the documented affirmative vote or mislabel abstention as uncertainty. The MINUSCA revision exposes a separate authority error when a field attributes Council adoption to a delegate. Preserve exact evidence and graded usability while improving the writer. Treat the two-event result as transfer evidence for constrained acknowledgment and generated dossier briefs. [Per-proposal receipts, errors, and controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-event-transfer-evidence/results.json)

### Fit the router on publishable briefs and swap source positions

Train two exact joint policies on the same ten development proposals and frozen reader outputs. Reward correct action in one arm and require a publishable brief in the other. Only two of 80 receipt-channel combinations separate the rewards, both from the same Haiti correction. Both fits improve on development and lower transfer utility from 0.100 to about 0.061 under quote readings. The brief fit delivers the decisive MINUSCA objection alone in 37.08% of episodes against 57.65% for the frozen policy. [Both fits and mask-level evidence](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-record-policy-results.json)

Swap the addresses of the two source cards while keeping statements and grades attached. This raises the unbalanced brief fit from 0.061 to 0.108. Training with both slot assignments yields 0.077 in either assignment, behind the frozen policy and greedy script. The result supports balancing source placement and shows that sparse audited prose failures give little leverage to train a transferable reward. Include repeated, independently graded contrasts where adding a second card changes whether a correct action can be published. [Training and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#train-routing-on-reviewed-briefs-and-balance-source-positions)

### Give the chair writer a local assertion and test the witness separately

Freeze receipt masks, the quote reader, and routing weights. Generate source-bound fields using the exact one-speaker assertion, then pair it with the same assertion plus the admitted witness. On Abyei and MINUSCA, each new arm yields seven of eight complete, error-free fields versus four under the original prompt. The witness preserves the duration in a MINUSCA revision and drops it in a common-ground brief. [Matched prompts, review spans and raw generations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#give-the-speaker-field-writer-the-decisive-witness)

Under split quote-only replay, independently phrased one-speaker fields raise strict actionable output from 22.31% to 48.76%. Adding the witness reaches 45.01%. With assertion-only fields, erasing acknowledgment reduces the learned policy to 35.33%. The greedy control reaches 50.92% and earns marginally higher net utility after simulated send costs, 0.367 versus 0.365. This improves final-record quality on exposed cases and isolates an acknowledgment effect under the frozen policy. It leaves an open comparison against greedy routing with the stronger writer and total inference costs counted. [All controls and per-proposal effects](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-witness-writer-evidence/results.json)

### Retrain independent routing and charge for acknowledgment

Apply the same quote reader and one-speaker writer across all routing arms. A policy trained with no feedback produces publishable briefs in 45.92% of the split transfer cases, against 35.33% when the frozen adaptive policy loses acknowledgment and 48.76% when it retains acknowledgment. Under the fixed decisions and a 0.10 payload fee, the adaptive policy loses its utility advantage over independent at about 0.097 per attempted acknowledgment. Track both message classes and the shared inference cache. [Independent fit and exact cost sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-feedback-cost-results.json)

At equal fees an optional-feedback fit requests fewer than 0.006 acknowledgments per case and nearly reproduces independent routing. The retrained mandatory protocol nets 0.308 against 0.293 for independent, while valid joint retentions fall from 17.09% to 2.10%. The result isolates an efficiency gain that trades away common-ground coverage, and the mandatory fit regresses on split development. Keep peer use, public-record usability by disposition, and cost separate in the benchmark. [Protocol and all policy comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#price-acknowledgment-and-retrain-the-independent-control)

### Test revision after actual peer drafts on Bosnia and Afghanistan

Freeze four new facilitator proposals and compare peer-visible revision, independent revision, and pooled solo under the same five calls and per-stage caps. The chair receives the same documentary material in every arm. Review each speaker's point and its source warrant, and score the final out of 70 with separate attribution, disposition, and citation checks. Peer and independent both score 49.5/70, with 1/4 strict passes and matching point and warrant coverage. Pooled solo scores 41.0/70 and also passes one strict case. [Dockets, prompt design and exact-span review](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#let-delegates-revise-after-seeing-each-others-actual-draft)

Two of eight revised notes change when peer text is visible. One is a paraphrase. Pakistan adds a source-backed cooperation commitment that the chair leaves out. The final points are identical for every peer and independent pair. On initial draft circulation the pooled opening transfers Pakistan's account to China. Both private arms preserve uncertainty in the body but still print retain. The same neutral chair also recognizes the Russian objection to the Office while printing withhold in all three arms. Extend deliberation scoring to whether an explicit question resolves an evidence or authority conflict and whether the final disposition incorporates that resolution. [Per-turn changes and final records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-deliberation-evidence/results.json)

### Test whether a clarification question changes the final disposition

Give one designated delegate a source-check question, with a matched source-local verification arm and a no-reply control. The two reply arms share chair-visible question and documents. On the Afghanistan circulation case, the question changes a false contradiction into a correct unknown and brings the consultation distinction into the final. This raises the asked score to 53.5/70 from 50.5/70 with independent verification, and all arms still get 3/4 headings right and zero strict passes. [Protocol, exact replies and rubric](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#ask-for-the-evidence-that-decides-a-disputed-heading)

The improved answer violates the reply delimiter. Quotation marks around exact witness text also cause gate failures. Removing them would admit an independent reply with valid quote bytes and an incorrect relation. Score format, exact witness and semantic fit separately. On Bosnia the chair correctly describes a contradiction and nevertheless prints withhold. The two identical Bosnia chair inputs also produce different wording with the same grade. Deduplicate identical prompt bytes when attributing improvements to peer content. [Exact model outputs and paired checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-clarification-evidence/results.json)

### Compute chair headings from typed local evidence

Apply the same constrained three-way source check with and without the clarification question. Both conditions get 5/8 relations right and yield identical admitted labels. Binding the archived speaker fields to their source IDs raises strict final passes from zero to two of four. Replacing only the heading leaves zero passes, and the same bound writer reaches four of four with authored labels. The typed reader misses the decisive Bosnia objection despite correct wording in the underlying card and draft. [Constrained labels, all receipt masks and grades](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#compute-the-heading-from-source-local-relations)

On the same typed readings, the learned router yields 15.79% publishable retentions, trained independent reaches 17.09%, and greedy reaches 18.69%. No system produces a valid revision. Better transport cannot fix a contradiction withheld by the reader. Pair format enforcement with an independent semantic check on vote, act and scope before using typed decisions as a reward. [Case-level and cost-sensitive replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-typed-clarification-evidence/results.json)

### Separate support from incompatibility and test option-order bias

A split binary reader with two presentation orders defers on every local relation. It scores 1/8 on Bosnia and Afghanistan, down from 5/8 for the three-way reader, and 0/8 on Abyei and the Central African Republic, compared with 4/8 for the three-way control and 8/8 for the archived exact-quote reader. None of the split decisions produces a publishable action. [Protocol and unchanged-writer replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/README.md#verify-support-and-incompatibility-in-separate-reads)

All 40 first-order binary probes choose letter B, which carries the negative answer. The reversed order alone gets 8/8 on the transfer cards and makes a false contradiction on the asked Afghanistan circulation claim. A favorable option ordering can make the classifier appear reliable. Report answer-letter frequencies, order agreement, and release by disposition before using the classifier as a reward. [Exact scores and order diagnostics](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/facilitator-split-relation-evidence/results.json)

### Compare evidence order in an exact-witness reply

Return the local relation followed by an exact quote, then reverse the two fields with the same source, model and token cap. Relation-first restores the decisive Bosnia objection and yields 8/8 correct local relations and 4/4 strict complete briefs, versus 6/8 and 2/4 with quote-first. Both reach 8/8 on Abyei and the Central African Republic. Quote-first copies seven entire transfer cards and generates 471 output tokens compared with 223 for relation-first. Repeating the earlier relation-first prompts reproduces their token IDs and output text.
