### Hold the router fixed and isolate the learned score correction

On 126 modeled conditions, the learned pair correction raises matched exact planner net by +0.0347 on the historical banks and +0.0701 on the scoped Resolution 2686 writer. Correcting only the full-receipt grade contributes little. Moving the pair halfway toward its additive singleton estimate matches or exceeds both gains. The historical benefit comes from overgraded rows while the scoped benefit is largest under undergrading. Inside the robust planner, learned and additive corrections improve the scoped mean by +0.0799 and +0.0844 against the same original team and remove both below-silence outcomes. On the stronger verbatim writer their effects are −0.00008 and +0.0014. The study preserves 882 exact and 216 robust dispatch checks, including a separate check of 14 randomized mixtures. All cases are exposed and transport is simulated. [Forecast crossover and robust follow-up](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/forecast-mechanism/README.md)

### Lock a new pair-complementarity docket on disability protection

Resolution 2475 supplies a new archival family with a verified UNBench author-test event and no matching row in the pinned UNSC-Bench adopted records. France's advocacy for full treaty implementation and Russia's reading of the resolution's operative paragraph jointly support a seven-point distinction in an authored control. Seven frozen Qwen calls fail to realize it. France's note omits full implementation and leaves ratifying authority unclear. The UK note already records UK–Russia common ground, so it supplies no measured two-note advantage. The added UK–Russia bridge misstates Russia's treaty reading and takes that receipt record from 21 to zero. In 36 modeled routing conditions, nominal, learned and additive pair forecasts tie within each writer bank. Absolute mean release falls from 13.64 to 6.35 with the comparison writer. In a separate authored-bank sensitivity, the learned correction gains +0.0135 and additive gains +0.0113 against nominal routing. [Source audit, native records, controls and 144 policy replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/disability-2475-transfer/README.md)

### Restore the Resolution 2475 evidence and check claim scope explicitly

Crossing restored notes with the original and scope-check prompts shows that degraded notes alone cannot account for the missing France–Russia comparison. None of 12 comparison sentences adds raw credit. The scope prompt restores the native Russia–UK pair to its 21-point verbatim baseline by preserving the distinction between legal categories and obligations. Both prompts bring the restored full record from 63 to zero by attributing Russia's position on categories to the UK. Native routing forecasts tie. On restored verbatim records the learned and additive corrections add +0.0158 and +0.0178 mean net points. Adding either bridge lowers absolute mean release from 46.40 to 39.37 or 40.54, even when a team gains more over its corresponding fixed control. These results use an exposed interventional case, same-assistant marks and simulated delivery. [Crossed prompts, archived records and 432 dispatch checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/disability-2475-transfer/scope-bridge/README.md)

### Bind the Resolution 2475 comparison to verified source text

On restored notes, both the exact source rule and the Qwen-gated template add seven points to the France–Russia pair and full record. Full release rises from 63 to 70. The earlier free-form bridges brought the same full record to zero. Across ten matched relation probes, swapped answer letters agree on two supported cases and abstain on eight. Averaging the mapped logits would admit five of six unsupported claims. The rule and classifier emit identical records, and neither can add the contrast to the original native notes. Nominal mean release rises from 46.40 to 48.13 on the restored template bank. The frozen learned forecast adds +0.0135 and the additive forecast +0.0113 net points over nominal there. The record covers one exposed, authored case and 288 simulated policy checks. [Matched classifier probes, bound rendering and routing](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/disability-2475-transfer/bound-comparison/README.md)

### Select role-local source passages and test a focused chair request

Six native Qwen3.5-4B selections preserve all three passage sets under menu reversal. Russia and the UK retain the required claims while France chooses participation over treaty implementation. A source-bound assembly with an authored public lead releases 56/70 in both orders. The first-in-order control swings from 70 to 42 and the canonical-only decoder from 49 to 56. Nominal adaptive routing wins all 18 matched simulations on the native bank. The learned forecast adds +0.0046 and additive correction adds +0.0105 over nominal. A second four-call test supplies France with the public lead and an explicit request for an instrument-specific position. Under the locked whole-message decoder it still selects participation in every condition. The additional context raises input usage without changing documentary release or any replayed route. The docket is exposed and the menus, public lead and renderer are authored. [Native selectors and routing](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/disability-2475-transfer/native-selection/README.md) · [Receiver context](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/disability-2475-transfer/native-selection/receiver-context/README.md)

### Learn which source clauses to send under shared return and delivery loss

A three-coefficient shared policy trained on 96 synthetic menus raises the 2475 source selection from 56 to 70 with authored clause tags. Local coverage reaches the same 70. On the archived Tribunals menus the fitted selector uses a joint Peru–Russia distinction and reaches 58 versus 57 for local and priority-forecast rules at full receipt. The choice loses expected points below a two-thirds independent receipt probability. Adjusting the model's features for delivery explains most of the improved synthetic holdout score, with +0.0073 from refitting. A fixed optimistic rule reaches the joint oracle at all four tested rates on both exposed archives. The learned selector leaves 0.1875 points on the table at 75% receipt. Shared-return fitting uses simulated rewards and authored features, with no natural-language model weight updates. The release scorer independently verifies 208 joint selection and receipt records. [Training, controls and archived records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/evidence-choice/README.md)

### Let a later desk see the delivered evidence before choosing what to send

A new shared-return fit conditions the later desk on the earlier delivered note. It gains +0.0539 points over the previous no-feedback fit on 48 synthetic cases and +0.0511 over an equally informed fixed rule. Removing the observation returns the mean to the earlier no-feedback baseline. On Resolution 2422 both adaptive arms reach the documentary ceiling and exceed the strongest static policy by 0.095–0.375 points across four independent receipt rates. They tie the static controls on Resolution 2475. To separate observing content from inferring a deterministic sender choice, the private variant gives Russia one of two available passages. Both content-aware selectors reach the oracle and beat the strongest arrival-only policy in all 12 conditions, by 0.031–0.451 expected points. The renderer verifies the source records and the private experiment independently checks the closed-form gains. The channel broadcast is free in this simulator, and the private inventories are fictional. [Fit, matched controls and private receipt test](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/evidence-choice/receipt-conditioned/README.md)

### Transfer receipt-conditioned selection to the written security-sector reform vote

Resolution 2553 supplies a newly evaluated family with all 15 official votes joined to UNBench author-test and no matching pinned UNSC-Bench adopted row. All arms tie when every role has both candidate passages. With privately restricted earlier evidence, the exact benefit of reading the delivered content over seeing only arrival averages +0.0916 when the UK chooses later and +0.3938 when Germany chooses later. The frozen learned and fixed policies tie in all 24 private conditions. Both fall below the arrival oracle five times. A later-UK response swap repairs three high-delivery losses, while a Belgium plan swap repairs the two Germany-later losses. One exact coordinate update per desk reaches the content oracle throughout. The source packet and criteria were locked before replay. The 288 dispatch checks use authored relative priorities, fictional inventories and modeled delivery. [Written-vote sources, matched transfer and component swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/README.md)

### Refresh partner forecasts before choosing what to send

A one-step forecast predicts how the two deciding desks react to the earlier delivered note. The fixed refreshed rule repairs all five Resolution 2553 regressions and reaches the oracle in all 36 archived 2553 and 2422 conditions. A refit on 96 synthetic cases improves the 48-case holdout by +0.0403 over the older fit under correct and shifted prior announcements, but leaves three high-delivery UK regressions. The two Belgium-plan repairs also appear when the refit's updated forecast term is zeroed. A coordinator comparing candidates under the same announced payoff model gains +0.0379 and +0.0300 by offering either learned policy, with no additional value from the refit and the same score from exact planning. The archived replay verifies 216 modeled outcomes. [Refit, archived controls and candidate portfolio](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/evidence-choice/receipt-conditioned/partner-forecast/README.md)

### Test the receipt observation inside the native language-model decision

On Resolution 2553, all 48 one-token Qwen choices prefer the literal B. Swapping labels gives zero paired agreements. Averaging the mapped margins nevertheless recovers a receipt-dependent selection. It improves the native arrival-only comparator by +0.848 expected points and the strongest arrival-only comparator by +0.095 across four modeled scenarios. It reaches the content oracle in three and loses 0.508 in the high-delivery UK condition, where it switches away from the stronger inclusion combination. The repaired fixed planner reaches the content oracle in all four. Belgium's choices are all zero-margin local-coverage fallbacks. These 92 dispatch checks retain authored priorities, one exposed source family and simulated delivery. [Native decision receipts, decoder checks and cost readout](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/native-decisions/README.md)

### Score complete JSON choices on the same native receipt decisions

Complete message likelihood increases swapped-code agreement from 0/24 to 6/24 on Resolution 2553, but reduces mean content-aware documentary release from 36.61 to 34.63. Canonical-only scoring reaches 35.41. The separate role swaps show losses from both the later desk and Belgium. A forecast instructs the high-delivery UK desk to expect Belgium's inclusion passage. It switches to the process passage after receiving Germany's process note. That adds 2.7075 points against Belgium's actual funding choice and loses 0.5077 if Belgium follows the forecast. The decoder scores 464 complete wire sequences and the scorer verifies 276 policy outcomes on one exposed synthetic channel. [Matched decoder, partner forecast and role swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/native-decisions/complete-wire/README.md)

### Fit a shared-return head over the native receipt margins

Fit three shared coefficients on Resolution 2553 and freeze them before 48 new Resolution 2422 calls. The stochastic training objective improves by 0.4143 points, while greedy content release falls from 36.6055 for raw Qwen to 36.2912 for the hybrid. On 2422 content views the hybrid reaches 50.7019, versus 41.4038 for raw Qwen and 51.3013 for both fixed rules. The fitted head without Qwen also reaches 51.3013. Removing the native margins while keeping the hybrid coefficients restores the ceiling. The margins erased Peru's observation-dependent choice at the lower staffing prior. Fixed receipt-aware selection remains the strongest tested controller for this docket. These are source-bound scores with modeled delivery and new native probes on an exposed archive. [Frozen fit, 84 transfer checks and component swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/native-decisions/reward-head/README.md)

### Learn what to review from the downstream team return

Fit a four-feature review policy on six event families at a time and test on the seventh, holding all its pair and triple grades outside the policy input. With one extra review, the learned policy makes exactly the optimistic heuristic's twenty choices. Its mean net scores are 48.34 and 46.70 under uniform and correlated inventory, versus 48.87 and 47.12 for the fixed pair selected from the training families. It wins on Myanmar and loses on climate, tying the other five families. Best-in-hindsight review has only 0.32 and 0.24 points of mean headroom over that fixed rule. Removing Resolution 2422 also removes the learned policy's advantage over immediate forwarding. The replay uses exposed writer records and controls, seven held-family folds, and simulated delivery. [Protocol, exact gradients and fold-level results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-kit/reader-use/writer-aware-bundles/reward-review/README.md)

### Trace a false consensus through a split vote on Resolution 2739

The UNBench author-test vote rows and official record establish a 12–0–3 adoption. The Japanese–US co-sponsors wanted continued reporting to inform Council deliberations, and Algeria valued the reporting despite abstaining over the earlier resolution's implementation. In both desk orders, the peer-aware note repeats an unsupported comparison from the opening note. Peer finals score 14/70 raw in each order, versus 28 for independent drafts and 28 or 56 for pooled solo. Both solo finals preserve the reporting overlap. All six fail release under one assistant review. Nine of twelve logical notes are capped. The 16-call study gives a new historical record diagnostic outside the frozen release and does not fit a policy. [Source joins, blind packets and matched evidence traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/README.md)

### Ask the receiving desk to check the opening against its own record

With both archived Resolution 2739 openings reused, give the second desk the same source-check instruction with and without the peer message. Both peer notes complete and repeat the unsupported first-note comparison. Both final records preserve the error. The independent sponsors' note names a missing local source for Algeria's rationale, and the chair still adopts the opening inference. All four new finals complete within the word cap and fail the source guard. Peer raw lower bounds remain 14/70 while independent bounds fall from 28 to 14. A shorter, complete reply can transmit the same attribution error. These eight calls use one exposed event, an unchanged chair and provisional assistant marks. [Locked intervention and exact-span traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/source-check/README.md)

### Supply the chair with an owner-verified correction

Cross deleting the opening's unsupported comparison with giving the chair an authored, source-checked reply in both Resolution 2739 orders. The combined edits recover Algeria's sovereignty qualification, and the verified sponsors' note restores their reporting rationale. The unchanged chair still invents an Algeria-specific sovereignty claim or upgrades a gap in the sponsors' statement into an explicit speaker claim. All eight final records fail release, including both archived controls. Four of the six new finals also exceed 130 words. For this chair and docket, the released team reward cannot distinguish the tested note edits. [Matched final prompts, exact grades and factorial effects](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/correction-bank/README.md)

### Compare a scope-instructed chair with an owner-bound composer

Keep all eight Resolution 2739 note pairs fixed. A chair instruction requiring support from both owned notes still earns zero release throughout. The raw score rises seven points in four conditions and falls seven in four. A separate composer uses an authored public lead and exact, hand-reviewed role excerpts. It releases 42/70 with the original notes and 49/70 with the verified reply, regardless of whether the opening comparison was removed. Both title swaps and withholding suppress the affected excerpts. The seven-point difference supplies a reward for verified reply delivery, and a fixed reply rule reaches that ceiling. This is one exposed case with manual admission. [Chair prompts, grades and released-reward table](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/correction-bank/chair-scope/README.md)

### Learn whether to forward and approve a source judgment

Jointly fit a proposer and a reviewer from shared admission reward using real archived Qwen margins on 19 claims from four event families. In held-family replay the greedy fit earns 0.3263 net versus 0.3888 for simple agreement. On eight new Resolution 2739 claims both systems reach 16/16 decisions, zero wrong admissions and 0.4656 net across the two mappings. Single-desk admission makes three false admissions and pooled logits make two. The fixed and fitted policies make the same held decisions. Both model mappings also make the same archival Mali scope error, which neither agreement policy detects. These are source judgments with modeled payoff and an exposed held docket. [Frozen fit, native probes and action ceiling](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/learned-admission/README.md)

### Turn scoped source judgments into a publishable Resolution 2739 record

Route the learned and fixed admission decisions into the same case-specific composer. A 42-point public lead plus both admitted reporting positions reaches 63/70. Four fresh frozen Qwen calls check the missing Algerian abstention rationale and an Algeria-specific overreach. The learned and fixed gates then release 70/70 in both mappings with both role packets, 49 with either and 42 with neither. Pooled logits tie the released results despite wrongly treating an unresolved overreach as a contradiction, which this affirmative-only composer does not publish. Single-reader full receipts score 70 or zero by mapping. Both packets jointly earn the 14-point overlap item. This is an exposed, adaptively completed documentary replay with authored claims and the same-assistant source key. [All receipt masks, native probes and final records](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/learned-admission/documentary-replay/README.md)

### Transfer the frozen gate to Myanmar and compile its public procedural fact

On eight fresh Myanmar Resolution 2669 claims, the learned gate gets 14/16 decisions, fixed agreement 12/16 and pooled margins 16/16. None of these three makes a wrong admission. The learned gate recovers the procedural-versus-national role distinction in one mapping, giving full-receipt scores of 63 and 70, versus 63 and 63 for fixed agreement and 70 and 70 for pooling. An independent first read publishes a false ASEAN attribution in one mapping. The frozen case already supplies the chair-role source publicly. Compiling that passage into all arms yields 70 in both mappings for learned, pooled and fixed agreement. This is a case-specific source-bound replay on an exposed family excluded from the gate fit. [Native margins, held records and public-source sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/myanmar-frozen-admission/README.md)

### Move contradiction off the shared answer letter and price a recheck

Collect 54 new two-token reads across the 19 archived role judgments and eight Myanmar claims using balanced option cycles. One new Mali mapping correctly reads Russia's plan for the final sanctions extension. Three-read unanimity defers instead of wrongly contradicting it, lowering wrong decisive archival admissions from one to zero at unchanged 15/19 accuracy. Pooling and majority retain the target error. A joint proposer and reviewer fit sees the actual recheck payoff with one paid query. It leaves every held decision unchanged from the first read and loses net through its extra queries. On Myanmar it earns 0.6125 against 0.6250 for the first read and 0.7250 for the confidence rule fixed on the archives. The prior archive reads were on CPU, with the new reads and the old Myanmar reads on GPU. [Mapped logits, recheck policies and exact comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/relation-label-balance/README.md)

### Train the reviewer before pricing the recheck

Split the original joint fit into reviewer-first and proposer-second stages using the same observed source-label channels and 160 updates per head. The staged fit calls more often and makes the same held decisions as the joint fit and first read alone. Its net falls to 0.3354 on archive folds and 0.5250 on Myanmar. With either learned reviewer frozen, no one-read query improves on the better no-query action in any of the 27 held rows. Giving the reviewer hindsight over its existing legal replies recovers useful queries on four archive rows and two Myanmar rows. The missing corrective replies include India's procedural presidency and the contradiction of a claimed affirmative vote. This is an exposed CPU replay with modeled query costs. [Stage fits, component swaps and counterfactual reply payoffs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/relation-label-balance/STAGED_CREDIT.md)

### Give the corrective reviewer frozen source representations

Recapture 81 exact three-option views, including projected hidden states, on frozen Qwen3.5-4B. Fit six components on each fold's training families and append them to the reviewer input. Compared on identical recaptured logits, this raises the reviewer training objective from 0.4572 to 0.5263 and changes no held decision, query count or available greedy-reply payoff across 27 cases. Myanmar remains 0.525 net against 0.625 for the first read alone. The recapture also changes 13 of 19 CPU-to-GPU top-two margins with zero label flips. All 62 GPU-to-GPU centered logits match. A training-selected confidence control changes its rule and drops from 0.725 to 0.600 on Myanmar. [Matched logits, held fits and margin audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/relation-label-balance/semantic-credit/README.md)

### Learn one retry from private link hints and archived final-record scores

Fit two hint senders and a chair using exact team return over archived writer outcomes. On matched transfer to Myanmar and Resolution 2739, learned retry improves silence from 53.3330 to 57.9189 and trails shared-prior fixed routing at 57.9592. Suppressing the learned hints leaves released points and retry rate unchanged on transfer and raises net by their cost. In Haiti, a retry can lower a 49-point starter record to zero. A post hoc check skips a retry when the public singleton preview is no better than the currently delivered record. It raises archive macro net by 0.1385, raises Haiti by 0.7492 and leaves both transfer dockets unchanged. The 232 held conditions use real archived writer scores, simulated transport and modeled costs. [Team fit, exact branch replay and dominance guard](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/private-retry/README.md)

### Price private hints and restrict learning to the hidden combined score

Enumerate every one-bit code at each receipt state. With the full archived score bank, paid hints add at most 0.0483 points in matched archive folds and 0.0653 on matched transfer. Every gain comes before either remaining packet arrives. Use the published singleton scores to solve that state analytically. Fit the chair only where retry reaches an unknown combined record. The selective fit improves the original team's net and reaches the priced ceiling in all 16 Myanmar and Resolution 2739 replay conditions. A fixed interaction-offset rule using the same first-stage solver reaches the same ceiling and slightly leads the fit on archive folds. These are exposed score banks and simulated delivery. [Exact envelope, selective fit and matched fixed controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/private-retry/SELECTIVE.md)

### Price a review before retrying the missing source packet

After the first receipt, let the chair pay for the combined record’s exact archived release score before deciding whether to retry. At a review price of 0.75, a fixed singleton-score rule gains 0.6931 net points over the same direct retry fallback across six archive families. Resolution 2422 accounts for the gain. The sampled residual fit gains 0.3211 and the greedy residual changes no retry decisions. On the exposed Myanmar and Resolution 2739 transfer cases, the greedy fit and fixed rule skip review and tie the fallback at 57.9940. These are modeled review costs and archived scores. The frozen benchmark is the public documentary suite. [Review value, family breakdowns and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/private-retry/REVIEW_VALUE.md)

### Test whether source-owner reviewers can replace an exact score preview

On Resolution 2422, run pooled and source-scoped frozen Qwen3.5-4B reviewers on the seven multi-source drafts within the original word limit. The first prompt yields one parseable result in 46 calls. A numbered checklist and post hoc exact compact decoding recover 45 of 46 follow-up verdicts. Both owners give identical labels to the two Peru–Serbia drafts. The reversed draft releases 55, while the forward draft fails the archived attribution guard because “Conversely” can imply disagreement. Selecting both gains five points in one receipt and loses 50 in the other. Across eight multi-source receipts, source-owner review averages 45.875 released points, pooled review and the checked fallback tie at 51.500, and exact routing reaches 52.875. Pricing calls widens the loss. This isolates a failure in the observed messages on one exposed docket before using them for policy training. [Prompts, launches, exact replay and costs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/private-retry/scoped-review/README.md)

### Bound learned dispatch from actual reviewer replies

Exhaustive dispatch on the two Resolution 2422 writer orders gives 50.2160 net for the checked fallback and 51.0800 for an archive-calibrated policy with public format flags. The pooled and owner replies add zero to that policy, even when free. A 72-word draft exposes the order when all three notes arrive. The remaining exact-grade gap is 0.2800 points, entirely at the Peru–Serbia pair receipt. A new binary question about unsupported links returns A under both mappings on all 13 cases. Requiring a quoted fault flags “By contrast” on both warranted and unwarranted controls and leaves the two pair dispatches at fallback. This combines 65 new frozen Qwen3.5-4B calls, archived grades and modeled delivery. [Observation bounds, review prompts and replays](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/private-retry/scoped-review/signal-value/README.md)

### Coordinate authenticated reports across a shared Iraq and Syria agenda

Give each desk one report across both frozen workshop dockets. Exact certificates credit a shorter-term first card in half the worlds and require a second card elsewhere. On 48 modeled held availability and transport settings, joint fitting earns 0.299660 with simultaneous reports and 0.332564 with receipt feedback. The matched fixed sequential rule reaches the 0.333387 ceiling. A dependent fit adds the public proof status. It keeps weighted decisions at 0.392022, eliminates 0.143865 redundant reports per agenda and reaches 0.341196 net. A fixed proof-aware follower matches it with the learned opener. A fixed opener raises the team to 0.342081. This is a verified action diagnostic with simulated delivery and priorities, without new language generation or final-record grading. [Certificates, return fits, held policies and swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/shared-agenda/README.md)

### Fit the workshop opener's downstream value and test the writer separately

Hold the proof-aware follower fixed and fit on conditional team returns. Accounting for standalone value, expected completion value and both fees reaches the exact optimum on all 192 replays across four fees. A refit on the old features also reaches the optimum at the training fee and trails in 19 of 48 settings at 0.25. The accounting embeds the payoff structure and uses declared rates and a known follower. The second card raises aggregate raw coverage from 56 to 70–84 but drops released points from 56 to 28 across the four pairs. Iraq regains substantive details in one pair and exceeds the cap. Syria loses a source guard in one pair by misreporting the reason for the nine-month draft's defeat. The two identical-input single-report pairs repeat deterministically. These are same-assistant grades of eight actual writer calls on exposed workshops. [Locked prompts, final texts, grades and verification](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/shared-agenda/documentary/README.md)

### Protect the official record and train the follower on documentary reward

Keep the certificate, source history, workshop package and limits of authority fixed. On four matched workshop pairs, the protected Qwen paragraph releases 224/280 with one report and 266/280 with both, versus 56 and 28 for the free writer. Literal transport reaches 224 and 280. The generated Syria overlap on duration omits both exact six-month conditions in one of four full receipts. Freeze the fitted opener and give the optional report credit for the measured increment in released points. At 0.06 per send, the cross-family fitted follower gains 0.008533 net on the scoped writer, behind the fixed 14-point rule at 0.009467. It ties exact planning on the literal writer at 0.014517. This uses six native model calls, assistant grades on two exposed families and a modeled last-report replay. ### Transfer the documentary follower to Myanmar's scheduling workshop

Lock the historical mandate and the warranted slot for four one-versus-two-calendar pairs in the existing fit-excluded Myanmar workshop. Literal records score 280/280 in both arms because the original rubric credits identifying an unavailable calendar. Requiring the actual second calendar changes the one-card score to 252/280. The scoped writer releases zero with one calendar and 140 with both, including unsupported exact claims about withheld availability and two full-receipt failures. At the nominal fee the frozen follower loses 0.0600 net by sending redundant literal reports under the original rubric. Scaling the estimate by available rubric points reaches the literal exact optimum under both contracts at all four fees, tying the fixed rubric rule. The adjustment loses useful scoped reports because rubric opportunity alone misses rescue from writer failure. This is a third exposed workshop family, six native calls and simulated transport. ### Refit the opening grade and decide when to pay for peer visibility

Freeze the recipient in the outer-space grade game and fit a send gate from downstream returns on four existing development grids. On four reserve grids, paid sends fall from 99.67% to 41.05% and net rises from 0.73805 to 0.74769, while certificate coverage falls 0.80 percentage points. Fitting the choice of grade and send action jointly raises net to 0.75337, ahead of every matched independent optimum. A uniform continuation forecast earns the same net return at the original fee. The frozen 12-message Qwen transport reproduces the score. On exposed Mali and Myanmar-linked fictional grade grids, the new opener matches the best response to the frozen recipient but trails both full-team optima. Those optima need no paid peer message on either grid. [Exact dispatches, fits, message costs and transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/README.md)

### Fit the recipient from counterfactual action rankings

Freeze the selective opener and train on the original four outer-space development grids. With balanced worlds on the four reserve grids, the fixed forecast raises net from 0.753372 to 0.764411. The value fit reaches 0.758625, and the ranking fit reaches the fixed-opener ceiling at 0.768750. The ranking gain comes entirely from grid 198 and reverses under uniform worlds, where its macro falls to 0.7500 versus 0.7545 fixed. It ties the fixed forecast on Mali and loses to it on Myanmar under both priors. A 0.02 forecast-regret check ties fixed throughout. The exact simulation keeps message budgets, verification and opener choices constant. Archived Qwen grade transport reproduces the reserve scores. [Recipient fits, exact regret and transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/RECIPIENT.md)

### Infer a changing world mixture from ordinary peer messages

Freeze both recipient policies and the opener. On reserved grid 198, where the ranking gain reverses under uniform worlds, 32 unlabeled local cards and peer receipts give 93.85% mixture identification and 0.758340 net, versus 0.750750 fixed and 0.759429 with the regime declared. Local cards alone reach 0.755509. Other reserve grids have no regime-dependent action benefit, and the rank fit loses on Myanmar under either mixture. Crossing both mixtures with peer delivery at 0.4 or 0.8 gives 0.743804 reserve macro net after 16 turns versus 0.741054 fixed. The planner that assumes 0.8 ties the joint-inference planner on the tested regimes. These are exact passive-history simulations with a frozen opening rule and known candidate regimes. [History likelihoods, posterior policies and stress replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/HISTORY.md)

### Score final records from all paired receipts on reserve grid 198

Enumerate 17 chair views induced by either recipient. The adaptive policy increases literal settled release from 54.653 to 55.184 under an equal mixture of balanced and uniform worlds, with the same report cost and 70 documentary points in both arms. The scoped Qwen writer releases zero on every view because of source errors, ambiguity or a cap. Shrinking the proof to outcome counts and fixing the grade disclosures earns 70 on three of four pivotal views. The remaining explanation calls its valid impasse “no feasible disposition,” which receives an uncertain guard. With that grade, the pivotal contribution changes from +0.531 points with literal rendering to −0.845 with the tally writer. The four-view test does not measure a full tally-writer deployment. [Complete records, annotations and paired audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/documentary-198/README.md)

### Verify the rendered record across the reserve and transfer panels

Render all 117 chair receipts reachable under the two recipient policies on four outer-space grids and the canonical Mali and Myanmar-linked grids. An independent parser recomputes evidence and checks 367 legal receipt combinations, including 32 with tied winners, plus 2,362 invalid mutations or inputs. The 32-observation mixture-only switch improves reserve settled coverage from 54.1181 to 54.2509 and regresses on transfer. Selecting the rank policy only when the expected-payoff model favors it preserves the reserve gain and matches the fixed policy on both transfer grids. The renderer reproduces the earlier graded literal grid-198 outcome. Broader coverage is machine-checked, with no new generation or independent prose grades. [CLI, records, verifier and selector](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/verified-records/README.md)

### Calibrate high-grade and low-grade cross-play from prior chair receipts

The two unfamiliar openers generate identical recipient-side histories, even when they share some paid reports. On six exposed grade grids, four archived chair receipts raise the silent condition from 0.602652 to 0.614652 net, near its known-partner ceiling of 0.614705. Shared-peer cross-play improves from 0.578839 to 0.586018. At 0.003 points per access, adaptive full-receipt review earns 0.613394 and 0.585316 with 0.419 and 0.234 reads on average. Presence-only metadata has zero information. Grade-only records recover most of the value for this pair. The same current evidence and final verifier are used throughout. These are modeled partner hypotheses and mechanically verified records, with no new language output or weight update. [Exact cross-play, access costs and receipt checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/verified-records/CROSSPLAY.md)

### Calibrate recipients when the partner falls outside the rule library

On 78 synthetic conditions across six exposed workshop grids, a selector uses the original model when it can explain the archived chair receipts, a mixture model when both pure rules fail, and direct reward estimates for receipts outside the rules’ combined support. At 128 observations, net reward is 0.59971 versus 0.59345 for the two-rule library and 0.60005 with the partner known. The gain includes median and public-priority partners, while the equal high/low mixture stays near its ceiling. Reads are unpriced and final records are mechanically verified. Transfer to unseen diplomatic records and language quality remain unmeasured. [Protocols, replay and results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/selective-disclosure/verified-records/UNKNOWN_PARTNER.md)

### Learn evidence selection from one sampled documentary score per exchange

Train the earlier, later and third delegates using one terminal score per rollout and a shared three-coefficient policy. At 14,400 sampled returns, the content-aware fit matches the enumerated gradient in all 48 held synthetic menus and 24 exposed Resolution 2553 conditions. It gains 0.10206 synthetic points over the sampled policy without peer receipt visibility and 0.05112 over an equally informed fixed rule. Keeping the later desk fixed increases the synthetic mean from 20.00748 to 20.05160. On the 2553 source records the learned and fixed content policies both score 34.53884/70. The experiment uses authored priorities, simulated delivery and a source-bound renderer. [Protocol, replays and role swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/evidence-choice/receipt-conditioned/sampled-return/README.md)

### Test separate listener weights and check what the shared head can express

With the same 14,400-rollout budget, a listener-only adjustment loses on one of 96 fresh synthetic menus and ties on 95 against the shared sampled fit. It makes the same decisions on all 24 Resolution 2553 conditions at 34.53884/70. An exposed feasibility audit uses the exact best actions to find shared coefficients that reach the 34.64402 ceiling throughout. Those coefficients downweight the uniform partner forecast and score 19.65996 on fresh synthetic menus, compared with 20.05466 for the sampled policy. The result establishes shared-head capacity on this case and shows a trade-off for the historical witness. It motivates testing the distribution of partner forecasts and private inventories before adding more role parameters. [Role fit and representation audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/evidence-choice/receipt-conditioned/sampled-return/listener-residual/README.md)

### Train with private opening cards and separate partner links

A forced private opener and varied availability priors reproduce the prior fit's per-menu scores on both held synthetic panels and all 36 archived 2553 and 2422 conditions. Making each desk's clauses complement different partners during training changes one documentary decision. Belgium switches from funding to inclusion at 75% delivery and reaches the 36.26563/70 ceiling, up 0.46875. The mean falls on both 96-menu synthetic panels, including the matched separate-link panel. This is an adaptive case-structure test from sampled documentary rewards, with no change to the writer. [Protocols, exact dispatch and results](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/cooperative-rollout-fit/evidence-choice/receipt-conditioned/sampled-return/listener-residual/private-opener/README.md)

### Replay the changed Belgium dispatch through the native chair

On the one Resolution 2553 condition changed by training, the fixed Qwen3.5-4B chair produces 12 briefs from every distinct receipt where Belgium arrives. Inclusion gains 0.78516 expected raw points over funding and zero released points, versus a +0.46875 advantage with the source-bound renderer. Ten briefs confuse South Africa's role as draft lead with authority over reform mandates. Two Belgium-only briefs have uncertain guards. Clearing both gives a release contrast of −0.23438 to +0.04688, depending on the remaining rubric ambiguity. This is an exposed, selected case with authored delegate cards, simulated delivery and same-assistant grades. [Prompts, native tokens and span-level adjudication](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/learned-send-writer/README.md)

### Distinguish draft leadership from assistance authority in the native chair

Append one source-backed clarification and regenerate the same 12 Resolution 2553 prompts. Draft-lead and assistance-authority conflations fall from 10 to one, and two inclusion briefs clear the release gate. The inclusion card's expected margin is +0.18750 raw and +7.75781 released. Clearing the uncertain funding brief in the highest-weight branch reverses the released difference to −0.78516. The eight uncertainty adjudications span −1.53516 to +11.13281. This is a targeted one-case prompt repair, with residual writer errors and the downstream result sensitive to same-assistant guard judgments. [Matched prompts, tokens and adjudication](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/learned-send-writer/authority-clarification/README.md)

### Preserve the public record and scope the native delegation writer

On the same 12 Resolution 2553 receipt inputs, verbatim public facts plus Qwen-generated delegation text release seven briefs versus two after the earlier authority clarification. Verbatim delegation notes release all 12 and have higher expected absolute scores in both arms. The learned inclusion choice gains 8.625 expected released points under the main native grades and 0.75 with verbatim notes. Clearing the borderline funding comparison in the heaviest branch reverses the native contrast. Across all 16 guard resolutions it ranges from −8.46094 to +14.17969. This isolates a public-source preservation benefit and shows that native comparison fidelity still drives instability in the downstream reward. [Native tokens, exact replay and scoring](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/learned-send-writer/public-record-anchor/README.md)

### Check whether final release supplies a stable direction for training

Across three exposed archival dockets, nine of 25 paired message contrasts keep a strict positive direction under every tested writer and admissible archived grade. The other 16 warrant abstention. The Resolution 2553 inclusion replacement has a native reward band of −8.46094 to +14.17969. On Resolution 2686, both source-scoped writers favor all 12 one-note additions, versus just two strictly positive additions across all four writers. Leaving one writer out still yields wrong directions on four of 25 issued labels on Resolution 2475 and two of 12 on the full Resolution 2686 panel. This measures reward attribution from archived grades, with no fresh training or independent rating. [Exact bands and held-writer controls](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/README.md)

### Fit a send policy using effects that agree across archived writers

In two 2475/2686 leave-event-out folds, an exact shared-policy fit skips ambiguous actor updates. It transfers better than single-writer fitting on the four free-form variants. It scores below the fixed standalone-credit policy on all three source-preserving variants. A simple threshold chosen to match training send counts reproduces every held action in the 2686-to-2475 fold. In the other fold the agreement fit beats the threshold on the two free-form writers and loses on both source-preserving writers. This tests learned send decisions in archive replay with modeled delivery and fees. [Fits, mask checks and volume control](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/policy-fit/README.md)

### Measure the remaining value of receipts with source-preserving writers

Across 96 modeled scenes per case on Resolutions 2475 and 2686, the best receipt-aware policy adds 0.05094 and 0.01334 mean net points over the best static plan. The fixed forecast using the authored checklist attains both ceilings. A shared residual trained on the other event falls short. Under checklist perturbations, the fit leads the forecast only on 2686 when all relation credit is omitted, by 0.13159 mean points, with 18 scene losses. The additive bank has zero benefit from past receipts. This isolates finite headroom under an explicit source-preserving writer and free receipt visibility. [Complete orders, exhaustive decisions and checklist shifts](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/receipt-coordination/README.md)

### Build a receipt-sensitive authority comparison on Resolution 2721

In an editorial workshop based on the Afghanistan envoy vote, choose one adopted clause to pair with a delivered Russian or African-members statement. The former tests consultation versus a national consent condition. The latter tests complementarity versus displacement of UNAMA. At 75% delivery and equal availability, content raises the achievable final grade from 50.6875 to 56.875 with 22 points assigned to a grounded comparison. Both frozen numeric controllers tie the fixed content rule in all 12 settings. Verify nine records and reject 135 mutations. UNBench joins all 15 official votes. The pinned adopted and vetoed UNSC-Bench files have no matching identifier. This is a source-based simulated test with authored clause-pair features. [Sources, exact ceilings and frozen transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/afghanistan-2721-source-contrast/README.md)

### Decode the Resolution 2721 authority choice from received text

Across eight original observations, paired Qwen3.5-4B choices are exact, individual calls score 13/16, and the lexical rule also gets 8/8. A dependent stress set removes overlap or makes it favor the wrong clause. The paired decoder gets 4/4, raw calls get 7/8, and overlap gets 1/4. With wrong overlap at 75% delivery and a balanced prior, the paired decoder reaches 56.875/70 versus 50.6875 for arrival-only and 44.5 for lexical choice. The small mapped margin on one stressed observation and the selected two-source setup limit generalization. This measures frozen text-conditioned dispatch through the same authored renderer. [Raw logits, matched variants and full branch scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/afghanistan-2721-source-contrast/text-decoder/README.md)

### Learn when to pay for a second answer order on Resolution 2721

With the first answer order randomized, a policy fit on the eight original observations scores 52.82660 net on the held lexical stress variants. Keeping the first answer scores 52.19563 and always rereading scores 51.13875. The policy gains 0.55494 against a random reread at the same cost, with no losses in that matched comparison. A single canonical-order call leads at 53.49500. This tests cost-aware policy learning from archived native margins and source-bound reward on two positions, without updating Qwen. [Fitting traces and matched-call audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/afghanistan-2721-source-contrast/text-decoder/optional-read/README.md)

### Test whether the native chair preserves the benefit of the Resolution 2721 editor

Across nine receipt sets, the fixed Qwen3.5-4B chair abstains on all five incomplete inputs. Two comparisons are uncertain and one falsely assigns the African members' rationale to the Council. At 75% receipt and a balanced prior, reading the peer text yields 17.50/70 with the native comparison, versus 31.00 for the best arrival-only policy on the same writer. The authored renderer gives 56.875 versus 50.6875. Clearing the uncertain Russian comparison can produce a native tie, while no resolution of both uncertain grades yields a gain over the best arrival control. This is a same-assistant review on one exposed set of source cards. [Nine dialogues, exact spans and counterfactual scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/afghanistan-2721-source-contrast/native-comparison/README.md)

### Check whether a native support gate preserves the certified Resolution 2721 comparisons

Across 14 label-reversed candidates, averaged Qwen logits reject all ten unsupported claims and admit one of four supported claims. The missed registered UNAMA comparison reduces the content-informed score from the exact rule's 56.875 to 50.6875 and removes its gain over arrival-only at 75% delivery. Requiring agreement drops both comparisons. A threshold selected to avoid false admits on the 2475 archive blocks every 2721 probe, while the reverse threshold admits five unsupported 2475 claims. These are frozen judgments over authored source pairs. [Source checks, model gates and cross-docket cutoff replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/afghanistan-2721-source-contrast/bound-review/README.md)

### Center the native receipt reader before a shared-return fit

Fit on Resolution 2553 with the same-mapping no-observation margin subtracted from the editor's receipt margin. On the four archived Resolution 2422 conditions in both orders, the centered fit reaches 51.30125/70, versus 50.85250 for the raw single-order fit and 51.15063 for fixed centered weights. The existing typed rule reaches the same ceiling. Arrival-only and no-observation scores remain below the strongest fixed rule. On Resolution 2721, text-only centering improves 20/24 to 22/24, while the paired decoder and rubric-feature rules reach 24/24. These are outcome-trained heads over frozen logits and simulated source-bound scores. [Training and matched transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/native-decisions/reward-head/receipt-centered/README.md)

### Hide relationship bonuses from the Resolution 2422 text reader

Keep the note texts and standalone values in 40 matched native requests but omit the joint bonus table. Qwen favors literal B in 39/40. The frozen centered fit scores 50.81172/70 across both orders, versus 51.00000 for standalone and lexical rules. Its reversed-order score of 51.22594 falls to 50.39750 in the canonical order. Averaging both centered margins gives 50.84937. The previously supplied-checklist rule reaches 51.30125. This is a measured transfer limitation on one exposed docket, with same frozen renderer and no new backbone updates. [Exact prompt transform, forward logits and score replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/native-decisions/reward-head/receipt-centered/text-without-pair-values/README.md)

### Score complete source passages with the relationship bonuses hidden

Replace arbitrary labels with the two exact candidate passages on the same 40 Resolution 2422 request states. Raw summed and per-token likelihood agree across all 20 display-order pairs and choose the standalone option throughout, scoring 51/70. Subtracting the same-order no-observation reference lowers the content mean to 50.47750, with losses in seven of eight scenes and agreement in only 10/20 pairs. The known-checklist ceiling remains 51.30125. The result uses 80 frozen sequence evaluations and the same authored score bank. [Token-level likelihoods and exact reward replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/native-decisions/reward-head/receipt-centered/text-without-pair-values/passage-likelihood/README.md)

### Learn omitted relationship bonuses from final scores and receipt IDs

On 2422 and both 2553 orders, train a pair-reward model from public and standalone credit, the observed receipt IDs, and one actual scalar per simulated exchange. With 256 scores the fit reaches 49.06903/70 on 2422 and 35.02892 and 34.68623 on 2553. The exact ceilings are 49.06903, 35.03723 and 34.68665. The fit improves over standalone and receipt lookup on all three means. Choosing exploratory actions for expected change in receipt-feature covariance adds 0.013–0.039 points at 64 scores over matched uniform exploration, with mixed results at shorter budgets. These are within-docket fits with held transport conditions and deterministic source-bound scores. [Reward fits and active receipt design](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/receipt-reward-fit/README.md)

### Cross receipt policies with native and source-bound terminal rewards

Fit the same 2721 receipt learner under the native, source-bound and mixed writer rewards. With optional silence and 256 scores, the native-reward policy increases strict native release from 19.80545 to 41.76341, while reducing delivery of the supporting pairs from 48.80% to zero and lowering source-bound release from 53.96227 to 41.76341. It sends a crossed clause after Russia's note and withholds after the African members' note. Mandatory forwarding and mixed reward retain some coverage. Alternate grades for the earlier native comparisons reduce the observed reward advantage. This isolates a writer-induced training incentive on one exposed docket. [Paired histories, policy actions and guard sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/receipt-reward-fit/writer-shift/README.md)

### Preserve source acquisition and learn the chair's composition choice

On 2721, the source-trained editor still delivers every available supporting pair, while the learned chair selects verbatim and restores 44.5/70 release at balanced 75% delivery. The native-reward editor with the same chair nets 42.8125 and never delivers a supporting pair. On 2553, an exact-receipt chair gate retains useful native drafts and raises inclusion from 34.28125 to 36.60156 net conditional on Belgium arriving. Holding the gate fixed while changing to earlier writers cuts the inclusion score to 28.16406 or 18.88281. This separates acquisition from composition and limits the gate claim to its calibrated writer and receipt bank. [Choice fits, source coverage and writer swaps](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/receipt-reward-fit/writer-shift/composition-choice/README.md)

## Post-draft release selection under writer and docket shifts

A matched replay compared 69 archived drafts with their existing fallbacks on
Resolutions 2553, 2721, 2686 and 2475. A ridge gate saw both texts and used
only length, lexical retention and novelty, number changes, and literal prefix
preservation. The target was the difference in strict released points. Training
omitted the entire test docket or, in a second check on 2553, the test writer.

The gate recovered neither of the two native improvements. Both improvements
came from one 2553 writer, so each holdout containing them had zero improvements
in training. It also dropped held-2686 release from 41.125 to 40.25 by admitting
a rewrite worth 28 instead of 35. On 2475 its only admissions released identical
text. The literal fallback remains the supported default on this archive. A
positive transfer test needs genuinely beneficial native drafts from multiple
event families and writer variants, with scoring fixed before generation. The
selector replay uses archived assistant marks and tests neither live generation
nor learned communication. Benchmark v1.5 still reproduces its frozen verification
and replay byte for byte. [Replay and scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/draft-aware-composition/README.md)

## Calibrating synthesis against the standalone facts on Resolution 2664

The adopted resolution assigned a monitoring role to the 1267 Committee.
India judged its request for more proactive Monitoring Team involvement and
robust reporting not fully addressed. A record that explains that difference
can earn 70/70, versus 58/70 for the same standalone facts. The shortest
authored synthesis adds four words. Four material misreadings and two
source-deletion controls trigger the release guard. Safe partial records
retain the points available under their actual evidence.

The 12 contrast packets use the v1.5 final judge instruction and span
validator. They are assistant-authored calibration fixtures based on the UN
meeting and resolution, and the event was previously exposed in UNBench
workshop development. They establish a second historical event family with a
demonstrable synthesis opportunity. The archive still needs actual paired
writer responses and independent scoring to assess transfer and calibrate a
release gate. [Blinded packets, source locators, and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/humanitarian-2664-admission/README.md)

### Run the Resolution 2664 drafting crossover

Frozen Qwen3.5-4B wrote six final briefs across independent notes, peer-informed
notes and pooled solo, in both speaker orders. All six expressed the intended
oversight distinction and all six failed the release guard. Four reversed
what the exemption permitted, and two treated a due-diligence request as a
stronger obligation. The raw difference between peer and independent flipped
from +14 to −14 with order. Released scores were zero across arms. Peer access
also carried a newly invented deadline from the second desk into one final.
This exposed docket supplies observed native failures for future release-gate
calibration. The positive synthesis anchors remain authored feasibility
examples. [Exact briefs, notes, scores, and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/humanitarian-2664-admission/drafting-crossover/README.md)

### Hold the Resolution 2664 factual record fixed and learn what the writer adds

Five frozen Qwen3.5-4B outputs isolate the optional comparison. With India
first, the native append lifts matched release from 58 to 70. With safeguards
first, the chair repeats India's rationale and leaves release at 58.
Neither partial condition nor the empty condition leads to an unsupported
append. The paired sources earn 12 additional points in the India-first
order and zero in the reverse order. Insisting on both receipts before calling
the chair preserves these five scores. This gives a measured native synthesis
positive on a second event family, from a different writing architecture
than the earlier Resolution 2553 positives. The work tests the terminal
reward surface available to cooperating desks. It uses one exposed event,
a fixed authored factual record, and assistant annotations, with no
policy or model updates. [Replay and exact message credit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/humanitarian-2664-admission/source-preserving/README.md)

### Fit cooperative send decisions on Resolution 2664 terminal scores

Train on 64 simulated exchanges scored by the archived source-preserving
writer. The critic that tracks receipt pairs and writer order recovers
the best send action in 12/12 held delivery and order settings, versus
6/12 for the order-pooled and additive critics. Exact deletion credit
matches the order-aware critic with 20 extra reward lookups. At 85% delivery,
sending both sources is preferable when India is shown first 90% of the
time, while silence has higher net utility at 10%. The cost is constructed
to expose this tradeoff. The policy uses known delivery and order
probabilities, and training reuses assistant-graded terminal scores from
one exposed docket. The result demonstrates a cooperative routing fit
and identifies a writer variable whose omission can suppress sends.
[Rollouts and exact held-condition replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/humanitarian-2664-admission/sender-credit-fit/README.md)

### Budget the grading needed to transfer a writer-dependent send policy

A held-docket replay across 74 archived alternatives and five dockets found
zero of three positive drafts with the transferred draft-aware gate. Two
direct audits recovered the Resolution 2664 append gain because there are
only two non-identical candidates. Ranking those candidates for one audit
chose the tie. On 2553, extrapolating one favorable audit to ungraded drafts
hurt mean release, while applying it only to its own draft preserved quality.
On the existing 2664 routing grid, two direct audits raised utility from 30 to
31.1615 by recovering the relationship reward in the India-first condition.
One uniform audit recovered half that gain in expectation. These are
archived assistant grades and a fixed simulated cost, with audit counts
reported separately. [Budgeted admission and routing replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reward-attribution-audit/budgeted-writer-audit/README.md)

### Transfer the source-preserving writer and send policy to Resolution 2739

Under either order of the two complete sources, the fixed record plus
native append releases 56/70. Both native sentences emphasize the vote
difference and leave the shared rationale for continued Council reporting
implicit. The author-reviewed comparison ceiling is 70. The empty and
one-card views abstain. Removing the co-sponsors' view prevents bounded
overlap, including when the adopted action fills the second slot. No draft
introduced unsupported consensus.

The 2664 critic transfers a 12-point synthesis bonus to the corresponding
Algeria-first condition. The target writer realizes zero. The imported
policy sends both sources in six of 12 conditions, increasing factual
coverage but reducing simulated cost-adjusted utility from 42 to 40.
Known delivery, constructed cost, one exposed target docket and assistant
grades limit the claim. [Scored records, crossed evidence, and policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/source-preserving-transfer/README.md)

### Ask the 2739 writer to compare stated purposes before votes

One additional instruction restores Algeria's stated value for the
reports in the co-sponsors-first sentence. It leaves the actual common
purpose implicit. Both source orders stay at 56/70, with zero reward from
the 14-point relation. Both crossed controls remain safe, though the
model now makes a low-value contrast in each. The 64-exchange target fit
learns zero joint bonus and matches the cost-adjusted optimum on 12/12
held transport settings at 42 points, versus 40 for the frozen 2664
policy. If the co-sponsors-first response is awarded full relation
credit, the transferred critic still expects the bonus in the wrong
order. This adapted prompt and archived reward fit measure one exposed
event family under an artificial send cost. [Exact outputs, fit and sensitivity](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/reason-before-vote/README.md)

### Give the 2739 chair exact purpose quotes from two desks

The two desks separately extract the relevant source-owned phrases and
the public-action desk abstains. The same chair sees both original cards,
with or without the quotes. Full-evidence release stays at 56/70 in both
orders and both arms. With one quote and no co-sponsor statement, the
writer makes an unsupported claim of shared purpose and release drops
from 49 to zero. A mechanical requirement for two admitted quotes
prevents that crossed release failure. It leaves the joint terminal
bonus at zero. The quote path adds 368 input tokens for each complete
pair when the extraction calls are charged. These are 11 frozen model
calls and assistant grades on one exposed event.
[Quotes, final records, and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/quoted-purpose/README.md)

### Verify an authored comparison before rendering it

On 2739 and 2664, frozen Qwen support judgments averaged over both label
mappings admit both canonical comparisons and one of the two valid
paraphrases. They reject all six negative controls. One mapping on its
own rejects all positives, while the other accepts both missing-source
controls. The model-supported exact renderer therefore preserves 70/70
on both registered records, versus 56 and 58 for the standalone facts.
The exact-source registry reaches the same score without model calls.
The comparison is available to the verifier as an authored candidate,
so this tests semantic admission after candidate construction. The
operative benchmark release remains frozen.
[20 forward judgments, controls, and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/relation-support-transfer/README.md)

### Generate the relation's purpose instead of asking for a full sentence

On 2739 the constrained phrase writer produces “to keep the Council
informed” in both complete orders. With the fixed attribution and vote
frame and exact two-source gate, both release at 70/70 versus the prior
free-writing score of 56. The phrase writer produces the same answer
when the second delegation's evidence is missing. The source gate
blocks both cases. The averaged semantic verifier rejects both correct
composed records and admits one crossed candidate before the source
gate blocks it. In the 64-exchange sender replay, only the mechanical
gate exposes the 14-point relationship reward and raises fitted utility
from 42 to 47.135. Always sending matches that utility in the tested
settings. This is a case-specific frame on an exposed docket, with
assistant grades and a constructed send cost. [Native phrases, gates, and cooperative fit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reporting-2739/generated-purpose/README.md)

### Transfer the short relation payload to authentic but mismatched cards

On 2553 Qwen generates the shared concern about rushed negotiations
in both complete orders and adds six points, from 31 to 37. It
also emits three unsupported overlaps when two real national cards
are present but their specific concerns differ. Checking the exact
relation pair restores the fixed 31- or 33-point output on these views.
A reply policy fit on 64 replayed exchanges now chooses Germany's
process card after the UK concern arrives and its Fund card otherwise.
It averages 30.632 points over the held grid, versus 30.107 for
always-process under the same renderer. An identity-only gate has the
same fitted values on the chosen path and can lose all points in an
off-policy branch. This remains one previously exposed transfer docket
with assistant grading and simulated transport. [Source controls, grades,
and reply fit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/concern-transfer/README.md)

### Route the German reply from unfamiliar wording

Replace the known first-message receipt with text and use terminal reward
samples on three canonical contexts to calibrate a frozen Qwen margin.
The fitted router sends the process card on all four supported rewrites
and on five of eight negatives. It ties a keyword rule at 7.50 incremental
reply points and exceeds the fixed replies at 7.00. The future-negotiation
control scores above the weakest genuine timing objection, exposing a
ranking error that scalar calibration cannot remove. There are 30 native
first-token decisions on one exposed family. Held messages are authored
stresses with simulated reply reward, with no end-to-end score for their
native rendering. [Held texts, reward fit, and token replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/text-reply-transfer/README.md)

### Check the speaker's explicit claim before selecting a reply

At the same call budget and with the same 2553 texts and reward
observations, the claim-based router improves calibrated reply value
from 7.50 to 7.833. Four true rewrites are recovered, with three
false process replies. The averaged logits now separate the classes
on this small panel. The original three-state reward fit places its
cutoff outside the separating gap. On eight Afghanistan controls the
zero-threshold averaged claim check scores 8/8, while transfer of the
2553 cutoff scores 4/8 by admitting all four negatives. A supplied
hypothesis and answer-to-action mapping make this a structured
source-reading test. It uses 46 frozen first-token judgments and
authored stress cases, with no new final-record judgments.
[Matched reward fit and threshold transfer](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/claim-scoped-routing/README.md)

### Route from the original Resolution 2553 written statements

The claim-scoped model and the reward-calibrated direct router each
select the best compiled reply on all six documentary views and
release a mean 35.167/70. The uncalibrated direct prompt matches three
views and averages 33.50. Fixed Fund averages 33.833. The measured
gain comes from using the UK negotiation concern when it is present
and allowing the separate Germany–Belgium funding comparison when
Belgium's explanation arrives. The source is the official annex text
rather than authored stress prose. The final record is compiled from
reviewed source cards, with no new model writing or policy fit. All
24 native choices replay. The six overlapping views represent one
exposed event family. [Documentary extraction, scored records,
and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/reform-2553-transfer/documentary-routing/README.md)

### Transfer the documentary router to Afghanistan and score attribution separately

On six official 2721 views the direct reader, the averaged claim check, and
the imported 2553 cutoff all reach the exact release ceiling of 55/70.
Only three views have a nonzero consequence for reply choice. All three
are routed correctly. On the consultation-only Russian excerpt and
China's statement the native readers confuse consultation with a condition
for appointment support. The imported cutoff additionally accepts the
empty view. Exact provenance binding keeps these errors from producing
unsupported comparisons. The scored record endpoint masks the semantic
errors, so both measurements remain necessary. The test uses 24 frozen
first-token decisions and a fixed renderer on one exposed docket.
[Official source extraction, records and scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/afghanistan-2721-source-contrast/documentary-routing/README.md)

### Require a source witness for documentary routing

On twelve original 2553 and 2721 views, a literal-quote gate and averaged
verifier turn two false support claims into one but miss both UK positives.
Mean compiled release drops from 45.083 to 44.417. A punctuation-only
recovery maps both UK quotes and the still-insufficient China quote back
to their original source text. The surviving consultation-only Russia
error has exact provenance and passes semantic verification. Removing
only the explicitly stated agreement condition from the full Russia
speech yields an exact but vague quote, which the verifier rejects.
The evidence shows separate formatting and attribution failures.
Nineteen frozen calls, assistant witness review and fixed terminal records
provide the boundary. [Exact spans, semantic checks, and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/witnessed-documentary-routing/README.md)

### Select indexed documentary evidence and check required claim elements

The indexed reader and scope-specific verifier get 12/12 on the original
source views and reject the separate Russia agreement-sentence deletion.
The previous verifier on the same six selected passages gets 11/12 and
accepts the deletion. Both UK timing claims survive byte-exact evidence
recovery. The ordinary full-message judge got 10/12, but all of these
routes with intact UK recall compile to the same mean 45.083/70 because
the original renderer neutralizes the attribution errors. The new
result separates retrieval repair from semantic verification at higher
call cost. It uses 37 frozen model calls, assistant-selected criteria,
and overlapping views of two exposed dockets.
[Verified sentence ranges, outcomes and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/indexed-witness-routing/README.md)

### Train both documentary desks with a shared terminal reward

In a bounded archival environment, one REINFORCE actor learns when to
forward a private source and the other learns which reply to send from
the observed claim bit. Both see only the shared final score minus
the constructed send cost. After 512 batches on 2553 and 2721 source
views, held utility averages 33.376 against the exact 33.451 ceiling.
The pair matches the ceiling on 162 of 192 held transport, price and
prior settings. Crossing the learned sender with a fixed reply policy
drops utility to 31.415. It demonstrates learned coordination over the
registered documentary observations, with the same underlying views
in training and test. The backbone and exact record renderer remain
fixed. [Shared-reward training and held policies](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/documentary-team-policy/README.md)

### Hold out a docket from shared team policy training

A receiver trained on either 2553 or 2721 still reaches the other
docket's exact utility ceiling when paired with a well-calibrated
sender. The frozen team falls short through forwarding choices:
2553 training produces silence on the Afghanistan grid, while 2721
training makes the 2553 sender overspend. A 32-batch warm start
recovers most of the 2553 loss and none of the Afghanistan loss.
An adaptive sender-only calibration with 128 randomized target
exchanges reaches the same-interface ceiling in all 192 held
transport settings, given the logged receipt flags and fixed partner.
This separates what transferred zero-shot from what required target
reward observations. The underlying reader and registry were exposed
during development. [Actor swaps, payoff coverage, and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/shared-policy-transfer/README.md)

### Retain reply identity when calibrating for new partners

Under the same 128 randomized exchanges per docket, a sender value
fit conditioned on which reply arrives reaches the exact ceiling
across six declared partner profiles and 1,152 held settings. Pooling
reply identity leaves mean regret of 0.363 points. Using 24 probes
to estimate each partner profile leaves 0.029 regret, concentrated
in the mixture dominated by the reversed reader. The already
verified document renderer and cost schedule stay fixed. Prompt
quality and noisy scores are outside this replay. [Learned values,
matched logs and held outcomes](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/partner-conditioned-calibration/README.md)

### Substitute a measured native judge for the archived relation reward

Across twelve authentic-receipt records, the label-averaged Qwen judge
gets ten relationship judgments right. It misses the four-point
Germany–Belgium relation and awards a 22-point relation to the Russia
record with the wrong reply. It rejects three of four diagnostic
mutations. On the same randomized calibration log and held partner
grid, replacing only the relation points reduces fitted sender
utility from 32.225 to 31.885. The preceding replay's perfect
calibration depended on accurately scoped reward. A single label
mapping can reject all relationships or admit all of them.
This is one native reward component with archive guard retained,
not a validation of a complete LLM judge. [Judgments, scores and
policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/judged-relation-reward/README.md)

### Require an answer witness before awarding relationship points

Requiring a sentence range from the submitted answer improves archived
criterion accuracy from 10/12 to 11/12 by recovering the valid funding
comparison. It still awards a 22-point relation to a Russia answer
that omits the distinction. Its selected passages hide the later
contradictions in two mutated answers, both of which receive credit.
On four separate event-family controls the grounded judge gets 2/4.
The fitted sender's mean utility changes from 31.885 to 31.898, below
the exact 32.225. Requiring both full and cropped judgments rejects
all mutations in a post hoc replay while preserving the original
nominal error and losing funding recall. This exposes a difference
between witness retrieval and whole-answer contradiction checking.
[Exact spans, mutation grades and rollout replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/answer-grounded-judge/README.md)

### Separate stated coverage from the factual guard

The two-question judge denies the unsupported 22-point Russia bonus.
The coverage question reads the entire answer with the criterion and
the fidelity question reads the entire answer with the evidence. At the
fixed threshold, the result is 11/12 archive grades, 4/4 rejected
mutations and 3/4 cross-family controls. The guard independently rejects
the four contradictions and clears all 16 clean records. It adds no
rejections over coverage on this roster. The same sender fit now yields
32.212 mean utility over 1,152 held settings, versus 31.898 for cropped
witness judging and 32.225 with exact archived rewards. The remaining
routing gap comes from the four-point Belgium–Germany comparison.
These are exposed compiled records with the archival guard and other
reward components held fixed. [Prompts, judgments and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/split-relation-judge/README.md)

### Deploy both learned desks against a native final writer

On nine receipt sets for Resolution 2721, the frozen Qwen chair writes
each complete brief from public facts and delivered excerpts. The
trained pair's average utility advantage over the optimal arrival-only
control flips from +2.57 on authored records to −15.14 on live
generation across the same 48 channel and price settings. Six briefs
fail the factual guard and no branch releases relationship credit. Raw
coverage with the guard ignored still leaves 48 losses. The best
content-aware policy on this generated bank always withholds the first
note. The sender's reward can therefore favor silence even when the
underlying evidence is worth communicating. [Locked prompts, grades
and exact policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/native-team-chair/README.md)

### Preserve delivered statements and generate only the relation

Keeping the source text verbatim restores standalone scores on 2721.
The direct Qwen comparison earns the Russia bonus, but two Ghana links
