claim more was adopted than the evidence supports. Exact witness
checking rejects 4/4 generated comparison blocks, restoring standalone
coverage. Apostrophe normalization would admit both matched pairs and
their semantic errors. The strongest content-aware and arrival-only
policies tie on both native banks, including the one that earns a Russia
bonus. The trained team gets 28.26 utility with the direct writer and
34.20 with witness fallback, versus 40.72 and 38.13 for each matching
arrival-only control. [Output spans and exact deployment replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/anchored-comparison-chair/README.md)

### Use typed relationships and challenge response-label stability

On four 2721 receipt pairs, the typed writer restores the Ghana link
under both label assignments. The assignments disagree on Russia.
Requiring agreement gives 3/4 on real pairs and leaves the learned
team at 38.93 utility against 40.72 for best arrival-only. One rotation
reaches 43.29 and appears to recover the 2.57-point trained advantage,
while failing both crossed pairs and all four fictional controls. With
a fixed UNAMA reply that rotation scores 29.41 versus 38.93 for the
source-bound record. The result exposes robustness errors hidden when
the trained pair routes around bad combinations. A post hoc phrase
rule gets all eight prompts right, so this roster cannot establish an
advantage over explicit polarity cues. [Typed records, controls and
policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/typed-comparison-chair/README.md)

### Derive each comparison from independent source claims

On 2721, checking the delegate's stance and the adopted text in separate
calls recovers both real relations and rejects both crossed receipts.
The same learned desks now reach 43.29 utility over 48 settings versus
40.72 for best arrival-only. The fixed-reply swap also matches the
authored reference at 38.93. The new foil roster breaks the phrase
rule on all eight paraphrased or quoted cases, all of which the
source-scoped model handles. Two prior clause-only reversals still
fool the averaged decision, including replacing preservation with
transfer of the mandate. The model reads typed propositions for one
exposed docket. These are simulated deployments with native relation
decisions and a fixed renderer. [Locked prompts, counterfactuals and
policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/role-scoped-comparison/README.md)

### Train both desks from native source-claim rewards

The separate verifier gets all six 2553 receipt pairs and all six
held quotation, reversal and paraphrase controls right. Its bonuses
and the prior 2721 bonuses exactly reproduce the twelve authored
double-delivery scores. Training both decentralized actors again
with 512 shared-reward updates reproduces the archived fit. Against a
freshly optimized arrival-only control, mean utility is 33.376 versus
32.894 on 192 settings. The advantage comes from 2721. The 2553 team
trails 25.584 by 0.061. Optimizing sends with its learned reply fixed
reaches 25.659, the full oracle there. Belgium sends account for 18
of the 20 suboptimal sender choices on that docket. The language
model reads source claims and the renderer supplies fixed clauses.
[Model rewards, final records and joint fit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/native-relation-team-fit/README.md)

### Learn forwarding value from paired send and withhold outcomes

Paired policy gradients, twice the sample batch and the exact expected
gradient all keep the 18 Belgium sender misses. The exact gradient
also misses all seven profitable Belgium sends on the training grid.
A sender that instead estimates the incremental score from delivered
messages and subtracts the known attempt cost reaches the full 2553
oracle at 25.659. The reply actor still learns jointly from sampled
shared reward. Across 192 settings the value-based pair gets 33.4502,
against 33.4506 for the content-aware oracle and 32.8936 for best
arrival-only. The test reuses matched conditional score queries and
caches native relation rewards. It is one seed on two exposed dockets.
[Paired and value-based learning traces](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/paired-sender-credit/README.md)

### Transfer delivery-value training to 2664 and 2739

The same learner starts from scratch on two additional exposed dockets.
The frozen source verifier recovers the authored relation decisions on
all 12 receipt pairs. At the original held costs, arrival reveals enough
for the strongest control to match the oracle on both dockets. The
value-trained pair matches 188/192 of those settings. At three lower,
untrained prices it matches all 192. On 2739 the lower costs allow
Algeria's objection-only excerpt to pay, making the content of the
received note consequential for the reply. The value-trained team
beats arrival-only in 80/96 settings there, averaging 40.084 versus
39.724. On 2664, the strongest arrival-only policy still ties the
full-content oracle. [Locked transfer and price stress test](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/value-routing-docket-transfer/README.md)

### Test a newly authored UNBench author-test docket

The 2744 delisting task uses matched choices between committee
decision authority and the review of the Focal Point. The role-scoped
Qwen verifier recovers all six real pair grades and rejects four
marked fictional reversals. After 512 updates from scratch, the
delivery-value team reaches the content-aware oracle in all 96
held settings. It averages 40.565 versus 39.457 for the optimized
arrival-only control and 40.391 for sampled joint policy gradient
at the same nominal reward-query budget. UNBench event 2545 is an
author-test event. The pinned UNSC-Bench table has no matching row.
The experimental rubric is authored and the records use a typed
renderer. [Provenance, prompts and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-2744/README.md)

### Replace the 2744 typed renderer with a live writer

Twelve genuine Qwen finals cover every receipt combination on
the 2744 docket. Native writing takes the same delivery-value team
from 40.565 to 6.210 utility. Best arrival-only reaches 16.001
and ties the content-aware oracle on the generated bank. The chair
states the A3+ comparison but leaves the Malta comparison implicit
and frequently omits the vote identifiers or public authority
qualifications. A factual-score sensitivity that restores exact
standalone credit while retaining only the native relation keeps the
content-aware and arrival-only ceilings tied at 38.349. The effect
therefore cannot be explained solely by strict adoption scoring or
factual guards. [Unedited finals, marks and policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-native-chair/README.md)

### Test generic and topic-guided native comparisons on 2744

The protected factual record gets back to 35.987 mean utility
for the trained pair. A generic native comparison writer abstains
on Malta and blurs legal force for the A3+, yielding 31.578.
Passing the verifier's topic question elicits a Malta sentence
but still omits who decides and introduces ambiguous ownership.
The focused A3+ draft turns continuous review into a mandate.
The fixed receipt gate prevents invalid crossed additions but
the admitted sentences lower utility to 22.761. Even a permissive
reading with full A3+ credit and clear Malta standalone coverage
leaves the best arrival-only and content-aware policies tied at
38.349. [Generic comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-comparison-writer/README.md)
· [Focused prompts and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-focused-writer/README.md)

### Train the comparison writer on separate source families

A rank-four Qwen3.5-4B adapter received 24 completion-only
updates on six valid relations and six abstentions from 2721 and
2664. All six 2744 prompts were excluded from the fit. Matched
frozen outputs reproduced the archive. The adapted writer says
NONE in every held pair, eliminating one ambiguous admission and
leaving the two valid comparisons unwritten. The learned routing
utility moves from 31.578 to the standalone-only value of 35.987,
below best arrival-only at 37.242. The result identifies suppression
of prose rather than acquisition of source-faithful comparisons.
[Training and token audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/comparison-adapter-transfer/README.md)

### Remove abstention targets from the held comparison fit

At the same 24 updates, repeating the six supported targets makes the
Qwen adapter write on all six held 2744 pairs. Malta's answer runs to
42 words and invents a recommendation. The A3+ answer blurs the force
of the Council's continuous-review intention. Neither comparison earns
its bonus. The fixed gate holds learned-team utility at the frozen
31.578. Ungated utility drops to 29.552. The mixed adapter's 35.987
comes from abstaining. The supervised token count rises from 400 to
752 at the same update count.
[Exact replies, training audit and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/comparison-positive-adapter/README.md)

### Quote operative language in the native comparison

On the same six held 2744 pairs, a source-quotation instruction leaves
both valid comparisons uncredited. The frozen writer skips Malta's
authority link and calls continuous review an adopted provision,
quoting a phrase from the delegate note. A fresh CPU control gives
the same 31.578 learned-team utility against 37.242 for arrival-only.
The single-credit replay leaves the team behind with either relationship
alone. Crediting both over the preserved facts gives 40.565 versus
39.457. The operative phrase needs to be tied to the correct source and
carry its force into the final sentence.
[Matched replies, source checks and policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-quote-writer/README.md)

### Select witnesses and link existing source paragraphs

The locked 2744 witness renderer loses both valid links. Malta's spans
exceed the display budget and A3+ fails exact punctuation matching.
A post hoc compact renderer keeps exact witness offsets and links
the existing paragraphs without repeating them. It recovers both
comparisons but applies two false crossed links and one false
fictional reversal. Ungated utility is 38.539 for the learned team
versus 39.384 for arrival-only. With the prior source gate, it rises
to 40.565 versus 39.457 on the same 96 settings.
[Raw witness choices, source offsets and policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-witness-render/README.md)

### Execute both 2744 policies with fresh source checks

A fresh worker ran the trained desk and best arrival-only desk on 48
channel branches using the same compact chair. At 80% delivery each
way and an eight-point send price, learned reply selection adds 2.304
released points under each prior. Re-optimizing arrival-only from the
fresh branch scores gives the same difference. Both arms always send
in this slice, so the gain comes from choosing a document conditional on
the received note. The learner adds 345 to 793 expected input tokens per
episode. This tests integrated inference on an exposed dossier.
[Branch records, token audit and policy comparison](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-live-routing/README.md)

### Transfer the frozen 2744 routing policy to 2739

The transferred receiver needs document-role binding and a standalone
fallback after silence. With a local novelty check on forwarding, the
frozen pair scores 40.036 versus 39.724 for arrival-only on the lower
price grid, and 35.958 versus 37.772 on the original grid. A target
best-response sender closes the gap to the content-aware oracle, and
a hand-written receiver can reproduce the transferred actions.
A faithful objection-only paraphrase flips the mean verifier margin
from −0.125 to +0.500 and lowers lower-grid utility to 35.404 versus
39.700. Requiring both label orders to agree fixes that paraphrase
but removes the valid A3+ link on the archived 2744 bank. Neither
transfer nor source verification is robust across these tested changes.
[Locked transfer, fresh logits and exact policy replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/delisting-to-reporting-transfer/README.md)

### Split the source verifier's necessary claims

Separating Algeria's reporting position from its abstention reason
fixes the 2739 objection-only paraphrase. Its component margins are
−3.375 and +3.875. A reporting-only foil also fails the combined
gate. The frozen transfer returns to 40.036 versus 39.724 at lower
prices. On 2744, the same method rejects the explicit time-bound
preference in both A3+ phrasings and reduces the learned donor to
39.039 versus 39.457. The useful source distinction in one docket
does not remove sensitivity to the wording of another criterion.
[Exact component verdicts and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/decomposed-source-gate/README.md)

### Fit transferable delivery value from paired terminal outcomes

Leaving each of 2664, 2739 and 2744 out in turn, the factored sender
matches the exact content-aware ceiling on all 576 canonical
settings. Its retained relationship fraction is 1.0 in every fold.
The unfitted rule using the full public rubric bonus makes the same
decisions. Raw value pooling matches 222 settings. On 2739's original
price grid the factored sender reduces mean send cost from 5.400 to
0.717 and ties arrival-only at 37.772. When the verifier falsely
accepts the objection paraphrase, the same sender drops below arrival-only
on the lower grid, 35.457 versus 39.700.
[Paired fits, rule control and held decisions](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/sender-value-family-transfer/README.md)

### Fit both routing desks on the compact writer's actual scores

On the ungated compact bank for 2744, joint fitting raises utility
from 38.539 to 40.411 against 39.384 for best arrival-only. The new
sender prevents the earlier guard failures, keeps 25.08% supported
comparison release and beats best arrival-only in 75/96 settings.
Swapping the sender alone produces the same utility. The reply
desk's content-conditioned decisions agree with the previous fit.
The frozen policy collapses to 22.852 versus 37.168 on the held
topic-guided writer, where useful pairings from training now fail
release. This demonstrates calibration to one writer's realized
payoffs on an exposed docket.
[Cooperative fit, matched baseline and writer swap](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/native-reward-routing-fit/README.md)

### Recalibrate after a known writer change

Two scalar probes of the eligible 2744 pairs retain the compact
writer's two relationships and clear the release failures from
stale admissions on the topic-guided and generic banks. After a
modeled 0.5-point writer-use charge, compact scores 40.438 against
39.372 for best arrival-only. The second probe creates the
difference. The other writers fall back to source-only. Adapting
the sender to that fallback raises utility from 35.896 to 37.242.
The replay assumes the measured writer scores repeat on the same inputs.
[Calibration traces, cost sensitivity and held settings](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/writer-change-calibration/README.md)

### Check exact-pair approvals under paraphrased inputs

On fresh 2744 inputs both supported comparisons survive when
the chair retains the complete source paragraphs. A3+'s selected
witness alone omits the preference for a time limit. Old
approvals and fresh source gates yield identical policy utility at
40.411 versus 39.457 for arrival-only. Both new fictional reversals
fall back during witness validation, leaving the fresh semantic
gate's ability to reject well-formed reversals untested in this run.
[Live outputs, exact source spans and scoring](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/writer-calibration-wording/README.md)

### Run a live historical slice of the closed benchmark

Frozen Qwen3.5-4B completes the two historical case matrices under
the published prompts. Peer-informed drafting averages −42 released
points on Myanmar and +18 on the Residual Mechanism relative to
independent drafts. Raw coverage drops on both dockets. Myanmar's
peer records misassign the reporting instruction. The Residual
Mechanism gain comes from one source-faithful record clearing the
word cap and guard. These 12 finals provide a released-score baseline
from one reader on fixed routing.
[Token replay, opaque packets and rubric scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/historical-benchmark-slice/README.md)

### Send exact source messages in the historical benchmark slice

On Myanmar an exact one-block selection from each desk raises the
peer briefs from zero to 35 and 42. This ties independent selection.
The UK consistently chooses the sponsorship passage, leaving the
operative reporting instruction out of the delivered evidence. On
the Residual Mechanism, all four exact-message briefs fail release.
The chair still invents voting rosters and crosses wording or
length boundaries. Peer access changes just one of the four
second-desk choices. The full transcripts expose these choices
for later reward-driven selection.
[Source choices, evidence availability and scored briefs](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/historical-source-messages/README.md)

### Measure the actual writer's ceiling on tribunal source selection

The complete one-block action bank has one released brief in 16
pair-and-order combinations. The maximum is 39 with Peru first and
zero in reverse. Under three independent source-availability rates,
the exact peer-informed and independent optima tie. Removing just
the word cap still leaves zero released peer headroom. This isolates
the final writer as a binding constraint for this tribunal setup.
[Full action bank, quote-bound verdicts and exact expectations](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/tribunals-source-action-bank/README.md)

### Fit routing with a protected tribunal chair

Verbatim public and received excerpts release all 16 tribunal
records at 38–52 points. With the tribunal statements desk first,
the trained Peru selector learns to send its unity appeal after
Russia's staffing note and its cooperation passage after the other
notes. The peer effect is 0.079 and 0.094 points on two held
availability rates. With Peru first it is zero. An unfitted rubric
rule reaches the same optimum. This identifies a genuine information
benefit under the protected renderer and no additional gain from
learning the visible scoring rule.
[Protected records, policy fit and held scores](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/protected-tribunals-routing/README.md)

### Compare decision and documentary rewards in the workshop suite

Across the eight v1.7 workshop worlds, the documentary-reward fit
scores 53.710 released points versus 50.033 for decision-only
learning. After the same send fees, the gain is 0.853. The second
desk sometimes sends its mandate even after the opening certifies
an outcome. Both fits attain their own exact optima and disagree
in ten of the 24 simulated conditions. Iraq and Syria use the same
mechanical information structure, and the source-bound release
marks are authored.
[All literal receipt records and learned reward comparisons](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/workshop-documentary-reward/README.md)

### Run the published model-judge calibration

Frozen Qwen3.5-4B returns 3/21 quote-valid final verdicts and
0/4 process verdicts. Raw final labels match 111/123 expectations,
but one usable verdict clears the assertion that the vetoed Syria
draft authorized cross-border aid. All four process replies are
fenced. Removing only the wrapper still validates zero. The guard
error makes this configuration unsuitable for unreviewed reward.
[Raw replies, quote validation and label checks](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/live-judge-calibration/README.md)

### Test numbered evidence citations for the judge

Appending an indexed citation instruction produces 0/25 usable
verdicts, with 111/123 raw final-label matches. It conflicted with the
existing exact-quote prompt. Replacing those instructions throughout
the prompt on four selected anchors still produces 0/4. The final
replies copy text or confuse answer and source IDs. The process
replies leave required idea mentions empty. Both indexed tests flag
the Syria false authorization in their raw guard even though the
verdicts cannot score. Usability and semantic reward validity remain
distinct targets.
[Full run](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/indexed-judge-calibration/README.md) · [Four-anchor follow-up](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/indexed-judge-direct-instruction/README.md)

### Isolate factual classification from citation generation

A three-label guard with two swapped label orders gets 17/21
authored anchors, matching the bundled judge's agreement count.
It still clears the false authorization. On 12 actual historical
finals it matches 6/12 guard labels and clears five blocked or
uncertain records. A threshold with zero false clearance on the
anchors still clears three on the actual outputs. Keeping the
original rubric marks and swapping in the averaged guard moves the
reported peer effect from −12 to −4 points. This isolates a reward
validity problem beyond citation format.
[Guard logits, threshold diagnostics and fixed-rubric replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/isolated-guard-calibration/README.md)

### Scope the guard to individual categories

Separate procedure, authority and attribution checks clear all
12 historical briefs, including all seven previously blocked or
uncertain. Their label accuracy falls to 16/21 on authored anchors.
Agreement across all six label evaluations eliminates anchor false
clears at the cost of blocking 16/17 valid anchors, and still clears
two problematic real drafts. The scoped historical macro remains
−4 because the two additional guard errors are masked by word or
token limits.
[Scoped logits, policy ablations and fixed-rubric replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/scoped-guard-calibration/README.md)

### Automatic reward can create peer headroom

On Resolution 2422, changing only the factual guard from archived review
to isolated Qwen labels gave the fitted peer policy +3.22/+4.85 points
relative to fitted independent selection at 0.65 availability, across
the two speaker orders. Both gains were zero under archived review. The
judge newly released five otherwise eligible records, including three
unsupported voting rosters and two uncertain attributions of Peru's
enforcement examples. Both letter mappings cleared all five. Exact
information bounds and held availability rates support the same result.
Treat automatic gains as provisional until crossed against reviewed
reward, with source identity and authority checked separately. This
experiment uses an exposed action bank and assistant annotations.
[Study, results and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/guard-reward-selection/README.md)

### Learn routing from one observed terminal score

On Resolution 2744, a receipt-value team reached the exact compact-writer
optimum across 96 held channel, cost and prior settings after 256 simulated
episodes with one terminal score each. Net score was 40.4205, compared
with 39.3838 for best arrival-only and 38.5390 for the fixed rubric route.
The estimator uses the IDs of delivered evidence at training time and
keeps actor decisions local. On the held topic-guided writer the same
peer route dropped to 22.6755 against 27.5144 for the frozen arrival-only
route, with zero-score joint records on 25.35% of episodes. This isolates
which gains survive single-trajectory training and which depend on the
specific writer. The score bank is deterministic and assistant-reviewed.
[Protocol, results and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/terminal-feedback-routing/README.md)

### Refresh routing after a writer change

On the same Resolution 2744 receipt task, a peer learner screened
comparisons with an exact source-and-record review cache. In a held
50/50 compact and topic-guided mixture it scored 38.241, above the
38.076 reviewed arrival-only ceiling and the 37.242 source-only route.
The stale compact route scored 31.548 and released unsupported
comparisons on 12.68% of expected mixed episodes. A 32-episode
exploration window changed the mixed phase's realized peer advantage
from −0.094 to +0.375 after charging one point per distinct review.
The experiment signals writer changes, uses archived assistant review,
and mixes two fixed output banks on an exposed docket.
[Protocol and chronological replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/writer-refresh-feedback/README.md)

### Prospective transfer on conflict-induced hunger

Resolution 2417 combines UNBench event 2224 and official texts. The
pinned UNSC-Bench files have no matching row. The initial frozen chair
earned 0/12 complete comparisons and a scope-and-timing prompt earned
1/12. That prompt improved the canonical screened ceiling to 39.457
for both arrival-only and peer routing. The reversed presentations
tied at 37.242. Replacing only the missing US reporting distinction
with the registered comparison created 1.108 points of exact peer
headroom. The experiment isolates final drafting as the bottleneck
under the locked score. It uses assistant grading, an exposed second
prompt, and simulated transport. Sender fitting was gated on a
positive reviewed peer advantage.
[Case, annotations and replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/hunger-2417-transfer/README.md)

### Witness-based synthesis and learned routing on Resolution 2417

Qwen recovered both US reporting comparisons with strict evidence
selection. It omitted two required sanctions witnesses in both source
orders. A source-bound completion step recovered those witnesses only
on exact registered packets. The repaired canonical and reversed
tables both support a +1.108-point peer advantage. After 1,024
simulated scores per arm the peer learner reached 40.565, against
39.457 for best arrival-only. The fixed registry-only policy matched
the learner in every held setting. Five crossed historical pairs and
three fictional controls received well-formed false model proposals,
all blocked by exact registration. The result supports source-bound
assembly and training replay on an exposed case. It leaves semantic
approvals on genuinely new records unvalidated.
[Witness selection, final records and audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/hunger-2417-transfer/witness-routing/README.md)

### Text-only routing transfer with no target pair key

A receipt-value head fit on 2721 and 2744 scores 39.859 on the
original 2417 cards against a 39.457 exact arrival-only bound.
It drops to 37.504 on held paraphrases. The fixed semantic conjunction
scores 40.565 and 39.728. Component swaps isolate send-value loss on
the original cards and primarily reply-selection loss under paraphrase.
An audit removes the target answer keys and reproduces every frozen
route. The score uses exposed target sources and a registered writer
reference, with no novel draft verification.
[Protocol and exact replay](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/text-only-routing-transfer/README.md)

### Relative reply choice with a neither option

Direct relative choice reached the 40.565 peer optimum on both
original and reworded 2417 cards, compared with the 39.457 exact
arrival-only bound. The fixed hard model rule led. A probability
calibration fit on four other dockets selected the default temperature
and abstention bias, so it matched the unfitted probability rule.
Averaged classification passed six of eleven donor views and one of
four fictional scope controls. This is a better dispatch observation
on the exposed 2417 docket with a registered writer. It gives no
incremental gain from calibration and no basis for automatic release
on unregistered pairs.
[Protocol, frozen actions and audit](research/unsc-marl-20261119/benchmark-v1-runs/relative-routing-transfer/README.md)

### Interactive Council workshop and model assignment

The workshop separates source-backed clause review from verified
decision outcomes on fictional private mandates. In the five-seat matched
pilot, replacing Kenya's GPT-6-Astra with GPT-6.1-Sol left both public
speech-action sequences and both nine-speech outcomes unchanged. Both
rosters certified impasse in seven speeches. Both had four signatures
at the feasible cap and completed agreement with one more speech. A
seat-local checked-exchange script attained both endpoints at the
original cap, so the cutoff exposed a difference in negotiation procedure
even though it revealed no difference between the two live assignments.
Report endpoint counts, calls, signatures and verified exclusions, with
continuations identified separately. The live pair is one exposed case
family with one opener and no repeated samples.
[Workshop protocol](research/council_workshop/README.md) · [Matched runs and replay](reports/council-workshop-sol-control-20261123/README.md)

The held workshop suite adds a three-seat Gulf of Guinea briefing and a
six-seat Iraq consultation summary. In the mixed impasse trace, Norway
supplies two exclusions that no other seat can record. Ghana uses both
in its certified decision. The all-Sol
trace supplies one of those exclusions and remains unresolved at the
same ten-speech cap. The scripted baseline succeeds in seven speeches.
Both rosters signed the same permissible package in five speeches in
the feasible twin. The live evidence covers two of eight registered
case and opener combinations. The full design crosses homogeneous and reciprocal mixed
assignments without treating an opener rotation as a new case.
[Registered suite and paired replay](reports/council-workshop-held-pilot-20261123/README.md)

The six-seat Iraq case shows a separate bottleneck. The all-Sol team
recorded three of four exclusions by the original cap. France could
have ruled out the last package earlier, but it reached the floor as the
current draft only after France's last speech. Switching to a scripted
policy just before that speech certified impasse at speech 15. A switch
just after it left France behind the queue when the budget expired. A
provider rate limit interrupted the mixed arm twice, leaving its outcome
unmeasured. The scorer's `--trace` view exposes which delegate could
make each exclusion and when that package was on the floor.
[Iraq traces and exact-prefix replay](reports/council-workshop-iraq-pilot-20261123/README.md)

On the exact archived input for France’s last speech, a fresh original
prompt and a permission-only reminder both yielded a local signature.
Explicitly prioritizing a valid exclusion instead yielded the missing
`closed` veto. The same instruction left Norway able to sign in the
feasible Gulf case where it had no exclusion to make. Replaying the Iraq
prefix with the prompted action and fresh standard-prompt replies led to
certified impasse at speech 15 and 52 calls. This is a one-step prompt
intervention followed by one live continuation on an exposed prefix.
[Frozen inputs and continuation replay](reports/council-workshop-exclusion-prompt-20261123/README.md)

Applying the same instruction to every speech from the opening led to
an Iraq impasse certificate in seven speeches and 21 calls. The archived
standard run remained unresolved at 16 speeches and 55 calls. In the
feasible twin, both prompts yielded agreement in 12 speeches, with 48
calls for standard and 41 for priority. The production runner stores
the prompt condition for replay and resume. This is one trajectory per
condition on the case used to develop the instruction.
[Whole-meeting comparison](reports/council-workshop-exclusion-policy-20261123/README.md)

The exact instruction transferred to the three-seat Gulf of Guinea
workshop. Norway supplied the missing off-draft exclusion on its first
turn and the team certified impasse in seven speeches and 17 calls. The
archived standard run was unresolved at ten speeches and 24 calls. The
feasible twin reached the same agreement with each prompt, taking one
additional speech and call under priority. The comparison covers one
trajectory per condition with Ghana opening.
[Gulf of Guinea transfer traces and replay](reports/council-workshop-exclusion-transfer-20261123/README.md)

When the opening draft already satisfied all three mandates, the
standard prompt secured three signatures in three speeches. The
priority prompt first spent three speeches ruling out other packages,
then ran out of time with two signatures. Strictly following priority
requires at least six speeches on this case. The deadline was five and
was visible to both conditions. The live pair and scripted controls
show why the benchmark needs cases where additional checking can delay
an otherwise attainable agreement.
[Opening-agreement stress case and replay](reports/council-workshop-opening-agreement-20261123/README.md)

Conditioning exclusion priority on a public checked rejection retained
the three-speech, eight-call feasible opening agreement. In the Gulf
impasse, China rejected the opening draft on speech two. Norway then
volunteered an off-draft exclusion on speech three. The group certified
impasse in seven speeches and 16 calls. These fresh runs each use one
case and opener already examined in the prompt comparisons.
[Conditional instruction and matched replay](reports/council-workshop-conditional-exclusions-20261123/README.md)

Changing only the opening draft in the five-speech case exposes a limit
of using a checked rejection to trigger exclusion priority. The standard
run proposed the sole feasible replacement immediately and agreed in four
speeches. The conditional run recorded three exclusions and reached the
same proposal on speech four, leaving one signing turn. The scripted
control keeps the first rejection and still agrees by proposing on speech
two. The contrast separates the cost of further exclusions from the cost
of acknowledging that the opening draft is incompatible.
[Rejected-opening case and scripted witnesses](reports/council-workshop-rejected-opening-20261123/README.md)

The matched rejection fork removes one permission from Norway's mandate.
China sees exactly the same prompt after Ghana's checked rejection. To
finish in five speeches it must propose in the feasible world or record
its unique exclusion in the infeasible one. This gives an exact 50%
success ceiling under an equal prior. The two prompt variants chose
different actions and each preserved one endpoint. With six speeches,
a shared scripted policy proposes first and branches on Norway's public
response, attaining both endpoints under the same call cap.
[Matched cases, frozen inputs and budget witnesses](reports/council-workshop-rejection-fork-20261123/README.md)

With six speeches and controlled routing, China proposed `briefing`
from the shared observation. Norway signed in the feasible world and
offered a counterproposal in the infeasible one. Agreement completed
in five speeches. The counterproposal left too few speeches to collect
the remaining exclusions and certify impasse. Replacing that single
turn with a checked exclusion and regenerating the three following
speeches completed the certificate. The trace keeps the fixed routing,
shared model reply and substituted action visible.
[Live six-speech results and substitution replay](reports/council-workshop-live-rejection-fork-20261123/README.md)

Rotating the registered Gulf impasse from Ghana to Norway opening
changed the all-Sol result from unresolved to certified in nine
speeches. Norway supplied the previously missed `briefing` exclusion
on speech four and a second exclusion on speech seven. A provider limit
interrupted the matched Astra-seat arm and its resume on the same floor
request. The completed pair with Ghana opening therefore covers one
order, while the all-Sol outcome varies across the two observed orders.
[Order comparison and archived attempts](reports/council-workshop-norway-opening-20261123/README.md)

All eight standard-prompt all-Sol cells are complete. All four feasible
cells agreed, and one of four impasse cells reached a certificate. The
UAE-opening Iraq run recorded all four exclusions but spent its last
call on the floor poll. One call on the exact archived prefix let Brazil
certify at speech 16. The original cell remains unresolved in the
registered score. [Completed all-Sol slice and continuation](reports/council-workshop-all-sol-matrix-20261123/README.md)

Replaying the eight all-Sol prefixes with automatic moderator
certification lets the UAE-opening Iraq run close after the final
verified exclusion at 53 calls. It also saves two calls in the
Norway-opening Gulf impasse. The other six traces are unchanged. The
registered matrix keeps delegate declaration as its closing rule, and
the moderator replay shows how much the terminal procedure changes the
score without any new model replies.
[Moderator rule and replay results](reports/council-workshop-moderator-certificate-20261123/README.md)

The per-speech budget audit proves two losses before the raw speech
counts alone expose them. After speech six in the Ghana-opening Gulf
run, Norway could no longer deliver both required exclusions before the
cap. A scripted suffix from just before that speech certified impasse
in ten speeches and 23 calls. The France-opening Iraq queue blocks
France's last exclusion after speech 13. In UAE-opening Iraq, two calls
remained when two floor polls and one declaration were still required.
[Budget bounds and verified recovery](reports/council-workshop-budget-headroom-20261123/README.md)

An exact search over floor requests and admissible exclusions confirms
the three impasse cutoffs. In UAE-opening Iraq, a scripted witness
changes France's floor response at call 52, skips its next poll, and
certifies on call 55 within the original budget. The live score is
unchanged. [Exact replay and queue witness](reports/council-workshop-exact-impasse-reachability-20261123/README.md)

The same Iraq floor decision was tested live with the original prompt,
a queue reminder, and a follow-up showing the exact remaining call
budget. France passed at call 52 in all three. It requested the floor
one poll later in the reminder arm, after the cost had been incurred.
All runs stopped with four exclusions and no declaration.
[Matched live floor decisions and replay](reports/council-workshop-live-floor-recovery-20261124/README.md)

The same Iraq prefix did close within budget when the moderator gave
Brazil the floor directly after the last checked exclusion. A fresh
Sol reply declared the impasse on call 54 under the cap of 55.
The handoff used Brazil's exact archived call 56 prompt and skipped
the two intervening floor polls. This is a controlled follow-up on
one prefix, outside the registered matrix.
[Protocol, fresh reply and exact replay](reports/council-workshop-certificate-floor-20261124/README.md)

Across the ten completed registered meetings, direct handoff had no
effect on seven, replayed an all-Sol Gulf certificate one call faster,
and needed a decision absent from the registered log in each of the
other two. The Iraq continuation
above recovered a certificate. In the mixed Gulf continuation, the
skipped poll had recorded a real request from China. Ghana still
declared the impasse on the final speech after seeing the shorter
queue, finishing at 23 calls instead of 25. Keep the floor rule
fixed across model assignments and report any alternate rule as a
separate protocol comparison.
[Full replay audit and live Gulf continuation](reports/council-workshop-floor-rule-audit-20261124/README.md)

Under the registered floor procedure, the completed Gulf impasse pairs
show opposite outcomes for the same seat swap. With Ghana opening,
Astra at Norway recovered a certificate. With Norway opening, Sol
certified on speech nine and the Astra seat swap ended unresolved.
The second pair matched through call 17 on structured fields. On
speech seven, Sol as Norway ruled out `regional` while Astra as Norway
proposed `review`. Astra ruled out `regional` on the final speech.
The offline audit and the fresh final reply both show that gathering
the last exclusion at that point leaves no turn for a declaration.
[Matched comparison and prefix replay](reports/council-workshop-norway-focus-resume-20261124/README.md)

In the France-opening Iraq impasse, the mixed run certified in
14 speeches and 48 calls. All-Sol remained unresolved at 16 and
55. Ghana's response on speech five was the first structured
decision to differ, before the UAE took its first speech in either
arm. The comparison records one trajectory per assignment.
[Registered comparison and exact resume](reports/council-workshop-iraq-focus-resume-20261124/README.md)

The France-opening feasible twin restores only the UAE's permission
to sign `hybrid`. The mixed roster agreed on it in 11 speeches and
46 calls, and all-Sol agreed on it in 12 and 48. The same mixed
roster recorded the correct outcomes on both sides of the
feasibility contrast. The four cells each contain one trajectory.
[Fixed-roster feasibility contrast](reports/council-workshop-iraq-feasible-focus-20261124/README.md)

The Gulf comparison now has all eight cells for the all-Sol and
Astra-at-Norway assignments across the two openers and mandate
variants. All four feasible runs agreed on `briefing`. With Norway
opening, the mixed roster took seven speeches and 19 calls, and
all-Sol took six and 17. Each roster certified one of its two
impasses, with different openers and one trajectory per cell.
[Full Gulf comparison and replay](reports/council-workshop-gulf-feasible-focus-20261124/README.md)

Starting before the Gulf loss, a fresh standard continuation again
missed Norway's `briefing` exclusion. A priority reminder at that one
turn recovered it, and the later live delegates completed the evidence
on speech ten. Both live branches remained unresolved under delegate
declaration. Automatic moderator replay certified only the completed
priority branch. [Matched prompt and closing-rule comparison](reports/council-workshop-gulf-cutoff-20261123/README.md)
